Debye-Waller factors are automatic: a spectator mode's carrier factor is e^{−η²/2} L_n(η²) ≈ 1 − η²(n + ½), exp[−η²(n̄ + ½)] thermally averaged (Monroe Eq. 11; Wineland 1998 Eqs. 122-127 for the shot-to-shot spread) **[verified]**.

**Tests and the sideband light shift.** The two-level closed forms (generalized Rabi frequency √(Δ² + Ω²_{n′,n}), drive phase φ + (π/2)|n′ − n|; Wineland 1998 Eq. 21, RMP Eq. 84) test the builder **[verified]**; the full model differs from them by the light shift Ω²/(2ω_m) a sideband pulse gets from its own off-resonant carrier (relative size (Ω/(2ηω_m))²), which the mode calibration extrapolates to zero power (Section 7.5) **[recomputed here]**. The left side of Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.5 should read |↑⟩|n′⟩ **[corrected]**.

**Rabi convention.** H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊ + h.c. (RMP, Monroe 2020, Lee 2005, Mount 2015): the carrier flops as sin²(Ωt/2) and a π pulse is Ωt = π. Wineland 1998, Monroe 1995 and Blümel 2021 write P = sin²(Ωt), half this Ω, and are converted at ingest **[verified]**.

#### 4.3.2 Stimulated-Raman drives

Two far-detuned beams give, after adiabatic elimination (Δ_i ≫ γ_i and ≫ the two-photon coupling), the single-photon form with k → Δk = k₁ − k₂, ω_L → ω₁ − ω₂, φ → φ₁ − φ₂ and Ω e^{iφ} → e^{i(φ₁−φ₂)}/(2ħ²) Σ_i ⟨↑|d·E₂ε̂₂|i⟩⟨i|d·E₁ε̂₁|↓⟩/Δ_i, which is Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ) for one intermediate state (Wineland 2003 Eqs. 2.2-2.3; RMP Eq. A12; Wineland 1998 Eqs. 37-41) **[verified]**; one function computes it and the light shifts from the level structure (Section 4.5.4). Beam 1 is the higher-frequency beam, absorbed from the lower qubit level, so the tone at ω₁ − ω₂ = ω₀ + μ drives |↓⟩ → |↑⟩ with momentum Δk, |Δk| = 2k sin(θ/2) at crossing angle θ, zero (and every η with it) for co-propagating beams **[derived]**. Four factor-2 traps are converted at ingest **[corrected]**: Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.3 prints 1/(4ħ²) because its Ω is defined by ħΩσ₊ (the Stark shift keeps the 1/(4ħ²)); RMP Eq. A13's Ω = −2|Ω_{g3}Ω_{e3}|/Δ_R is twice the product over Δ_R; the arXiv v1 of Lee et al. 2005 puts 2 where 2Δ belongs in its Stark-shift denominator and swaps the beam-to-level assignment of its Eq. 2 (the journal version is right); and the single-photon symbol g is Ω/2 in Monroe 1995, Wineland 1998, Ozeri and Section 4.5.5 but Ω in the Lee comb chain.

**ac Stark shifts.** Each beam shifts level m_S by δ(m_S, j) = (1/4ħ²) Σ_i |⟨m_S|d·E_jε̂_j|i⟩|²/Δ_i (Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.10) **[verified]**, summed over the level structure (Section 4.5.4) rather than taken from Wineland's eight-term 9Be+ Eq. 2.11, which is not transcribed; the sum has the polarization null of a field-sensitive qubit and none for a clock qubit, whose differential shift is Ozeri 2005 Eq. 3 **[verified]**. The ratio of total scattering to Stark phase saturates at 0.9579 γ/Δ_hf (Ozeri 2005), 0.0154 photons per radian for 9Be+ at the clock-point splitting **[contested]**. Asymmetric bichromatic detunings add the Stark term s_i = μ_{i+} − μ_{i−} (Monroe Eq. 20) **[verified]**. Light shifts enter as (ħ/2)δ_St,i(t)σ_z^i following the instantaneous intensity, scaled from the calibration as (Ω/Ω_cal)^p, p = 1 for a two-photon drive and 2 for a single-photon optical or a microwave drive **[corrected]**; the scheduler compensates the believed shift by detuning every spin-flip tone, a σ_z force's tone keeping its beat note (Section 7.5).

**Clock-qubit Raman coupling and scattering** (Ozeri et al. 2007) **[verified]**. Ω_R = (g_b g_r/3)(b₋r₋ − b₊r₊) ω_f/[Δ(Δ − ω_f)]: the fine-structure paths interfere destructively and Ω_R falls as 1/Δ² beyond ω_f. The probability of any scattering event per π rotation, P_total = (πγ/ω_f)(2Δ² + (Δ − ω_f)²)/|Δ(Δ − ω_f)|, is independent of power and waist and minimal at Δ = (√2 − 1)ω_f. The gate error is its Raman part ε_S, whose minimum inside the fine-structure interval is 8πγ/(3ω_f) **[corrected]**; ε_S = 2π|Ω_R|ħω_{3/2}³w₀²/(3c²P) with P the power per beam (Eq. 17, whose λ_{3/2} = c/ω_{3/2} should read 2πc/ω) **[corrected]**; scattering into D levels is not protected, ε_D = f P_total with branching f **[verified]**. Ozeri's η carries a factor 4 under the root **[corrected]**, and his 9Be+ γ/2π = 19.6 MHz is one of four readings spanning 9% (the species table uses Monroe 1995's 19.4 MHz) **[contested]**. The scattering channels are Section 4.5.5's.

**Comb error model.** Mount et al. 2015's three tooth pairs (weights 1 : 2 : 1, Magnus-averaged) give R = exp[−(i/2)θ(1 + ε)(σ_φ + δZ)], the error model of Section 4.3.5 **[verified]**; Section 4.3.7 builds the comb drive itself.

#### 4.3.3 Microwave drives

A microwave drives the hyperfine transition with η ≈ 0; motional coupling needs a field gradient (Section 4.4.5). The archetype is Harty et al. 2014's 43Ca+ clock qubit at 146.094 G **[verified]**. Spectator Zeeman transitions 50 MHz away give 10⁻⁷ flopping and an ac Zeeman shift of −1.0 Hz while the drive is on, which with δ = ω_drive − ω₀ raises the effective detuning, δ_eff = δ − δ_ac **[corrected]**. Identity gates are delays, one pulse slot plus dead time per replaced pulse, so idle errors count; Section 9.2 reproduces the paper's error per gate of about 10⁻⁶.

#### 4.3.4 Phases, frames and virtual Z

The phase φ = φ₁ − φ₂ (plus Δk·X̄_i) may be zeroed once per ion; every later operation on that ion is referenced to it (Wineland 2003) **[verified]**. Carrier and sidebands share the factor e^{i(Δk·X̄_i − Δφ_i)} (Lee 2005 Eqs. 6-8) with Δφ_i = φ₂ − φ₁ the beat-note phase (−φ₁ for one beam), so beam 1's path phase enters σ₊ as e^{+iφ₁} and beam 2's as e^{−iφ₂} (Monroe 2020 Eqs. 16, 18) **[corrected]**. In a bichromatic pulse the spin phase φ_s = (φ_b + φ_r)/2 sets the force axis and the motion phase φ_m = (φ_b − φ_r)/2 the phase of the force on the mode (Haljan; Lee Eqs. 28-30; Monroe Eqs. 20-22) **[verified]**; Lee's φ_S = −(φ_b + φ_r)/2 is converted at ingest **[corrected]**. The sideband coupling carries an explicit i, and i[σ₊e^{iψ} − h.c.] = σ_{ψ+π/2}, so in the same-Δk geometry the force axis is π/2 + Δk·X̄_i + φ_s, a quarter turn from the carrier axis; crossed Δk (η_r = −η_b) adds π and cancels Δk·X̄_i **[corrected]**. The native MS(φ₀, φ₁, θ) plays φ_{s,i} = φ_i − π/2 (Section 7.6). With both beat notes of a beam pair on the same side of the third beam, Δk_r = Δk_b and the optical phase enters the spin phase (phase sensitive); on opposite sides, Δk_r = −Δk_b and it enters only the motion phase, which cancels at loop closure (insensitive) **[verified]**. RZ(θ) shifts the phase of every later pulse on its qubit by −θ in time order (Section 7.6) and costs nothing but the phase-switching dead time **[corrected]**.

#### 4.3.5 Single-qubit gates and composite pulses

GPi(φ) and GPi2(φ) are resonant carrier pulses of area π and π/2 at phase φ; the target is R(θ, φ) = exp(−iθσ_φ/2), σ_φ = X cos φ + Y sin φ (Mount 2015 Eq. 1) **[verified]**, and the exact dynamics add the off-resonant sidebands, Debye-Waller factors and crosstalk that the intrinsic budget reports (Section 9.6). The half-Pauli generator is load-bearing: a 2π segment is −I, and R(π, φ)R(α, β)R(π, φ)† = R(α, 2φ − β) is the reflection every corrector phase descends from **[verified]**. Products fold right to left against the time order, and a negative area is played at phase φ + π, exact under amplitude error but not under detuning error.

**Error primitive.** M(θ, φ; ε_a, ε_d) = exp[−iθ{(1 + ε_a)ρ̂(φ)·σ + ε_d σ_z}/2], ρ̂ = (cos φ, sin φ, 0), exact for a square segment under static errors, is Mount's R and Cummins' quaternion pulse (ε_a = g, ε_d = f = δ/ν₁, no rescaling) **[verified]**. Merrill and Brown's four channels map onto it **[verified]**: amplitude ε_A = Ω′/Ω; pulse length ε_T, the same propagator for a square pulse but kept separate for the coupled model; addressing ε_N = Ω′/Ω on a spectator, whose ideal is ε_N = 0 (M = R(θε_N, φ), not R(θ(1 + ε_N), φ)); and detuning ε_D = Δ/Ω, whose generator keeps its sign where the drive phase is π. Detuning does not commute with the ideal generator, which is why CORPSE needs a Magnus expansion and BB1 does not.

**Order and measure.** Order n means residual O(ε^{n+1}), infidelity slope 2(n + 1) **[corrected]**. The internal measure is F_K = ¼|Tr(U_id†U)|², with F_C = √F_K and F_avg = (2F_K + 1)/3 as conversions, so 1 − F_K = 2(1 − F_C) and 1 − F_avg = (4/3)(1 − F_C) at leading order **[verified]**; Low-Yoder-Chuang's ‖UV†‖ and Brown-Harrow-Chuang's 1 − ‖V†U‖ are identically 1 and 0 for unitaries and are replaced by F_K **[corrected]**. Below 1 − F ≈ 10⁻⁸ sequences are scored by the phase-quotiented distance E = min_g ‖U − e^{ig}V‖₂, with 1 − F_K ≈ E² **[corrected]**.

**The library.** Sequences are (θ_l, φ_l) in time order with the target phase added to every entry, the target pulse included (subtracting it leaves O(ε)) **[corrected]**; k = arcsin(sin(θ/2)/2) and φ₁ = arccos(−θ/4π).

| Family | Sequence, time order | Corrects | Order | Printed defect or note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SK1 (BHC Eqs. 9-10) | (θ, 0), (2π, φ₁), (2π, −φ₁) | amplitude, addressing | 1 | Eq. 10's U_1X(+θ) doubles the bare error; use U_1X(−θ) **[corrected]** |
| SKn (BHC Eqs. 13-14) | M_{A_n}⁻¹ U_nX(‖A_n‖^{1/n}) M_{A_n} SK(n−1) | as SK1 | n | spectral norm, not trace; M_{A_n} is the imperfect pulse; U_nY conjugates with π, not π/2; SK2 needs φ_γ = arcsin(−sin 2φ_SK1/2) **[corrected]** |
| BB1 (Wimperis) | (θ, 0), (π, φ₁), (2π, 3φ₁), (π, φ₁) | amplitude | 2 | **[verified]** |
| NB1 | (θ, 0), (π, φ₁), (2π, −φ₁), (π, φ₁) | addressing | 2 | differs from BB1 only in −φ₁ for 3φ₁ **[verified]** |
| PB1 = P2 | (θ, 0), (2π, φ_P), (2π, −φ_P), (2π, −φ_P), (2π, φ_P), φ_P = arccos(−θ/8π) | both, weakly | 2 | five pulses, not four **[corrected]** |
| P2j, N2j, B2j (MB Eqs. 44-50) | T_{2j}(1, φ_{P2j}) after (θ, 0), φ_{P2j} = arccos(−θ/(2πf_j)) | amplitude (P, B), addressing (N) | 2j | f_j = (2^{2j−1} − 2)f_{j−1}, f₁ = 4 (P) or 2 (N, B), not the printed −1; odd-k B2j layers need the palindromic M(kπ,φ)M(kπ,3φ)M(kπ,3φ)M(kπ,φ) **[corrected]** |
| CORPSE (CLJ Eqs. 13-15) | (2π + θ/2 − k, 0), (2π − 2k, π), (θ/2 − k, 0) | detuning | 1 | 420°/300°/60° at θ = π **[verified]** |
| short-CORPSE | (θ/2 − k, 0), (2π − 2k, π), (θ/2 − k, 0) | detuning | 1 | returns −R(θ, 0), a global phase **[verified]** |
| SCROFULOUS (CLJ Eqs. 18-23) | (θ₁, φ_S), (π, φ_S − arccos(−π/2θ₁)), (θ₁, φ_S), θ₁ = arcsinc(2cos(θ/2)/π), φ_S = arccos(−π cos θ₁/(2θ₁ sin(θ/2))) | amplitude | 1 | arcsinc on (0, π]; the minus in φ₂ is load-bearing **[corrected]** |
| CinSK, CinBB (Kabytayev Table I) | the CORPSE block, then (2π, −φ₁), (2π, +φ₁) (CinSK) or (π, φ₁), (2π, 3φ₁), (π, φ₁) (CinBB) | both at once | 1 in each | slope 4 under ε_a = ε_d, where SK1, BB1 and CORPSE fall to 2 **[verified]** |

SCROFULOUS has four times the primitive's detuning sensitivity where BB1 has almost none, so BB1 is the default when both channels are live **[recomputed here]**; CORPSE's order is fitted below 10⁻⁴, above which its ε_D³ term dominates **[verified]**. Trotter-Suzuki phases are root-found on the leading ε coefficient rather than taken from the closed form, and T_{2j−2} is cached as one 2 × 2 matrix power whose repetition count is a record, never a multiplier applied again.

**Low-Yoder-Chuang.** Their length-L = 2n sequences of 2π pulses, PD_n (palindromic, even n only) and AP_n (antipalindromic), are at most quartic in tan²(φ₁/2), with AP1 = SK1, PD2 = PB1 and ToP_n seeding a numerical continuation in γ = θ_T/2π, so the standard names are aliases of these forms **[verified]**; the printed reality domain |γ| ≤ 2⌊n/2⌋ + 2 is only an outer bound **[corrected]**. Eq. 11 certifies order n without an ε sweep, Φ_L^j(φ) = f_L^j(γ) for 0 < j ≤ n, where Φ_L^j = Σ_{h₁<…<h_j} exp(−iΣ_k(−1)^kφ_{h_k}) alternates the sign over the position within the subset and sums over combinations, and f_L^j(γ) = Σ_k(−1)^k C(T, k)C(L − T, j − k) with T = (γ + L)/2; it applies to equal-area families only **[verified]**. Toggling PD_n (ψ_k = −Σ_{h<k}(−1)^hφ_h + Σ_{h>k}(−1)^hφ_h) gives broadband BB_n, and toggled PD2 is BB1 **[verified]**.

**Durations and the floor.** Mount's PD6 phases are indexed by the target angle θ_t, not the target phase **[corrected]**; PD6 at arbitrary θ_t needs the continuation above. At constant amplitude τ_seq = Σ_l θ_l/Ω, (4π + θ)/Ω for SK1 and BB1, (4π + θ − 4k)/Ω for CORPSE and (8π + θ − 4k)/Ω for CinSK and CinBB **[recomputed here]**; the scheduler reports duration beside infidelity because total rotation sets the scattering and heating exposure, and since no source gives a rule for choosing a family, the choice is the caller's. The sources are pure 2 × 2 algebra, so their residuals are a floor: sidebands, Debye-Waller factors, leakage, scattering, the correctors' own light shift and heating come from playing the same list through the Hamiltonian of Section 4.3.1.

#### 4.3.6 Micromotion in the drive

A stray-field displacement phase-modulates the drive at Ω_T with index φ_Ω = (q_x/2)[k_xΔ_x − k_yΔ_y] (Wineland 1998 Eqs. 71-72), multiplying the carrier by J₀ and adding micromotion sidebands (Section 4.1.1) **[verified]**. The builder's default, `carrier_j0`, multiplies every drive coefficient by J₀(β), the shot average without an rf phase reference, and omits the J_n²(β) sideband weights; `modulated` plays e^{iβ cos(Ω_rf t + δ)} with δ from an rf-locked drive (`Drive.rf_locked`, `rf_phase_rad`) or a sampled rf phase and refuses an unlocked drive without one, since a result that depended on the integrator's time origin would be an artifact; `none` drops the modulation. C₀ is already inside η (Section 4.1.1). β is the shot's: a sampled stray-field drift adds to the residual field of Section 4.1.1 in every drive's index as it moves the ion, so on the 50 V/m anchor a −9.94 V/m drift along x takes β from 0.4090 to 0.3277 and J₀ from 0.9586 to 0.9733 **[derived]**.

#### 4.3.7 Frequency-comb (mode-locked) Raman drives

Mode-locked 355 nm Raman systems on 171Yb+ drive the qubit with a pulse train, so the chain of Sections 4.3.2 and 4.5.4 becomes a sum over comb-tooth pairs. The sources (Hayes 2010, Islam 2014, Inlek 2014, Mizrahi 2013 and 2014, Campbell 2010, Lee 2016) cover one species at one wavelength; the inputs are (ν_rep, τ, Δ, species, geometry).

**Comb and teeth.** The train E(t) = Σ_n f(t − nT) e^{iω_c t} (Hayes Eq. 1) has teeth at ω_c + 2πmν_R **[verified]**. A Λ Raman process sees only tooth differences, so the carrier-envelope offset cancels and only the inter-comb (AOM) phase, the force phase, is carried **[verified]**. Tooth k has g_k = g₀ √(πν_rep τ) sech(2πkν_rep τ) with g₀² = Σ_k g_k² = γ²Ī/(2I_sat) (Lee Eq. 1; Mizrahi Eq. 20) **[verified]**, γ angular and Ī the peak on-axis time-averaged intensity 2P̄/(πw₀²), w₀ the 1/e² intensity radius **[corrected]**. τ parameterizes the field envelope sech(πt/(2τ)), whose field and intensity FWHM are 1.6768 τ and 1.1222 τ **[corrected]**; the sum rule is asymptotic in ν_rep τ, so the profile is built over hundreds of teeth and normalized to it **[recomputed here]**. Hayes' E₀ is fluence-normalized, so a measured peak passed as E₀ overstates Ω₀ by π/2 **[corrected]**. The only tooth width is the Dirichlet kernel's, 1.2067 ν_R/N FWHM in amplitude and 0.886 ν_R/N in power **[recomputed here]**.

**Rabi frequency.** Pairs of separation l add at the beat note l(2πν_rep): Ω^{(l)} = Σ_k g_k g_{k+l}/(2Δ) ≈ Ω₀ sech(πlν_rep τ), Ω₀ = g₀²/(2Δ) **[verified]**. The pair argument is half the single-tooth one, a cheap flag for a factor-2 bug, and the pair form exceeds the exact convolution by about 5% **[recomputed here; corrected]**. The qubit-splitting suppression has three circulating forms, sech(ω_qτ/2) (Mizrahi Eq. 22), (ω_qτ)/sinh(ω_qτ) (the exact convolution) and (ω_qτ/2)/sinh(ω_qτ/2) (Hayes, whose τ is twice the Lee-family τ) **[contested]**; the simulator uses sech(ω_qτ/2) and reports the gap to the convolution (2.65% at 80 MHz and 10 ps). One source prints Ω = g²/(2Δ) (Chain I) and Ω = g²/Δ (Chain II), both self-consistent **[contested]**; the convention of Section 4.5.4 selects Chain I **[background]** (Section 12), so the printed π-pulse energy is Chain II's and Hayes' Ω₀ = sγ²/(2Δ) is halved at ingest. The fine-structure detunings are signed, Δ_{1/2} = +33 THz and Δ_{3/2} = −67 THz at 355 nm with 1/Δ = 1/Δ_{1/2} − 1/Δ_{3/2}; magnitudes would cost a factor 2.941 in Ω **[recomputed here]**.

**Resonance.** One comb drives the qubit only at integer q = ω₀/(2πν_R), and only if the pulse bandwidth exceeds ω_q **[verified]**. Two copies offset by an AOM satisfy |2πjν_R + Δω| = ω₀ ± ω_t with j and Δω = 2π(ν₁ − ν₂) signed, the sign of Δω selecting red from blue (Mizrahi Eq. 29), and j is root-found **[verified]**. Islam's lock branch ν_sb = nν_rep − |Δν_M| = ν_LO + ν_M2 needs nν_rep > ν_LO and the unstated ν_M1 > ν_M2 **[corrected]**; Inlek's red and blue legs close at tooth orders n = 160 and m = 154, and its printed AWG frequencies are each 2ν_α = 5.00 MHz high **[corrected]**.

**In the builder.** A comb drive is a set of tones (Section 5.2) with Ω_j = Ω₀ sech(jω_rep τ/2) on the one operator σ₊ ⊗ Π_m D_m(iη) **[verified]**. Beat notes within 10/t_g of the resonance stay explicit and all others fold into the static fourth-order shift below, never both (the self-beat l = 0 is the intensity, not a tone; keeping the nearest off-resonant note, 120 MHz away, would multiply the integration steps 40-fold); moving the cut changes the folded shift but not the total, and the gate-phase change is reported **[corrected]**. Mount's model is this set truncated to three beat notes **[background]**. The validity of the pulse-train-to-continuous-wave mapping is checked when the drive is built, a failing clause warning and a clause without inputs reported as not evaluated **[verified]**: N ≫ 1; ω_qτ ≪ 1; ω_q ≪ 1/τ ≪ |Δ| ≲ ω_FS; θ ≪ 1 per pulse; ω_rep τ ≪ 1; and ω_t t_N ≫ 1 with η√(n̄+1) ≪ 1 for resolved sidebands, against ω_t t_N ≪ 1 for the ultrafast kick. Train duration against the trap period plus the Lamb-Dicke condition selects the picture, not J₂ ≪ J₁ **[corrected]**; the sources do not bound the crossover, so the guard routes it to the exact pulse-by-pulse propagator (Section 12). The single-pulse ceiling is the Rosen-Zener P_{0→1} = sin²(πu) sech²(πν_HF T_pulse) (Campbell Eq. 6; Mizrahi Eq. 18) with ν_HF an ordinary frequency **[recomputed here]**. The Rosen-Zener contrast, the kick-area reduction and the tooth-pair suppression are one sech(ω_qτ/2) factor, applied once **[corrected]**. The pulse count is N = f_rep t and Ω₀ = f_rep θ; Mizrahi APB Eq. 23's 2πω_rep forms are wrong by (2π)² = 39.478 **[recomputed here; corrected]**.

**Two regimes.** Slow: V = e^{−iH₀T} exp[−i(θ_p/2)(σ₊e^{iΔk x̂} + h.c.)], H₀ = ω_t a†a + ½(ω₀ + Δω)σ_z (Hayes Eqs. 4-6) **[verified]**; inside the Lamb-Dicke regime the pulse sum is an N-slit grating at ϑ_r = (ω₀ + Δω − ω_t)T = 2πj with sideband coupling Ω_sb = θ_p ηN/2 over the train **[corrected]**. Fast: a counter-propagating pulse pair is a Kapitza-Dirac kick, U = exp{iΘ_B sin(Δk x̂ + φ)σ_x} = Σ_n e^{inφ} J_n(Θ_B) D(inη) σ_x^n, exact in η **[verified]**. The printed Ω/2 prefactor with Bessel argument ∫Ω dt is inconsistent, so one symbol Θ_B is defined as the Bessel argument, a perfect kick at Σ_k Θ_{B,k} = π; the ideal kick e^{iφ′}D(iη)σ_∓ − e^{−iφ′}D(−iη)σ_± squares to −𝟙, which is why the area is π **[recomputed here; corrected]**. Hayes' "k" is the two-photon Δk (only Δk = √2 k reproduces its η = 0.1), and at its assumed n̄ = 40 the Lamb-Dicke branch is illegal **[recomputed here; corrected]**. The blue-sideband generator squares to n̂ + 1 and n̂ on the two spin blocks, so its printed cos/sin form holds only for n = 0 → 1 and the rate is Θη√(n+1) **[recomputed here; corrected]**.

**MS gates.** Single-loop closure is ηΩ/δ = ½ with Ω the per-tone carrier Rabi frequency, the K = 1 case of Section 4.4.1 **[verified]**. In the phase-insensitive geometry (Inlek Eqs. 4-5, whose two printed lines of Eq. 4 disagree by 2; the second line and Eq. 5 are implemented) **[contested]** Δk_r = k_A − k_{B,r} and Δk_b = k_{B,b} − k_A, with φ_{S,i} = −½(Δk_r·X_i − Δφ_r + Δk_b·X_i − Δφ_b) and φ_{M,i} = ½(Δk_r·X_i − Δφ_r − Δk_b·X_i + Δφ_b): k_A and its path drift cancel from φ_G = φ_{S,i} + φ_{S,j} ≈ φ_{B,b} − φ_{B,r} (the sensitive geometry keeps −2φ_A) and move into the per-ion φ_{M,i} **[verified]**. The wave-front term Δφ_M = 2πl sin θ/λ′, λ′ = 2π/Δk, is implemented with an explicit end-to-end convention, the source's own numbers not closing **[corrected]**.

**Stark shifts.** In signed detunings the second-order shift is E^{(2)}_{00} = (g₀²/12)(1/Δ_{1/2} + 2/Δ_{3/2}), and E^{(2)}_{10} the same with Δ → Δ + ω_HF, so the differential shift depends only on g₀² **[verified]**; the printed (1/Δ − 2/(ω_F − Δ)) writes the P_{3/2} detuning as the positive ω_F − Δ, and pairing its −2 with a signed Δ_{3/2} double-counts the sign, 133-fold at 355 nm **[corrected]**. The fourth-order shift is E^{(4)}_n = −Σ_{a≠n} (Ω_{n,a}²/4) Σ_l sech²(πlν_rep τ)/(ω_a − 2πlν_rep), a single sum over the tooth separation l, identical to the printed double sum once its sign is corrected (δ_{a,n} = −δ_{n,a}) and its sech applied once (Ω_{n,a} carries none) **[corrected]**. The sum runs to eight half-widths of the sech² weight, l_max = ⌈8/(πν_rep τ)⌉, validated by doubling, because partial sums plateau falsely a factor 2 low near |l − j| ~ 10 **[recomputed here; corrected]**; l = 0 is kept, since it carries the equal factors of the Zeeman partners behind the elliptical-polarization cancellation E^{(4)}_{10} = 0, and a term is refused only when its denominator at the nearest folded beat note falls below 10⁻⁹ ν_rep **[corrected]**. For one comb the symmetrized sum gives δ₄ ≈ (7ζ(3)/π²) Ω₀²ω_qτ/ω_rep to leading order, the Bloch-Siegert term included as its j = −n member **[verified]**; for two offset combs the printed counter-rotating sum needs a plus, and the Stark-nulling AOM offset is root-found on the exact sum, not on the three-pole closed form **[recomputed here; corrected]**. At the source's full-power operating point the coupling exceeds the residual detuning, so the module warns when |δ_{n,a}| < |Ω_{n,a}| and does not claim the series there **[contested]** (Section 12). The printed δω^{(2)}/2π and δω^{(4)}/2π are ordinary frequencies inside angular papers **[corrected]**.

**Noise.** Three channels are comb-specific (Section 6.1). Repetition-rate drift is amplified at the qubit by the tooth index, δν_sb = nδν_rep **[verified]**, and is driven from a ν_rep(t) process (quasi-static per shot plus a sampled band), with a fast jitter fitted to the measured unlocked Ramsey time **[corrected]**. A feed-forward lock cancels drift only on the locked tooth's lower sideband, leaving (m − n)δν_rep on tooth m's lower sideband, (m + n)δν_rep on its upper and mδν_rep on a bare tooth, applied pair by pair **[corrected]**. Intensity noise Ω₀(1 + ξ(t)) costs ε ≈ (π²/2)αΩ₀²T with Ω₀ in Hz (in rad/s it is wrong by (2π)²), default α = −120 dB/Hz, the prefactor loose by order unity **[recomputed here; contested]**. The beat-note phase φ₀ is sampled once per shot; this layer adds no Lindblad rate.

**What the comb sources do not supply.** No spontaneous emission (every comb Hamiltonian is post-elimination), so a comb drive's scattering is not computed; no multi-ion comb gate (modes, η and closure come from Sections 4.1.3 and 4.4.3); no closing wave-front tolerance (only ΔL ≪ cτ ≈ 3 mm, printed as c/τ **[corrected]**); and no per-pulse intensity-to-Rabi chain, so a comb drive takes Ω₀ from the continuous-wave Raman derivation. Section 12 lists these with the missing noise spectra and the fourth-order shift of a resonant carrier drive.

### 4.4 Two-qubit entangling gates

All entangling gates are geometric-phase gates on shared motional modes, built by the builder of Section 4.3 from ordinary drive terms; there is no separate gate implementation. The equations below are what calibration fits, what the pulse solvers use and what the validation suite checks.

#### 4.4.1 The Mølmer-Sørensen interaction

**Hamiltonian.** Bichromatic light at ω₀ ± δ on N ions and one mode, H = ħν(a†a + ½) + (ħω_eg/2)Σ_i σ_z^i + Σ_i Σ_{t=1,2} (ħΩ/2)(σ₊^i e^{i(η_i(a+a†) − ω_t t)} + h.c.) (Sørensen and Mølmer 1999, 2000) **[verified]**, is integrated exactly in η. In the Lamb-Dicke regime without the carrier the propagator is exact, U = e^{−iAJ_y²}e^{−iFJ_y x}e^{−iGJ_y p} with F = ∫f, G = ∫g, A = −∫Fg (Sørensen-Mølmer 2000 Eqs. 6-9) **[verified]**. In the adopted notation (Kirchmair 2009 Eqs. 3-4; Haljan 2005), with the tones ε = ν − δ from the sidebands and S_α = Σ_i σ_α^i,

H(t) = −(ħηΩ/2)(a†e^{iεt} + h.c.) S_y,  U(t) = D̂(α(t)S_y) exp[i(λt − χ sin εt)S_y²],  α = (ηΩ/2ε)(e^{iεt} − 1),  λ = η²Ω²/(4ε),  χ = η²Ω²/(4ε²),

with Ω the per-tone Rabi frequency of Section 4.3.1 **[corrected]**; the adopted sign gives α the opposite sign to Kirchmair's, with identical moduli and S_y² phase. The printed −ħηΩ(...)S_y with α = (ηΩ/ε)(e^{iεt} − 1) holds for an Ω defined without the ½, the reading under which Roos's Ω_c = |ε|/(4η) is the closure below; the ingest converter carries the factor. Closure with maximal entanglement is ηΩ/ε = 1/(2√K), τ = 2πK/ε, in every spin normalization; only the phase target rescales, −π/2 on J_y², −π/8 on S_y² and χ = π/4 on σ_yσ_y **[corrected]**. The bichromatic coupling Ω̃ = −(Ωη)²/(ν − δ) is independent of n, the paths through n ± 1 interfering destructively, and the gate needs η√(n+1) ≪ 1 and Ω ≪ ν **[verified]**. D̂(αS_y) = P₀ + P₂D̂(2α) + P₋₂D̂(−2α) with P₀ = 1 − S_y²/4, P_{±2} = (S_y² ± 2S_y)/8 gives thermal averages analytically **[verified]**; observables rotate as V†OV, not VOV† as Kirchmair 2009 and Roos 2008 print **[corrected]**.

**Beyond the leading order** (Roos 2008) **[verified]**. In a frame following the carrier, H_eff/ħ = −ηΩ(J₀ + J₂)S_{y,ψ}(a†e^{i(εt−ζ)} + h.c.) − (η²Ω²/2δ)J₀²S_{y,ψ}² + (2η²Ω²/3δ)J₁²S_{z,ψ}², in Roos's Ω, with Bessel functions at 4Ω/δ and the spin axis rotated by ψ = (4Ω/δ) sin ζ: the force saturates as (J₀ + J₂), and a counter-rotating S_y² and an unwanted S_z² coupling appear, second-order Magnus terms rather than Stark shifts **[corrected]**. Smooth envelopes close only when repeated with the coupling reversed, at 2τ **[corrected]**. In the per-tone Ω the argument and the tilt read 2Ω/δ, so a motion phase φ_m tilts the axis by (2Ω/μ) sin φ_m **[recomputed here]**, its Ω the amplitude the field switches on with, so an envelope rising from zero has no tilt; the played waveforms therefore use equal tone phases, and the start phase is set by the hardware chain (Section 7.10). The exact integration contains these terms; the closed forms are test targets and the intrinsic-budget saturation term (Section 9.6).

**Thermal observables and light shifts.** For |↓↓⟩ and a thermal mode (Kirchmair Eq. 14) **[verified]** p₂ = (1/8)(3 + e^{−16|α|²(n̄+½)} + 4cos(4γ)e^{−4|α|²(n̄+½)}) and p₁ = (1/4)(1 − e^{−16|α|²(n̄+½)}), γ = λt − χ sin εt; Kirchmair's fidelity formula needs −Im ρ_{dd,uu}, not +Im **[corrected]**. Kirchmair's tone imbalance (Ω_{b,r} = Ω(1 ± ξ)) and Innsbruck's third tone, which cancel the carrier light shift **[verified]**, are not implemented: the scheduler detunes both legs by the believed shift (Section 7.5), and the closed forms assume balanced legs **[contested]**.

#### 4.4.2 Phases and geometry

The force couples the mode to σ_{φ_s} = e^{−iφ_s}σ₊ + e^{iφ_s}σ₋ with the phases of Section 4.3.4 **[verified]**. With co-propagating beat waves a common optical phase fluctuation enters φ_s; with counter-propagating ones it enters only φ_m, and only slow, global motion-phase fluctuations cancel at loop closure **[corrected]**. Quantinuum's wrapper pulses and the phase-insensitive geometries are scheduler options; asymmetric detunings add the Stark term of Section 4.3.2.

#### 4.4.3 Multi-mode gates and pulse shaping

Every mode of the driven axis participates. In the Lamb-Dicke limit (Choi et al. 2014 Eqs. 1-3; Zhu, Monroe, Duan 2006) **[verified]**

U(τ) = exp[Σ_i φ̂_iσ_x^i + iΣ_{i<j} χ_{ij}σ_x^iσ_x^j],  φ̂_i = Σ_m(α_{i,m}a_m† − α*_{i,m}a_m),  α_{i,m} = iη_{i,m}∫₀^τ Ω_i(t) sin(μt) e^{iω_m t} dt,

χ_{ij} = Σ_m η_{i,m}η_{j,m} ∫₀^τ dt′∫₀^{t′} dt [Ω_i(t)Ω_j(t′) + Ω_j(t)Ω_i(t′)] sin(μt) sin(μt′) sin[ω_m(t′ − t)],

summed over unordered pairs (Choi's Σ_{i,j} would double χ). The Magnus kernel is odd under t ↔ t′, so χ_ab = K_ab + K_ba; Choi's printed 2Ω_i(t)Ω_j(t′) holds only for proportional envelopes and otherwise misses χ by tens of percent, with opposite signs for its two readings **[corrected]**. Blümel 2021's kernel lacks the pair sum and targets π/8, so it is doubled **[corrected]**. The sine beat note is a convention of these integrals only: the kernels carry the motion phase, Choi's form being φ_m = π/2, while the played waveforms use φ_m = 0 (Section 4.4.1). A pulse applies exp(+iχσσ) with χ signed by the detuning side (tones inside the sidebands give χ > 0), against XX(χ) = exp(−iχσ_xσ_x) (Section 7.6); the scheduler realizes either sign with a π on the second ion's tones, so a solution at −π/4 is valid **[verified]**. XX needs |χ_ab| = π/4 mod π/2 and α_{i,m}(τ) = 0 for both gate ions on every mode either couples to; with equal envelopes and 2N + 1 segments this is linear, and a drive coupling both transverse families needs 4N + 1 segments, Blümel's constraint rows doubling likewise **[corrected]**.

**Amplitude modulation** (Zhu 2006; Choi 2014) **[verified]**: constant-amplitude segments, with the thermal residual-displacement infidelity of Section 4.4.7. Zhu's Eq. 8 disagrees by 2 with the fidelity formula above it, its mode frequency is ω_{ξ,k} = √λ_{ξ,k} ω_z (the printed ω_ξ is tenfold wrong for transverse modes at β_x = 10) and its Eq. 7 omits 1/l_ξ **[corrected]**. **Frequency modulation** (Leung 2018) **[verified]**: H_MS = (Ω/2)Σ_k [S^k a_k† e^{iθ_k(t)} + h.c.] with θ_k(t) = ∫₀^t δ_k dt′ (the printed Eq. 5 needs parentheses to be Hermitian) **[corrected]**, α_k(t) = (Ω/2)(η_{i,k} + η_{j,k})∫₀^t e^{iθ_k}dt′ and error ≈ Σ_k|α_k|². Robustness to a common detuning drift nulls the time-averaged trajectory, which needs enough time-symmetric vertices for every mode's closure and robustness conditions **[recomputed here]**, and β_ij = Ω² cos γ Σ_k η_{i,k}η_{j,k}β_k weights the trajectory areas **[corrected]**. **AMFM** (Blümel 2021) **[verified]**: Fourier-sine controls satisfying closure and its first K frequency derivatives, MA = 0, with infidelity f = (4/5)AᵀFA, χ = AᵀKA and the power-optimal solution the top eigenvector of the projected kernel; the printed (S6), (S8), (S9) drop a complex conjugate **[corrected]**. **Phase modulation** **[background]** (Milne 2020; Green and Biercuk 2015; Lu 2019): per-segment motion phases at fixed amplitude and beat note, carried in the legs' half-difference so the spin phase never moves; since the motion phase is the modulation, Roos's tilt is intrinsic and the leakage correspondingly higher.

All four families solve the same α and χ integrals in the π/4 convention, and every solution is verified by exact integration through the JOINT_EXACT engine in the calibration layer (the control layer never imports the dynamics layer). The exact check adds the carrier, Debye-Waller and off-resonant terms the Lamb-Dicke solvers omit, which make them overpredict χ by one to two percent; the spot check of Section 7.5 corrects the amplitude.

#### 4.4.4 Light-shift (σzσz) gates

**Field-sensitive qubits** (Ballance et al. 2016) **[verified]**: Raman beams at δ = f_z + 2/t_g close K = 2 loops on the axial COM mode inside a spin echo. The error forms: thermal (π²/4)η⁴n̄(2n̄ + 1) for a gate calibrated at n = 0 ((π²/4)η⁴n̄(n̄ + 1) when calibrated on the thermal ensemble); heating ṅt_g/(2K), an empirical fit for small errors; and motional dephasing under L = a†a√(2/τ), α_K t_g/τ with α_K = (8K + 3)/(16K²) (11/16, 19/64, 35/256 at K = 1, 2, 4; the printed 0.686 is a finite-t_g/τ value) **[corrected]**. The paper's 2 µK is n̄hf_z/k_B for n̄ = 0.02, not a Bose temperature, so simulations start from n̄ **[corrected]**, and its 0.83 carrier reduction is J₀(0.851) of axial micromotion, with an unlisted 22% first micromotion sideband **[corrected]**.

**Implementation** **[derived]**. From each qubit level's two-photon self-coupling Ω_gg (Section 4.5.4) the drive is ½ Σ_tones Ω_LS e^{−i(μt − φ)}[w_↓P₀ + w_↑P₁] ⊗ D + h.c. with Ω_LS = (Ω_↑↑ − Ω_↓↓)/2 and weights (Ω_↓↓, Ω_↑↑)/Ω_LS: Zhu's lab-frame σ_z force ħΩ_j cos(Δk·q_j + μt)σ_z^j (Eq. 2) **[verified]** plus a spin-independent force. The same beams' Raman coupling is kept unless its excitation is below 10⁻¹². A force on one level only (weights (−2, 0)) gives a single-qubit phase four times the two-body angle, which the echo cancels; the sign follows the detuning side. The ZZ native on an MS device is the wrapper construction of Section 7.1.

**Clock qubits** (Baldwin et al. 2021) **[corrected]**. |↑⟩ = |F=1, 0⟩ is the only ground state the gate light couples (to the Zeeman-split D₃/₂ |F=1, ±1⟩ levels) and |↓⟩ = |F=0, 0⟩; the paper's opening clause inverts this against the rest of the paper. Four couplings with phases {0, 0, −π/2, π/2} form a polarization gradient detuned by the Zeeman splitting (about 1.4 MHz/G), H ≈ ηΩΣ_j(−1)^j(1 + σ_z^j)(a e^{iδt+iφ_j} + h.c.), Ω ≈ g²/Δ, on the axial stretch mode. One loop gives 2π(ηΩ/δ)² on (σ_z² − σ_z¹)², hence 4π(ηΩ/δ)² on σ_z¹σ_z²; the paper's 8π(Ωη/δ)² is the coefficient over the K = 2 loops of the echo, which gives diag(1, i, i, 1) or, for tones above the mode, its conjugate **[recomputed here]**. Its η carries a factor 4 under the root, and its D-state scattering condition Δ ≪ Δ_hf refers to the 12.6428 GHz ground splitting, under which |↓⟩ never scatters and every scattering event is a which-path measurement **[corrected]**.

#### 4.4.5 Microwave-gradient gates

Srinivas et al. 2021 Eq. 1 **[verified]**: H_I = ħΩ_g J₂(4Ω_μ/δ)(σ_z1 − σ_z2)(a e^{iΔt} + h.c.) with Ω_g = (r₀/4)[∇(B_g·r̂_q)·r̂](∂ω₀/∂B) and Ω_μ = (B_x/2ħ)⟨↓|μ̂_x|↑⟩, decoupling dephasing intrinsically at J₀(4Ω_μ/δ) = 0 (Ω_μ/δ ≈ 0.601). The gradient field is a device parameter and the drive is derived **[derived]**: Ω_μ from the magnetic-dipole coupling, ∂ω₀/∂B from the Zeeman diagonalization (Section 4.5.1), and a force per ion and mode w_{i,m} = ½(∂ω₀/∂B)∇B (r̂·ê_m) c_{i,m} √(ħ/(2m_iω_m)), which is ±2Ω_g on an antisymmetric two-ion mode with r₀ of the total mass. The builder adds Σ_{i,m} w_{i,m} σ_z^i (a_m + a_m†) cos(ω_g t + φ_g) beside the microwave tones, so J₂ and the decoupling emerge from the exact dynamics, or optionally the eliminated J₂ term alone. The qubit must be field sensitive, since ∂ω₀/∂B vanishes at a clock point. The force is a Bessel function of the tone amplitude, so the linear solvers of Section 4.4.3 do not apply; Walsh sign modulation is the family, played through the light-shift spin-echo path.

#### 4.4.6 Smooth gates without ground-state cooling

Hughes et al. 2025 write a geometric-phase gate as H_g = ħδ(t)a†a + (ħΩ_g(t)/2)Ŝ_α(a† + a) **[verified]**; the maximally entangling angle of their Eq. 4 is π/4, not π/2 **[corrected]**. Ramping δ(t) adiabatically gives a temperature-insensitive gate with the printed θ_g ≃ ∫(Ω_g² + α̇²)/δ dt, twice what the displayed H_g gives a closed square pulse, ∫Ω_g²/(2δ) dt **[contested]**. Eqs. 9-10 need ±ħβ̇x̂ for ±iħβ̇p̂, Eq. 16 mislabels β̇/δ as β̇, Eq. 19 prints δ_max^{j} for δ_max^{−j} (freezing the ramp) with a dimensionally inconsistent b, and Eq. 20 omits a†a **[corrected]**. The ramp is therefore not reconstructible: the FM machinery (a callable detuning, Section 4.4.3) is provided and the Hughes ramp is not.

#### 4.4.7 Error model of a gate, as the sources quantify it

Bermudez et al. 2017's closed forms for an N-ion gate are scale estimates with fitted coefficients (Γ_d and Γ_I are never given values) **[corrected]**: motional ε_m ≈ [πN(δ − ω_z)/(2ω_z²t_g)]·0.8(n̄ + 1) + [π²N(N − 1)η⁴/(8N²)](1.2n̄² + 1.4n̄), dephasing 2Γ_d t_g Σ_{ij} e^{−|z_i−z_j|/ξ_c}, intensity Γ_I t_g η²(n̄ + ½) + Γ_I t_g η²(N − 1)/4, and recalibration t_g(0)(1 + η²(2n̄ + 1)/N). The simulator's targets are the forms derived from the exact dynamics **[corrected]**:

- spectator loops: ε_loop = Σ_{m≠g}(2n_m + 1)Σ_i|α_{i,m}(t_g)|² = π²NK Σ_{m≠g}(2n_m + 1)(ω_z/ω_m) sin²(δ_m t_g/2)/(δ_m t_g)², δ_m = μ − ω_m;
- Debye-Waller: (π²/8)N(N − 1)(η⁴/N²)(n̄² + n̄), the bracket being Var(n);
- dephasing: ε_d = (Γ_d t_g/2)Σ_ij C_ij Cov_t(σ_z^i, σ_z^j), at most Nt_g/(2T₂) for uncorrelated and N²t_g/(4T₂) for global noise, T₂ the single-ion 1/e time;
- intensity noise: the ε_I of Section 6.4, whose factor N is Var(Σ_i σ_α^i) = N for a product input and whose 3/(8K) comes from ∫(1 − cos δt)²dt = (3/2)t_g **[recomputed here]**;
- recalibration: η²(2n̄ + 1) without the 1/N.

Kirchmair's budget adds laser frequency noise (2 × 10⁻³ per gate), heating Γ_h t_g/2 and a Rabi-frequency variation δΩ/Ω ≈ 1.4 × 10⁻², the first and last figures attributed there to Benhelm et al. 2008 **[corrected]**; Ballance's largest term is photon scattering (Section 4.3.2). Bermudez's mapping of ε onto Pauli channels has no transcribed formulas and is not implemented (Section 6.8). Section 6 turns these errors into collapse operators and sampled parameters, and Section 9.4 holds the simulated gate to each derived form.

**Reconciliations.** (1) The published thermal Debye-Waller infidelities are one formula, (π²/4)η⁴⟨(n − n_ref)²⟩, with n_ref = n̄ (Sørensen-Mølmer, who re-optimize the duration), 0 (Ballance) or −½ (Zhu): a calibration reference, which a device records **[corrected]**. They are the angle error alone; the exact infidelity of an n = 0-calibrated gate adds residual spin-motion entanglement at the same order, about (π²/4)η⁴n(n + 1) in total for Fock state n **[recomputed here]**. (2) Roos's Table 1 κ/θ = 8/(3η) for the σzσz gate contradicts his Eq. 15's (8/3)η, and his Eq. 22 sign is inverted against Eq. 19 **[corrected]**. (3) An arbitrary angle XX(χ) rescales the null-space amplitude by s with s²χ_max = χ and re-checks closure **[background]**. (4) With ε_ent = Σ_{j,m}|α_{j,m}|²(2n̄_m + 1) the entanglement infidelity, Landsman's (4/5)ε_ent is the average gate infidelity and Leung's Σ|α|² is ε_ent at n̄ = 0, while Zhu's sum is a quarter of ε_ent for Choi's α, consistent only if his α is twice Choi's (his Eq. 8 against his Eq. 7) **[corrected]**; the simulator reports ε_ent and converts. (5) Trout's circuit-level r_d = 15 s⁻¹ and T₂ = 0.5 s do not reconcile under the derived dephasing coefficient **[corrected]**.

### 4.5 Atomic structure and the light-atom coupling chain

Every optical quantity of Sections 4.2 to 4.4 and 8 (Rabi frequencies, light shifts, scattering rates, branching ratios, repump and detection rates, the qubit frequency and its field sensitivity) is a function of a few atomic constants per species and of the beam geometry. A species module holds only measured constants with their citations (Section 4.5.6); this layer derives everything hyperfine- and Zeeman-resolved from them, for any species, in Steck's conventions (Quantum and Atom Optics and the alkali D-line notes), which write out every phase and normalization. The formulas are standard atomic physics **[background]**; the numbers quoted are **[recomputed here]**, and the acceptance anchors are in Section 9.

#### 4.5.1 Fine, hyperfine and Zeeman structure at the operating field

A species module lists per fine-structure level (n, L, S = ½, J) the energy, the lifetime, the hyperfine constants A and B and the measured g_J, and per isotope the nuclear spin I and moment μ_I. For every level the atomic layer diagonalizes, exactly at the configured field (block by block in M = m_I + m_J), the hyperfine-plus-Zeeman Hamiltonian in the |m_I, m_J⟩ basis,

H/h = A I·J + B [3(I·J)² + (3/2) I·J − I(I+1)J(J+1)] / [2I(2I−1)J(2J−1)] + (μ_B/h)(g_J J_z + g_I I_z) B,

in Hz (μ_B/h = 1.399624 MHz/G), converted to rad/s at the layer's boundary (Section 5.6). The B term exists only for I ≥ 1 and J ≥ 1, and g_I = −(μ_I/(I μ_N))(m_e/m_p) in Steck's sign, so both g factors enter with one sign **[background]**; the nuclear-Zeeman forms of Steck, Langer and Harty are each self-consistent and are never mixed (Harty's g with Langer's sign moves the 43Ca+ f₀ by 83.76 kHz) **[corrected]**. The operator is built from powers of I·J: Steck's QAO Eq. 7.132 prints it with half-value ħ exponents, and his uncoupled-basis elements (QAO 7.328-7.331) cover only J = ½ or I = ½ **[corrected]**. Eigenstates are labelled by adiabatic continuation from B = 0, and drive elements use the field-dressed basis.

The eigenvectors give the mixing the dipole elements of Section 4.5.2 need; the eigenvalues give every transition frequency, its first and second derivatives in B (consumed by H_int, Section 5.7, and the dephasing model, Section 6.3) and the field-independent points dν/dB = 0. For J = ½ the Breit-Rabi form checks the diagonalization and never replaces it, because at the stretched states its radicand collapses to (1 ± x)² and it returns the wrong branch beyond |x| = 1 **[verified]**:

E(F = I ± ½, m_F)/h = −ΔE_hfs/(2(2I+1)) + (g_I μ_B/h) m_F B ± (ΔE_hfs/2)[1 + 4m_F x/(2I+1) + x²]^{1/2},  x = (g_J − g_I) μ_B B/(h ΔE_hfs),  ΔE_hfs = A(I + ½).

The low-field slope is g_F μ_B m_F/h with g_F = g_J[F(F+1) + J(J+1) − I(I+1)]/(2F(F+1)) + g_I[F(F+1) − J(J+1) + I(I+1)]/(2F(F+1)); the "1.4 MHz/G" of 171Yb+ is g_F μ_B/h at F = 1, not g_F. The second derivative is stored under two names, d²ν/dB² (Harty's form) and the Taylor coefficient ½d²ν/dB² (Langer's, and that of the 310.8 Hz/G² quoted for 171Yb+), and accumulated phase is 2π∫[ν(B) − ν(B₀)]dt with the bracket in Hz **[corrected]**. A splitting published at nonzero field is never stored as a zero-field constant (the 171Yb+ values 12.642815 to 12.642821 GHz are the second-order shift at 3 to 5 G).

The measured g_J replaces the Landé formula (g_J = 2.000 moves the 43Ca+ clock point by 0.16 G) **[verified]**: 40Ca+ S₁/₂ and D₅/₂ take 2.00225664(9) and 1.2003340(3) (Tommaseo 2003, Chwalla 2009), 351 Hz and 1.64 kHz from Landé on the S₁/₂ splitting and the S(−½) ↔ D(−5/2) line at 4 G, and 43Ca+ reads them from the 40Ca+ table. For the 171Yb+ ground state the table adopts g_J = 2.002615(70) (Han et al. 2025, MCDHF and MRCI), giving the clock transition's Taylor coefficient (g_J − g_I)²μ_B²/(2h²A) = 310.87 ± 0.02 Hz/G² **[recomputed here]**. No measured absolute g_J of 25Mg+ exists (only g_I/g_J), it is not back-solved, and that table stays incomplete **[corrected]**. The level ordering follows from the sign of A (Section 4.5.6) and is derived, never stored. The operating field is a `Field` with magnitude, direction and noise spectrum; its direction is the quantization axis (Section 4.5.3), and a sampled field offset (Section 6.3) becomes per-ion, per-level frequency offsets through the computed sensitivities, never through a hand-entered MHz/G.

#### 4.5.2 Dipole matrix elements, lifetimes, branching and saturation

The decay rate of an upper fine-structure level J′ into a lower level J fixes the reduced dipole element in Steck's normalization (Section 4.5.6 lists the others),

Γ_{J′→J} = ω³/(3πε₀ħc³) · (2J+1)/(2J′+1) · |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|²,

the total rate out of any sublevel of J′ into J, equal to b(J′ → J)/τ **[verified]**. The fine-structure branching of a level with several decay channels is a radial quantity the species module stores with its citation (0.501(15)% for 171Yb+ P₁/₂ → D₃/₂); everything below it is computed. By the Wigner-Eckart theorem (Steck's Rb87 notes, Eqs. 35-37), in the |(J I) F⟩ coupling order,

⟨F m_F|d_q|F′ m_F′⟩ = ⟨J‖d‖J′⟩ (−1)^{F′+J+1+I} √((2F′+1)(2J+1)) {J J′ 1; F′ F I} · (−1)^{F′−1+m_F} √(2F+1) (F′ 1 F; m_F′ q −m_F),

nonzero only for m_F = m_F′ + q **[verified]**. The ingest test checks its sum rules (Rb87 Eqs. 39-40): every upper sublevel decays at Γ_{J′→J}, the relative strength S = (2F′+1)(2F+1)(2J′+1)(3j)²(6j)² of a channel is its branching ratio, and a lower sublevel's absorption strength summed over upper states and polarizations is |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|². The 171Yb+ P₁/₂ F′ = 1 level decays 1/3 to F = 0 and 2/3 to F = 1, F′ = 0 only to F = 1: the branching behind R_d (Section 8.1) and optical pumping (Section 4.2.6). At intermediate field the element is taken between the eigenvectors of Section 4.5.1, which makes the 43Ca+ shelving probabilities of Section 8.1 computed rather than tabulated.

A beam of intensity I has peak field E₀ = √(2I/(ε₀c)) and drives Ω_{ab} = E₀ Σ_q ε_q⟨b|d_q|a⟩/ħ in the ħΩ/2 convention of Section 4.3.1. The saturation intensity I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) takes the vacuum wavelength and the angular partial rate of the cycling branch, Γ = 2π γ_hz × branching (171Yb+ 369.5 nm: 50.83 mW/cm²); it is the cycling value, one third of the random-orientation ħω³Γ/(4πc²), and Acton prints ħ for h **[corrected]**. Only on a closed two-level transition is Ω = Γ√(I/(2I_sat)) and s = I/I_sat = 2Ω²/Γ², and the angular algebra decides which transitions qualify: the stretched σ⁺ cycling line through P₃/₂ has relative strength exactly (2J+1)/(2J′+1) = ½, so Acton's s = I/I_sat is exact there. The 171Yb+ detection line S₁/₂ F = 1 → P₁/₂ F′ = 0 has relative strength 1/3 per Zeeman component; any single beam gives Σ_m Ω_m² = Ω²/3 (the magic angle Ω²/9 each), so with three equally populated ground states the excited fraction is s_∘/18 at low intensity, s_∘ = 2Ω²/Γ², and four equally populated levels give the ceiling Γ/4: the prefactors of Section 8.1, and Noek's s in the (Γ/6, 2/3) form is s_∘/3 = I/(3I_sat) **[corrected]**. The code never accepts "I/I_sat" for a transition it has not classified as closed two-level, and a designated cycling, repump or shelving transition must name a tabulated `Transition`.

#### 4.5.3 Polarization in the atomic frame

The quantization axis is the field direction B̂. A beam's laboratory polarization ε̂ (with ε̂ ⊥ k̂) is decomposed as ε̂ = Σ_q ε_q ê_q with ê_{±1} = ∓(x̂′ ± iŷ′)/√2 and ê₀ = B̂ in a right-handed frame about B̂, ε_q = ê_q*·ε̂, and q = +1, 0, −1 drive σ⁺, π and σ⁻ absorption |F m_F⟩ → |F′ m_F + q⟩; the rotation into the B̂ frame is this layer's construction, and it asserts Σ_q|ε_q|² = 1 **[background]**. This q is the photon-helicity label. Steck's formulas index the operator component by q_op = m_lower − m_upper = −q, because d·ε̂ = Σ_q (−1)^q d_q ε_{−q}, so his tables list σ⁺ under q = −1; the code stores the helicity, converts once where the Wigner-Eckart routine is called, and a test asserts that a pure ε_{+1} beam drives only m → m + 1 **[corrected]**. Steck's Rb87 Eq. 57 omits Γ and returns a population, not a rate **[corrected]**. A beam along B̂ carries no π component, a linear polarization ⊥ B̂ carries equal σ⁺ and σ⁻, and ε̂ ∥ B̂ is pure π. Beams store k̂ and ε̂ in the laboratory frame and the layer returns their q components at each ion, so the polarization purity behind Harty's ac Zeeman shift (Section 4.3.3), optical pumping (Section 4.2.6), coherent population trapping in detection (Section 8.1) and the light-shift gate (Section 4.4.4) is computed. Polarization impurity is a device parameter (ellipticity and misalignment angle), sampled quasi-statically when a spectrum is given.

#### 4.5.4 Raman couplings and light shifts from the level structure

For beams j = 1, 2 and intermediate levels |e⟩ (every hyperfine and Zeeman sublevel of P₁/₂ and P₃/₂ at its field-dependent energy) far detuned, |Δ_e^{(j)}| ≫ Ω, Γ_e with Δ = ω_L − ω_eg (Section 13), adiabatic elimination gives the two-photon Rabi frequency and each ground state's light shift **[background]**:

Ω_{g₁g₂} = Σ_e Ω^{(2)*}_{e g₂} Ω^{(1)}_{e g₁}/(2Δ_e^{(1)}),  δ_g = Σ_j Σ_e |Ω^{(j)}_{e g}|²/(4Δ_e^{(j)}),

with Ω^{(j)}_{eg} = E_j⟨e|d·ε̂_j|g⟩/ħ, so red-detuned light lowers a ground level and the coupling is the coherent sum over all sublevels with their signs. The minus sign of Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.3 (and of the Section 13 row) comes from that paper's Ω ≡ −E₀⟨↑|d·ε̂|↓⟩/(2ħ), a phase the drive phase absorbs. The detuning carries three indices, Δ_i(m_S, beam) = ω_beam − (ω_i − ω_{m_S}); one Δ per intermediate level returns exactly zero for the 0 ↔ 0 clock light shift **[recomputed here]**. The sums have no iγ/2 and serve only far from resonance; within a few linewidths the Bloch solve of Section 4.2.8 takes over. A species without an E1 transition raises here unless its qubit pair is a tabulated E2 pair (Section 4.5.7).

The closed forms of Sections 4.3.2 and 4.4.4 test the sums. Ozeri's clock-qubit Ω_R = (g_b g_r/3)(b₋r₋ − b₊r₊) ω_f/[Δ(Δ − ω_f)] is the sum with the excited hyperfine splittings neglected, the P₁/₂ and P₃/₂ paths entering with opposite signs; his g_{b,r} are peak fields times the stretched cycling element over 2ħ, not a reduced element (about 30% in Ω_R), and his τ_π = π/(2Ω_R) is a half-Rabi convention converted on ingest **[corrected]**. For 171Yb+ (ω_f/2π = 99.84 THz) the paths interfere destructively at every detuning and the 1/Δ² regime is out of reach, so the closed form, not the exponent, is the check **[recomputed here]**. The other tests are Wineland 2003's 9Be+ differential shift (Eq. 2.11) with its polarization null and the vanishing differential vector shift of clock states. Scalar, vector and tensor polarizabilities serve reporting only.

Two approximations enter by default, the only ones added to the optical rotating-wave approximation (Section 1.2), both reported with their size: the adiabatic elimination, whose residual excited population Σ_e|Ω^{(j)}_{eg}|²/(4Δ_e²) is exactly what the scattering channels of Section 4.5.5 use; and the counter-rotating two-photon pairing near twice the qubit splitting, replaced by its second-order shift ~|Ω_{g₁g₂}|²/(2ω₀) (about 2π × 40 Hz at a 1 MHz Raman Rabi frequency on 171Yb+), which calibration absorbs. A comb drive is the same computation over tooth pairs (Section 4.3.7), its residual excited population summed over teeth, so its scattering stays consistent by construction.

#### 4.5.5 Scattering amplitudes: Raman, Rayleigh and differential Rayleigh decoherence

Off-resonant light scatters through the same intermediate sum. The rate of scattering a photon of beam j from ground state a to b is the coherent Kramers-Heisenberg sum over paths,

Γ^{(j)}_{a→b} = Σ_{q′}|Σ_e √Γ_e c_{e→b,q′} Ω^{(j)}_{ea}/(2Δ_e^{(j)})|²,

with √Γ_e inside the sum because the emission element ⟨b|d_{q′}|e⟩ = √(3πε₀ħc³Γ_e/ω_e³) c_{e→b,q′} carries it; Γ_e outside is exact only when every intermediate level has the same total rate (9Be+) and is wrong by up to 11× per channel when P₃/₂ branches to D levels **[corrected]**. The decay amplitudes carry ω³ per channel (each raw element weighted by ω_{e,b}^{3/2} before normalization), so a channel's weight is its partial rate, and Σ_{b,q′}|c|² is the level's tabulated total branching: every upper level of an E1 transition must have its tabulated branchings plus its declared `Level.untabulated_branching` sum to 1 within 10⁻⁹, and an incomplete set is refused, never renormalized over the tabulated channels **[corrected]**. This generalizes Ozeri 2005's Γ_{i,f} = g²γ|a^{(1/2)}/Δ + a^{(3/2)}/(Δ − Δ_f)|²; the 9Be+ amplitudes (Raman a^{(1/2)} = −√2/3, a^{(3/2)} = +√2/3) and Ozeri 2007's closed forms (Eqs. 14-18, linear polarization, equal couplings) are its tests.

Ozeri 2007 as a source **[corrected]**: the sentence after Eq. 18 defines λ = c/ω, contradicting the paper's own tables, and λ = 2πc/ω is used; its Table II Yb+ row contradicts its Eq. 17 (0.2 × 10⁻⁴ and 2 mW printed, 0.29 × 10⁻⁴ and 2.9 mW computed) and is not used; P_total's two equal minima 2√2πγ/ω_f at Δ = (√2 − 1)ω_f and −(√2 + 1)ω_f are no gate-error floor, since the Raman probability per π pulse falls as 1/Δ²; the 5/12 recoil average is isotropic (9/20 for fixed polarization); its D(β) is the conjugate of QuTiP's; its ε_S is an upper bound; and its P_Rayleigh includes the D-level Raman channel for Ca+, Sr+, Ba+ and Yb+. Here P_Rayleigh is the elastic amplitude sum, the D-level finals count as leakage in ε_S, ε_D = fP_total with f derived from the tabulated D branchings (9.69 × 10⁻³ for 171Yb+ at 355 nm), and the excess of Ozeri's form is reported, not subtracted.

The channels are Lindblad operators on the joint space (Section 5.7), one per emitted polarization and emission direction, with rates scaling with the played intensity:

- **Raman** (b ≠ a): √Γ_{a→b}|b⟩⟨a| ⊗ R, R the recoil operator of Section 4.2.8. Outside the qubit pair this is **leakage**, read bright or dark by the final manifold (Section 8.1). `Physics(internal_levels=d)` with d > 2 simulates it, switching the scattering channels on (leakage into the sink level, spin flips, differential Rayleigh) without recoil displacements, which cost 30 to 50 times more and are an explicit choice (`Physics(scattering="channels", scattering_recoil="minimal" or "vector")`); at d = 2 the per-pulse scattering probabilities are estimates in the intrinsic budget (Section 4.3.2).
- **Rayleigh** (b = a) with equal amplitudes for the two qubit states: identity on the spin, acting on the motion through R only, so it heats without decohering.
- **Differential Rayleigh** (Uys et al. 2010), r_↑ ≠ r_↓: the ½(r_↑ − r_↓)σ_z part dephases the qubit; it vanishes for clock states far from the fine structure, not for Zeeman qubits.

Uys et al. 2010 **[verified]**: with signed amplitudes A^{i→j}_{J,λ} = b_λ⟨j|d·ε̂*_{λ+(i−j)}|J, λ+i⟩⟨J, λ+i|d·ε̂_λ|i⟩/(δ_{iJλ}μ²) normalized to the stretched element μ, Γ_{ij} = Ω_R²γ Σ_λ(Σ_J A^{i→j}_{J,λ})² with Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ), and Γ_el = Ω_R²γ Σ_λ(Σ_J A^{d→d}_{J,λ} − Σ_{J′} A^{u→u}_{J′,λ})², entering as 𝓛_el ρ = −(Γ_el/4)[ρ − σ_zρσ_z], so the coherence decays at (Γ_Ram + Γ_el)/2 while populations relax at the Raman rates. Its conventions **[corrected]**: the amplitudes are signed (per-term moduli destroy Γ_el); λ indexes the incident component; Ω_R is half the Ω of Section 4.3.1, so feeding that Ω in is a factor 4; Σ_λ|b_λ|² = 1 is asserted; Uys's δ is positive for red detuning; and Γ_el is a dephasing rate, zero for equal amplitudes and 2(Γ_dd + Γ_uu) for opposite ones, so where the two elastic rates are equal Ozeri 2007's rate-difference estimate vanishes while Γ_el is maximal. The operators are ½√Γ_el σ_z, √Γ_du|u⟩⟨d| and √Γ_ud|d⟩⟨u|: the prefactor Γ_el/4 is correct and is not "fixed" to Γ_el/2 (√(Γ_el/2)σ_z decays twice as fast) **[recomputed here]**.

Wineland 2003's Eqs. 2.13-2.18 **[corrected]**: the eight-channel rationals are relative line strengths per polarization component normalized to the stretched σ₊ element, not branching ratios, each bracket summing to 1 for ²P₁/₂ and 2 for ²P₃/₂; its Ω is a half-angle amplitude (a π pulse at Ωτ_π = π/2), converted on ingest; Δ is referenced to ²P₁/₂. The optimum is of R_SE/|Ω|, never of R_SE: x = Δ/ω_F = √2 − 1, P_SE = 2√2πγ/ω_F for the m_F = 0 clock line ((8π/√6)γ/ω_F for |2,2⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩), and δ_{0↔0} = −(ω₀/γ)R_SE from the same bracket, a polarization-independent shift that cannot be nulled **[recomputed here]**. R_SE over-estimates decoherence when |Δ| ≫ ω_F, where Γ_el replaces it **[contested]**. The recoil operator for these dissipators is the kernel of Section 4.2.8, untested in this use (Section 12).

#### 4.5.6 What the species database stores and what it derives

| Stored per species, with citation | Derived by the atomic layer |
|---|---|
| Mass; nuclear spin I; nuclear moment μ_I with its shielding convention | g_I; the full ∣m_I, m_J⟩ hyperfine-Zeeman spectrum of every level at any field; state labels by adiabatic connection |
| Per level: energy, lifetime, A, B, g_J | Zeeman energies and mixings; transition frequencies with dB and d²B sensitivities; clock points; the level ordering |
| Per fine-structure transition: vacuum wavelength, Γ, branching, multipole | reduced dipole element; I_sat of closed two-level transitions; every resolved dipole element, decay branching and absorption strength |
| Nothing about polarization, Raman couplings or scattering | q components of every beam at every ion; Ω_{g₁g₂}, light shifts, comb sums and elimination residuals; Raman, leakage and Rayleigh rates and their Lindblad operators |
| The designated qubit pair, cycling, repump and shelving transitions | whether each is closed two-level; bright and dark manifolds for the readout POVM |

Tables exist for 171Yb+, 40Ca+, 43Ca+, 133Ba+, 137Ba+, 9Be+, 25Mg+ and 88Sr+; a table lacking a constant raises `IncompleteSpeciesTable` naming it, and `available()` lists those that build (171Yb+, 40Ca+, 43Ca+, 137Ba+, 9Be+, 88Sr+).

**Conventions** **[corrected]**. Three dipole normalizations coexist: Steck's ⟨J‖d‖J′⟩, the stretched cycling element of Ozeri, Uys and Wineland (smaller by √(1/2) on a J = ½ → 3/2 line) and Edmonds' round bracket (larger by √(2j + 1)). Steck's Γ is the m-independent total rate out of J_e into J_g, so on a unit-branching D2 line it is 1/τ, the γ of those papers, with no second factor 2 (applying the degeneracy ratio again is a factor 4). Every stored element and rate carries its convention and is converted once; elements are derived from the stored lifetime, never typed in. The two hyperfine factors are directed: S_FF′ = (2F′ + 1)(2J + 1){…}² sums to one over excited F′, b(F′ → F) = (2F + 1)(2J′ + 1){…}² over ground F. The sign of A_hfs is an input sources rarely print, inferred from μ_I when absent: 9Be+, 43Ca+, 133Ba+ (whose |0, 0⟩ qubit state is the upper one) and 171Yb+ ³D[3/2]₁/₂ are inverted, and A > 0 would remove the 43Ca+ and 9Be+ clock points, the ingest test of the sign. Steck's g_S = +2.0023193 is opposite to CODATA's g_e and is used throughout. Nuclear moments are declared per table in their shielding convention (9Be+ corrected, 25Mg+ uncorrected, 43Ca+ the free-ion moment of Hanley et al. 2021); mixing them is a 0.2 to 0.25% error in g_I.

Apparatus figures (trap-rf and microwave ac Zeeman shifts, trap shifts already subtracted from f₀, Ramsey contrast times, differential Stark detunings, the quantization field, beam powers, waists and collection efficiency) are device scalars defaulting to zero or none, never species constants. Conditional provenance is a field of the cited value: the 171Yb+ P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ branching 0.00501(15) holds only with τ(P₁/₂) = 8.07 ns, because the fit measures γR, and the D₃/₂ g_J 0.79917(3) only with the ground state's calculated 2.002615(70), because Galstyan et al. 2026 measure the F = 1 g_F ratio 0.998060(5) by microwave spectroscopy (Meggers 1967's observed 0.802 is 2.8 × 10⁻³ higher) **[verified]**. For 171Yb+ the 6p g_J are the Landé factors with Steck's g_S (NIST ASD's 0.667 and 1.333 are their g_S = 2 roundings), and the D₅/₂, ²F₇/₂ and bracket values are Meggers 1967 observations, good to a few 10⁻³ **[extracted]**. Each decay channel is stored with its own wavelength (the 171Yb+ P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ channel is at 2.438 µm, 935.2 nm being the D₃/₂ → ³D[3/2]₁/₂ repump), and the repump level's 297.143 nm channel is a declared `Level.untabulated_branching`, because its lifetime is not primary-verified **[extracted]**. The tables do not copy NIST ASD's Ba II Landé column (its 1.12 for 5d ²D₅/₂ is wrong; the measured 1.20036739(24) of Arnold et al. 2020 is used) **[corrected]**; the 9Be+ g_J is the measured 2.00226239(31), Dickopf et al. 2024's value being a calculation **[verified]**; the 9Be+ ²P₃/₂ A and B are theory values in tension with experiment and with sign(B) = sign(Q) **[contested]**; and the 40Ca+ P₃/₂ linewidth quoted as 23.4 MHz matches no measurement, so the table takes Meir et al. 2020's lifetime **[contested]**.

#### 4.5.7 Electric-quadrupole coupling for optical qubits

The 40Ca+ 729 nm and 88Sr+ 674 nm S₁/₂ ↔ D₅/₂ lines are electric-quadrupole (E2) transitions outside the dipole algebra. Sources: James 1998 (Sec. 4-5, Appendix), Roos 2000 (thesis), Schindler et al. 2013.

**Drive.** The E2 drive on |S₁/₂, m⟩ ↔ |D₅/₂, m′⟩ is the travelling-wave Hamiltonian of Section 4.3.1 with the single beam's k, |k| = 2π/λ_vac, so its Lamb-Dicke parameters and sidebands are those of the dipole drives **[verified]**; ηâ†σ₊ is the blue sideband at Δ = +ν (the thesis prints the opposite sign once) **[corrected]**. James's standing-wave construction is not used; its node and antinode positions (Eqs. 4.9, 4.13) are a factor 2 too large **[corrected]**.

**Rabi frequency** **[corrected]** (James Eqs. 4.8, 5.2-5.4; Roos Eqs. 3.17, 3.26, whose printed second line drops a factor k):

Ω_{mm′} = (eE₀k/(2ħ))|⟨S₁/₂‖r²C^{(2)}‖D₅/₂⟩||Λ(m, m′)||g^{(q)}|,

with C^{(2)}_q = √(4π/5)Y_{2q} the Racah tensor (r²Y_{2m} is √(4π/5) off), Λ the 3-j symbol (1/2 2 5/2; −m q m′) and g^{(q)} = c^{(q)}_{ij}ε_i n_j the contraction of polarization and propagation direction with James's rank-2 tensors. Asserted conventions: q = m − m′ = −Δm (load-bearing for circular light); the tensors are c^{(q)} = (2/3)b^{(q)} with b^{(q)}_{ij}r_ir_j = r²C^{(2)}_q, normalized to Σ|c^{(q)}_{ij}|² = 2/3, not 1 (unit normalization inflates Ω by √(3/2)) and not the conjugate duals (which reverse q for circular light); and a carrier π pulse takes t = π/Ω (twice the Boulder value). |Δm| ≤ 2 admits 10 of the 12 Zeeman pairs **[verified]**. With B̂ = ẑ, k̂ = (sin φ, 0, cos φ) and ε̂ = (cos γ cos φ, sin γ, −cos γ sin φ): g^{(0)} = ½|cos γ sin 2φ|, g^{(±1)} = (1/√6)|cos γ cos 2φ + i sin γ cos φ|, g^{(±2)} = (1/√6)|½ cos γ sin 2φ + i sin γ sin φ|, γ = arcsin|ε̂·ŷ| (Roos's wording of γ is degenerate) **[corrected]**, with (g^{(0)})² + 2(g^{(±1)})² + 2(g^{(±2)})² = 1/3, only |Δm| = 1 driven at φ = 0, only |Δm| = 2 at φ = γ = 90°, and Δm = 0 never with k ⊥ B **[verified]**. The stretched |Δm| = 2 component at φ = γ = 90° has the global maximum |Λ|g = 1/6, where Ω = (eE₀/ħ)√(5λ_vac³A/(64π³cα)) **[verified]**; James's alternative σ^{(E2)} formula is the same object **[corrected]**.

**Reduced element from the lifetime** **[corrected]** (James Eqs. 5.6-5.10; Roos Eq. 3.27): A^{(E2)} = cαk⁵|⟨S‖r²C^{(2)}‖D⟩|²/(15(2j′ + 1)) with j′ = 5/2 the upper level (the lower 2j + 1 is √3 off), cα = e²/(4πε₀ħ), and A the branch rate into S₁/₂ (1/τ to 10⁻⁵ for Ca+; Sr+ has a 9 × 10⁻⁵ M1 branch to D₃/₂, an LS estimate **[background]**). k is the vacuum wavenumber; James's calcium wavelengths and Roos's table are in air, which biases k by 274 ppm **[corrected]**. The 40Ca+ D₅/₂ lifetime is 1.168(7) s (Barton et al. 2000, concordant with Kreuter et al. 2005), giving 9.73 a₀² **[corrected]**; the 88Sr+ one is 390.8(1.6) ms (Letchumanan et al. 2005), giving 13.81(3) a₀², against 14.70 a₀² from the superseded 345(33) ms **[verified]**; its blackbody deshelving is eleven orders below the decay rate at 300 K. The 88Sr+ wavelength comes from the NIST clock frequency (674.025591 nm), not from the level energies or NIST's air Ritz value **[corrected]**. The E2/E1 amplitude ratio is ka₀/2 ≈ α/16, not α/2 **[corrected]**.

**Metastable lifetimes** (Kreuter et al. 2005) **[verified]**. Measured and theoretical 40Ca+ D lifetimes are never merged: the D₅/₂ record keeps the measured 1.168 s with Kreuter's signed one-sided systematics; the all-order theory (1165(11) ms from A_vw = (1.11995 × 10¹⁸/λ[Å]⁵)|⟨v‖Q‖w⟩|²/(2j_v + 1) s⁻¹, vacuum wavelengths, upper-state degeneracy) is a cross-check on the reduced element **[recomputed here]**. That prefactor is Johnson's (1/15)(ω/c)⁵ normalization; the (1/75) convention gives one fifth of the rate (its elements √5 larger), and each cited element of the 40Ca+ table states its normalization. The theory errors are correlated and are not quadrature-combined in the lifetime ratio **[corrected]**. Three channels are physical inputs, off by default (`MetastableChannels`): blackbody M1 mixing between the D levels, W₁₂ = A₁₂n̄(ν, T), the Bose occupation multiplying the rate (dividing is low by n̄² = 8.75 at 300 K), upward rate ×6/4 **[recomputed here]**; collisional quenching and j-mixing, R = Σ_sΓ_sp_s/(k_BT) from cited coefficients in cm³/s, a partner without one raising (the source's printed bound is below its own coefficients' 4.36 × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹ at 10⁻¹¹ mbar) **[contested]**; and reshelving, ṗ_D = −Γp_D + R(1 − p_D), whose floor R/(Γ + R) makes a bare-exponential fit return a longer lifetime **[recomputed here]**.

**Zeeman structure** **[verified]**. With I = 0 the LS Landé factors (2, 2/3, 4/3, 4/5, 6/5 for S₁/₂, P₁/₂, P₃/₂, D₃/₂, D₅/₂) place component (m, m′) at ν₀ + (g_D m′ − g_S m)μ_B B/h: the ten components span 5.6μ_B B/h, with the table's measured S₁/₂ and D₅/₂ g_J (Section 4.5.1) the Δm = 0 qubit S(−½) ↔ D(−½) moves 0.5612 MHz/G and the stretched S(−½) ↔ D(−5/2) line 2.7988 MHz/G (0.560 and 2.799 with the Landé factors); Section 4.5.1 reproduces these as its I = 0 case. Schindler's p_{|1⟩} is the D₅/₂ excitation probability **[corrected]**.

**Dissipation, dephasing, crosstalk** **[corrected]**. The collapse operators are √(A w(m, m′))|S, m⟩⟨D, m′| with the A that fixed Ω and w the 3-j squares normalized to one over m at fixed m′; at A ≈ 0.86 s⁻¹ they are negligible over microsecond gates and are not added by default. Dephasing (laser frequency noise below about 1 ms, Zeeman sensitivity above) is not exponential, so there is no default σ_z term: it goes through the filter function of Section 6.9 with F = 4 sin²(ωT/2) and S_b = S_δ/4, C(T) = exp[−(2/π)∫₀^∞dω S_b(ω)F(ω)/ω²], or a sampled phase trajectory (Section 6.3). Shot-to-shot intensity noise is a per-shot Rabi sample with ΔΩ/Ω = ½ΔI/I on a resonant carrier and ΔI/I for an ac-Stark operation. Crosstalk is the amplitude ratio ε_ij of Section 6.6; Schindler prints it inverted and squared (his 18% is the amplitude ratio, 3.3% the intensity ratio).

**The E2 record.** An E2 `Transition` (vacuum wavelength, the upper level's rate, branching) per line: 40Ca+ (729.347 nm, 1.168 s, 1) and 88Sr+ (674.025591 nm, 0.3908 s, 1 − 9 × 10⁻⁵). The module derives k, A, the reduced element, the ten Rabi frequencies for any geometry and the Lamb-Dicke parameters from the same k (Schindler's quoted η refer to an unnamed mode; only his geometry is used). The ac Stark shift of a driven component by the nine others is second order in the same Ω(m, m′) table (lower level +|Ω|²/(4Δ), upper −|Ω|²/(4Δ), a degenerate component raising), with no published number to validate it (Section 12) **[contested]**.

# Part III. Numerics, noise, control and readout

## 5. Hilbert space, frames and numerical strategy

### 5.1 Composite space

Each ion is a qudit of dimension d, 2 by default; `Physics(internal_levels=d)` with d > 2 adds the leakage levels the species marks reachable (for 171Yb+ the other F = 1 Zeeman states; for an optical qubit the other D sublevels) and switches on the scattering channels that fill them (Section 4.5.5). Each resolved mode is a truncated oscillator, and the space is the tensor product of ions then modes. A `HilbertSpace` is a declaration that allocates nothing: operators are built lazily, once, and cached, and the guards of Section 11.5 are checked on the declaration before any joint operator exists.

Two truncation schemes, selectable per run:

- **Per-mode cutoff** n_max,m: d_m = n_max,m + 1 levels per mode, motional dimension Π_m d_m (Section 11 quotes d_m); exact for any product of per-mode populations.
- **Excitation-number restriction (ENR)**: the total excitation of a group of M modes capped at N_exc with QuTiP's `enr_*` functions, dimension C(M + N_exc, N_exc) instead of (n_max + 1)^M, its displacement operators built inside the ENR space (Section 5.1.1).

The default is per-mode cutoffs for the modes a pulse resolves (its driven modes and the spectators the contribution criterion of Section 11.3 selects), every other mode frozen or dropped (Section 5.2), because the exact displacement operator, the factorized kernel (Section 11.3) and the boundary monitor assume a product space. ENR serves a group of cold, undriven modes that must still be carried (mode class "enr") **[corrected]**: `ptrace` on it raises in QuTiP, so its marginals are index sums over the ENR basis; every operator and state the space hands out carries the space's dims, the group one factor of dimension C(M + N_exc, N_exc); its displacement is unitary but wrong near the cap (⟨n₀⟩ = 2.2412 against 2.25 after α = 1.5 at N_exc = 6), so N_exc ≈ 2n_phys and the top-shell population is its only convergence diagnostic; and it cannot be applied mode by mode, so drives on an ENR mode are assembled. A Mølmer-Sørensen gate displaces its mode transiently by |α| up to about 1, which the cap's margin (Section 5.1.1) absorbs.

#### 5.1.1 Constructing the displacement operator in a truncated space

The **matrix exponential of the truncated generator**, expm[iη(a + a†)] (QuTiP's `displace`; the ENR ladder operators in an ENR space), is unitary to 10⁻¹⁵ but wrong near the truncation boundary. The **analytic elements** ⟨n′|D(iη)|n⟩ = e^{−η²/2}(n_<!/n_>!)^{1/2}(iη)^{|n′−n|}L^{(|n′−n|)}_{n_<}(η²) are exact element by element but not unitary (the top column loses 9% of its norm at d_m = 10 and η = 0.1). The modulus in the exponent holds for a purely imaginary displacement; for a general α the element is α^{n′−n} below the diagonal (n′ > n) and (−α*)^{n−n′} above it **[corrected]**. The exponential against the analytic elements **[recomputed here]**:

| d_m (Fock levels) | η | max element difference, block n, n′ < d_m/2 | analytic norm loss, column d_m/2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 1.0 | 2 × 10⁻¹³ / 2 × 10⁻⁶ / 1 × 10⁻³ | 3 × 10⁻⁸ / 6 × 10⁻³ / 0.3 |
| 10 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 1.0 | 3 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 7 × 10⁻⁸ / 2 × 10⁻⁴ | 2 × 10⁻¹⁰ / 1 × 10⁻³ / 0.18 |
| 20 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 1.0 | 1 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 1 × 10⁻¹⁵ / 3 × 10⁻⁹ | 1 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 3 × 10⁻⁸ / 3 × 10⁻³ |
| 40 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 1.0 | 7 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 8 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 7 × 10⁻¹⁶ | 1 × 10⁻¹⁵ / 4 × 10⁻¹⁶ / 3 × 10⁻⁹ |

In an ENR space the operator is the exponential of the sum generator Σ_m iη_m(a_m + a_m†) (within 2 × 10⁻¹⁰ of the analytic elements for n₁ + n₂ ≤ N_exc/2 at η = 0.1, N_exc = 6). The per-mode generators do not commute there, so the product of per-mode exponentials, the physical operator the factorized kernel applies, differs near the cap and is used in product spaces only. The rules: (i) the Hamiltonian uses the matrix exponential, whose unitarity keeps the norm and the boundary monitor meaningful; (ii) the analytic elements are the oracle: each resolved mode's `ModeTruncation(mode, d, expected_n_range, eta_max)` asserts that the exponential's interior elements match them over the declared range to the table's value (interpolated in η, floored at 10⁻¹²) whenever the margin above that range is at least four levels, and below that reports the difference and leaves the boundary monitor in charge, so the cap-raising retries of Section 5.5 can start small; the required margins (about 6 levels at η ≤ 0.1, 10 at 0.5, 20 at 1) are a fixture interpolated in η, and a GATE_LOCAL step derives its own (Section 5.5); (iii) the table Ω_{n′,n} of Section 4.3.1 and every closed-form test use the analytic form; (iv) the boundary monitor makes either construction trustworthy.

D is dense within each mode (|n⟩ couples to every |n′⟩ with weight of order η^{|n′−n|}(n_>!/n_<!)^{1/2}/|n′ − n|!), so the drive operator σ₊^i ⊗ Π_m D_m has 2^{N−1}Π_m d_m² non-zeros, and this, not the step count, sets the cost model of Section 11. Dropping small elements removes almost nothing at d_m ≈ 8 and η ≈ 0.1; the remedy is to apply D as mode-local factors (the factorized kernel of Section 11.3).

### 5.2 Frames and the form of the Hamiltonian

The Hamiltonian is built in the frame rotating at each ion's qubit frequency (the optical rotating-wave approximation) and in the Schrödinger picture for the motion, H₀ = Σ_m ħω_m a_m†a_m (plus static qubit-frequency offsets), with every drive on ion i

H_drive,i(t) = (ħ/2) Σ_tones Ω_i,tone(t) e^{−i(μ_tone t − φ_tone(t))} σ₊^{(i)} ⊗ D_i(Δk) + h.c.,  φ_tone ≡ Δk·X̄_i − Δφ_i,

where D_i(Δk) = Π_m D_m(iη_im) over all modes, μ_tone is the tone's detuning from the carrier (Section 13), and Δφ_i is the phase of the beat note written E ∝ cos(ω_L t − Δk·r + Δφ_i): φ₂ − φ₁ for a Raman pair with Δk = k₁ − k₂ and E_j ∝ cos(k_j·r − ω_j t + φ_j), −φ₁ for a single-beam optical drive **[corrected]**. X̄_i carries the shot's sampled stray-field displacement u₀ = QE_dc/(mω²) (Section 4.1.1); the addressed ion's own drift is absorbed by the frame alignment of Section 7.5, leaving Δk·(u₀(j) − u₀(i)) on a neighbour j **[corrected]**. A virtual RZ(θ) shifts every later φ_tone by φ → φ − θ in time order (Section 7.6), pinned by RZ(0.1) then GPi2(0) on |0⟩ equalling GPi2(−0.1)|0⟩ (Section 9.6); the native gate phase, the Mølmer-Sørensen spin phase (Section 4.4.2) and crosstalk vector addition (Section 6.6) all refer to this sign. Index 0 is the lower qubit level, σ₊ = |1⟩⟨0|, and the energy operator σ_z = |1⟩⟨1| − |0⟩⟨0| of H_int and H_Stark is the negative of the computational Pauli Z of the native-gate matrices; the two are never aliased.

The operator part σ₊ ⊗ D_i is time independent and built once, each tone a complex scalar coefficient (`QobjEvo([H0, [A, c(t)]])`); the exact displacement carries the carrier, every sideband, every Debye-Waller factor and the off-resonant terms without expansion, and the textbook model is `BuilderOptions(lamb_dicke_order=…, rwa=True)`. H₀ sets the fastest timescale (|n⟩ rotates at nω_m; about 14 to 45 integrator steps per period of the highest mode in the Schrödinger picture) **[recomputed here]**, so the engine integrates every ket segment (`sesolve`, `mcsolve`) in the exact rotating frame ψ = e^{−iH₀t}φ of the diagonal H₀ = H_mot + H_int (plus a constant Stark shift): phases are applied to the state around the unchanged drive operators, collapse operators that are eigenoperators of ad_{H₀} acquire their e^{iλt}, and outputs are rotated back. It is an exact change of variable, 3 to 8 times fewer right-hand-side evaluations for the same states (Section 11.1) **[recomputed here]**; segments it cannot cover (a non-diagonal static part such as an anharmonic term, a non-eigenoperator collapse such as a recoil kick or intensity noise) and every `mesolve` segment run in the Schrödinger picture (`SegmentReport.frame` names the picture used). The interaction picture proper, `BuilderOptions(frame="interaction")`, expands σ₊ ⊗ D into Π_m(2n_max,m + 1) sideband operators e^{ikω_m t}σ₊ ⊗ A_k, truncated at |k| ≤ `k_max` with the dropped weight Σ_{|k|>k_max}‖A_k‖ reported; it holds the evaluation count flat but ran 5 to 10 times slower at n_max = 41.

Coefficients are QuTiP 5.3 callables `f(t, **kwargs)`, never strings (which need Cython). Envelopes, phases and detuning schedules are functions of the time since the pulse start (a constant is a square pulse, an array is sampled uniformly and cubic-spline interpolated); the beat-note phase is μt in absolute time for a phase-continuous tone (`phase_mode="continuous"`, Section 7.10), μ(t − t_start) under `"reset"`, and μ(0)t_start + ∫₀^τ μ dτ′ for a phase-continuous detuning schedule, whose μ(0)t_g the per-gate reset cancels (Section 7.10). A coefficient's static factors are the sample's Rabi scale, ε_ij, J₀(β) (Section 4.3.6) and the frozen spectators' Debye-Waller factors; sampled phase and amplitude noise enter as array coefficients.

**Mode classes.** Every mode gets exactly one class per run (`Diagnostics.mode_class`) from the residual displacement α_m(τ) and entangling-angle contribution χ_m(τ) of the Section 4.4.3 integrals over the played waveforms at the prepared occupations **[corrected]**:

- **dropped** when |α_m|²(2n̄_m + 1) < 10⁻⁶, |χ_m| < 10⁻⁴ rad and the shot-to-shot spread of its Debye-Waller rotation, max_i η²_{i,m}√(n̄_m(n̄_m + 1)), is below 3 × 10⁻⁴ rad: no tensor factor, no Debye-Waller factor, no Fock branch, and its summed contribution reported as a loss nothing absorbs (`Diagnostics.dropped_contribution`);
- **frozen** when |α_m|²(2n̄_m + 1) < `freeze_alpha_max` (10⁻⁴) and |χ_m| < `freeze_chi_max_rad` (0.05): outside the joint space, every drive multiplied by the exact Debye-Waller factor e^{−η_m²/2}L_{n_m}(η_m²) of its Fock state, and its χ_m loss (`Diagnostics.frozen_contribution`) absorbed by the exact spot check on the reduced space, so a frozen run is calibrated to the joint run's angle;
- **resolved** otherwise, with a cap from Section 5.5.

A mode no entangling gate touches is frozen if a carrier pulse couples to it and dropped if nothing does. A run enumerates a frozen mode's Fock states as weighted branches of the initial mixture (Section 5.3); a direct engine call draws them per dynamical sample and says so. Inside one shot the factor is always a Fock state's, never the ensemble mean e^{−η²(n̄ + ½)} (Section 13). The frozen error bound is the off-resonant excitation Σ(η_mΩ√(n_m + 1)/(μ − ℓω_m))² over the nearest sidebands (`Diagnostics.frozen_excitation_bound`); a tone exactly on a frozen mode's sideband makes it infinite, and GATE_LOCAL's `discrepancy_bound` carries the infinity; the guard |μ_tone − ℓω_m| > 20η_mΩ√(n_m + 1) is a note, never a refusal. Freezing makes Doppler-cooled spectators affordable (n̄ ≈ 10 to 20 would need n_max ≈ 100); the transverse spectators of a long chain within tens of kilohertz of the tones cannot be frozen, so long chains run in GATE_LOCAL (Section 11.3).

### 5.3 Solvers

The options named below are fields of `Numerics`.

- **Pure states** (`sesolve`): `dop853` at atol 10⁻¹⁰, rtol 10⁻⁸, nsteps 10⁷ at every mode dimension (a 10⁻⁸ floor above d_m = 100 is 8× less accurate at the same speed); `dop853` and `vern9` agree to about 6 × 10⁻⁷ in norm, which is what "identical results" means **[recomputed here]**. The ladder `dop853` → `vern9` → `vern9` at atol 10⁻⁸ with max_step a fourteenth of the fastest period escalates only on QuTiP's `IntegratorException` and records every retry. Never a multistep method: `adams` aborts at n_max ≈ 40, and BDF is unstable or damped on the imaginary-axis spectrum of −iH.
- **Branches.** The prepared state is diagonal in the computational and Fock bases (pumped populations, thermal weights of the carried modes, frozen modes' Fock states), so its branches evolve as pure states recombined with their weights, exactly up to those below `branch_weight_min` (10⁻⁶), whose weight is reported (`Diagnostics.dropped_branch_weight`) and renormalized; a cutoff that keeps no state of a factor is refused with a `RunError`. A density matrix handed to the engine is split the same way whenever no segment can carry a collapse operator (`JointExactEngine.is_unitary`), including a run with the device's channels on whose rates are all zero.
- **Density matrices** (`mesolve`) up to `mesolve_dimension_max` (128): the reference for dissipative channels, not a path at scale (one Liouvillian application costs about 2D state-vector products); the drive operators are CSR products of per-mode exponentials, converted to CSR before `liouvillian`, since a dense generator makes a dense Liouvillian.
- **Trajectories** (`mcsolve`, parallel map) above it: `ntraj` (64) per pure initial state, seeds spawned from the root seed (Section 3.4), on the same integrator ladder. With `improved_sampling` the no-jump member carries `McResult.deterministic_weights` and the stochastic ones `McResult.runs_weights`; shots are drawn from that weighted mixture, never uniformly **[corrected]**. Improved sampling applies when the trajectory path is entered once per schedule; otherwise trajectories are uniformly weighted and the notes say so. The count is fixed by default; `trajectory_target_tol` enables a two-phase count (a serial `target_tol` run, then a replay of that many keyed seeds), because QuTiP 5.3.1's `target_tol` accepts a zero-variance first batch.
