- **Propagators** on internal-state-only spaces only: the segment propagator is integrated once through the same ladder, exactly (no segmentation), and cached on the built Hamiltonian's fingerprint, which digests the device hash, the space, the mode frequencies, the sampled tones, offsets and Fock states and the builder options (Section 11.3). No propagator is built on a joint space.
- QuTiP's Floquet and Bloch-Redfield solvers are not used: explicit rf dynamics is outside the builder (Section 4.1.1), the Bloch layer takes periodic steady states from one-period propagators (Section 4.2.8), and coloured noise during pulses is a sampled time series (Section 6.1).

### 5.4 Fidelity levels

- **JOINT_EXACT.** The full state of all ions and resolved modes goes through every pulse; the only approximations are the truncation and the mode classes of Section 5.2. The practical range is a joint dimension of about 2 × 10³ to 4 × 10³ (two or three ions with two or three resolved modes, or more ions with one or two and frozen spectators), bounded by the guards of Section 11.5 (`joint_dimension_max` 4096, `nnz_max` 2 × 10⁷). `level="auto"` sends a larger run to GATE_LOCAL (`Diagnostics.level_reason`); an explicit `level="JOINT_EXACT"` above the guards, or a cap growing past them, is refused with the numbers and the two knobs named. Every validation result of Section 9 runs at this level.
- **GATE_LOCAL.** Each gate step (the pulses of one gate piece in `Schedule.targets`, merged with whatever overlaps them, so a segmented Mølmer-Sørensen waveform is one step) runs exactly on its own space: the addressed ions, ions receiving crosstalk above `crosstalk_threshold` (10⁻³) and the modes Section 5.2 resolves at the tracked occupations. Its channel is extracted by state-based process tomography along one of three routes (`TomographyRecord.route`), which agree to the solver tolerance: *states*, the reference and the only route for a dissipative step, propagates Π_i d_i² pure inputs per motional branch (`mcsolve` with n_traj ≈ 1/ε_map at `map_accuracy` ε_map = 10⁻³ when channels are active) and fits the Choi matrix by least squares; *isometry*, for a unitary step, propagates only the Π_i d_i basis kets per branch and reads the Kraus operators off the Stinespring isometry they form; *propagator*, on an internal-state-only space, takes the cached segment propagator as the branch's Kraus operator. The Choi matrix is projected onto the intersection of the completely positive cone and the trace-preserving set by Dykstra's alternating projection with both residuals reported, since a positive-semidefinite projection alone would change the register's normalization at every step **[corrected]**. The map acts on the register (a density matrix up to `register_dm_max_qubits` = 12, a pure-state ensemble beyond), and the motional model takes each step mode's reduced state and keeps the others' thermal states.
- GATE_LOCAL traces out the spin-motion and mode-mode correlations a step leaves. Each step reports that loss: the residual displacement per spin eigenstate with its bound Σ_m|α_m|²(2n̄_m + 1) at the step's starting occupations (`GateLocalStep.residual_bound`), the motional purity deficit, the frozen excitation bound and the dropped crosstalk; Section 9.8 holds a JOINT_EXACT comparison to that bound. On the isometry route a superposition input's boundary population is bounded by Π_i d_i times the reported maximum.
- Three declared relaxations keyed to ε_map add reported terms to `GateLocalReport.discrepancy_bound`: the tail rule drops the lightest motional branches up to a total weight w ≤ `tomography_dropped_weight_max` (ε_map/4 unless set), changing the channel by at most 2w in diamond norm (`branch_error_bound`); a unitary step with resolved modes integrates at atol = 10⁻⁵ε_map, rtol = 10⁻³ε_map when the caller kept the defaults (`tomography_tolerance_keyed`), the term being d times the trace-norm change of the dominant branch's Choi matrix under a ten-times-tighter integration (`tolerance_change`); and the step cap keeps the margin for interior elements exact to 10⁻⁵ε_map (`margin_element_tol`, Section 5.5). Dissipative and internal-state-only steps are never loosened, and `tomography_dropped_weight_max=0`, `tomography_tolerance_keyed=False` with the fixture margin is the reference that JOINT_EXACT and Section 9 keep.
- Both levels share preparation, readout, noise sampling and result formats, and `Diagnostics.level` records which ran. Replaying per-gate channels for large circuits is not a core level: the application of Section 14 builds it from the channel summaries of Section 6.8.

### 5.5 Convergence and truncation policy

- **Caps.** A resolved mode's cap is the highest Fock index its thermal state, displaced by the pulse's closed-form coherent excursion max_t Σ_i|α_im(t)|, populates above the boundary threshold, plus the margin of Section 5.1.1, clamped to `mode_dimension_max` (64) with a `TruncationWarning` naming the mode and both dimensions.
- **Boundary monitor.** After every pulse the population in each mode's top two Fock levels (the top shell for ENR) is recorded (`Diagnostics.boundary_population`). Above `boundary_population_max` (10⁻⁶ of the population the pulse moves, overridable for hot modes), or with the margin above the populated range below the required one, the run repeats with the cap raised up to the retry budget, and reports the margin reached and the growth (`margin_reached`, `cap_growth`, a note per retry). One threshold serves both checks, scaled to the mixture: a Fock branch of weight w is measured against min(`boundary_population_max`/w, 0.5). A boundary population still above it after the retries is a `TruncationWarning` (a `UserWarning`), so `warnings.simplefilter("error", TruncationWarning)` makes a silent run a test that the caps held.
- **Margins.** JOINT_EXACT keeps the fixture of Section 5.1.1. A GATE_LOCAL step derives its margin at `margin_element_tol` (10⁻⁵ε_map unless set): the smallest at which the interior elements over the populated range match the analytic ones to that tolerance (measured, since the error at fixed margin grows with the range) and one displacement from the top populated level leaks less than a tenth of the boundary threshold past the cap, never more than the fixture; the step reports the measured element error (`GateLocalStep.element_error`).
- **Tolerances.** `convergence_check` (off by default; it triples the cost) repeats the evolution at atol/10 and rtol/10 and reports the change in register populations as `Diagnostics.convergence`; the test suite's convergence harness runs the three arms of Section 9.9 (tolerances ÷10, ×10, caps +2).
- **Sampled coefficients.** Envelopes are sampled at 20 times their bandwidth and cubic-spline interpolated. A noise band is synthesized per dynamical sample by the spectral method on a fixed grid with Δt ≤ 1/(10ω_max), stored with the run and interpolated with `order=1`, never on the solver's adaptive steps; a test compares its periodogram with the configured spectrum. Quasi-static drifts are Ornstein-Uhlenbeck chains over the shot clock (Section 6.1).

### 5.6 Units and constants

Internal units are SI with angular frequencies in rad/s and times in seconds; the Hamiltonian is divided by ħ. Public interfaces take frequencies in Hz and apply the 2π once, at the boundary, since factor-2π errors are the commonest bug in this domain; names carry the unit (`_hz`, `_rad_s`, `_per_s`). Rates (heating in quanta/s, 1/T₂, scattering and collapse-operator rates such as `CollapseOp.rate_hz`) are ordinary rates in s⁻¹ and never take the 2π **[corrected]**. Constants come from CODATA with the source in `units.py`; `Species.mass_u` is the ion mass, the cited atomic mass minus one electron.

### 5.7 The governing equations in one place

Everything the simulator integrates is one master equation on the joint space of Section 5.1,

ρ̇ = −i[H(t), ρ] + Σ_k 𝒟[L_k]ρ,  𝒟[L]ρ = LρL† − ½{L†L, ρ},  H(t) = H_phys(t)/ħ in rad/s,  L_k = √γ_k A_k in s^{−1/2},

run as `sesolve` on the branches of the initial mixture, `mcsolve` with keyed trajectories or `mesolve` on ρ (Section 5.3). The terms below carry their ħ and point to the subsections that specify and tag them; this list is an index, not a new claim. Every term is switchable, and every switch is recorded in `Result.diagnostics.approximations`.

**Hamiltonian, H(t) = H_mot + H_int + Σ_i H_drive,i(t) + Σ_i H_Stark,i(t) + H_anh.**

- H_mot = Σ_m ħω_m a_m†a_m over the resolved (and ENR) modes, zero-point energy dropped: the modes of Section 4.1.3 at their calibrated frequencies (Section 4.1.5) plus the sample's offsets (Section 6.2).
- H_int = Σ_i (ħΔ_i/2)σ_z^i (Σ_p ħΔ_{i,p}|p⟩⟨p| when d > 2): each ion's true transition minus the frame frequency, from the field sample through Section 4.5.1 (Section 6.3), the ac Zeeman shift (Section 4.3.3) and the believed-against-true qubit frequency (Section 7.3).
- H_drive,i(t) = (ħ/2)Σ_tones Ω_{i,tone}(t)e^{−i(μ_tone t − φ_tone(t))}σ₊^i ⊗ Π_m D_m(iη_{i,m}) + h.c. (Sections 4.3.1, 5.2), with C₀ inside every η (Section 4.1.1) and Ω from intensities and dipole elements (Sections 4.3.2, 4.5) through the hardware chain (Section 7.10). With Ω → ε_ijΩ on a neighbour it is crosstalk (Section 6.6); times J₀(β), or e^{iβcos(Ω_rf t + δ)} for an rf-locked drive, excess micromotion (Section 4.3.6); Ω_i → Ω_i(1 + (Ω″/2Ω)x̂_i²) beam curvature (Section 6.2); a sampled φ(t) or Ω(t) laser phase or intensity noise (Sections 6.3, 6.4); a light-shift drive replaces σ₊ by a level-weighted projector (Section 4.4.4); for d > 2 σ₊ becomes the pair operators |p⟩⟨q| (Section 4.5).
- H_Stark,i(t) = (ħ/2)δ_St,i(t)σ_z^i, following the instantaneous intensity (Section 4.3.2).
- H_anh: the cubic and quartic mode couplings of Section 4.1.4 (`Trap.anharmonic_terms`), opt-in; the resonance checker and phase estimate run whenever the trap carries them. The explicit rf Hamiltonian (Section 4.1.1) is not part of the builder.

**Collapse operators.** Heating √(Γ(N̄ + 1)) a_m and √(ΓN̄) a_m† (Sections 4.1.5, 6.2); motional dephasing √(2/τ_m) a_m†a_m (Section 6.2); qubit dephasing √(γ_φ/2)σ_z^i, under which the coherence decays at γ_φ = 1/T₂ for the white part, the slow part being a sampled Δ_i (Section 6.3); photon scattering √Γ_{p→q}|q⟩⟨p| ⊗ R and the elastic ½√Γ_el σ_z (Sections 4.5.5, 6.5), R the recoil operator of Section 4.2.8; laser intensity noise √D H_drive(t) (Section 6.4); shelf decay √(1/τ_D)|S⟩⟨D| and photon counting during readout (Sections 8.1, 8.2).

**Stochastic elements**, per dynamical sample from keyed streams of one root seed (Sections 3.4, 6.1): mode- and qubit-frequency offsets with their inter-ion correlation, Rabi scale factors, beam and beat-note phases, stray-field offsets (each ion's position and every drive's micromotion index) and the laser phase and intensity time series.

**Initial state** (Section 4.2): ρ(0) = ⊗_i ρ_int,i ⊗ ⊗_m ρ_th,m(n̄_m), the pumped internal states with their preparation error and each mode's cooled state, evolved as weighted branches (Section 5.3).

**Measurement** (Sections 8.2 to 8.5): the joint internal outcome is sampled projectively from the final state, so entangled correlations survive; each ion's photon record is generated conditioned on it, coupled to its neighbours' by crosstalk (Section 8.5), and discriminated into a bit. The product POVM of Section 8.4 is the fast path that replaces the record and discriminator layers, so the readout error is applied once, and its per-ion table is indexed by the sampled internal level. It is exact only at zero readout crosstalk; with crosstalk the fast path carries the register-wide confusion (factored by neighbour range, dense to twelve ions), and `Readout(mode="full")` is the reference **[corrected]**.

## 6. Noise and error models

### 6.1 Principles

Every error channel is a physical process whose rate or spectrum comes from device parameters, entering as (a) a Hamiltonian term (crosstalk light, Stark shifts, beam curvature, coherent over-rotation); (b) a Lindblad operator (heating, fast dephasing, photon scattering, shelf decay); (c) a quasi-static parameter drawn per dynamical sample at the shot clock (field, mode-frequency, Rabi-frequency and beam-phase drift, each an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck chain over the shot times); (d) a sampled time series in a drive coefficient (laser phase and intensity noise, the frequency trajectories of Sections 6.2 and 6.3, mains harmonics with a per-shot trigger phase); or (e) discrete events (Section 6.7). The correlation time decides: white noise is (b), noise slow against a shot is (c), and the band between, 1 Hz to 10 kHz, where magnetic dephasing and every decoupling sequence live, is (d).

A spectrum is stored two-sided in angular frequency (e^{−iωt} kernel) with a declared white level, and the declaration, never the spectrum's shape, fixes the route: the white level is the Lindblad rate and the tabulated band is synthesized per sample, x(t) = Σ_k √(S(ω_k)Δω_k/π)[a_k cos ω_k t + b_k sin ω_k t]; a grid that cannot resolve the band is refused. Circuit-level channels are outputs (Section 6.8), never integrated. Every rate carries its apparatus provenance, and a budget that mixes apparatus says so in `Diagnostics.approximations` **[extracted]**.

### 6.2 Motional channels

**Heating** uses the operators of Section 4.1.5. The gate-level forms the simulated gate reproduces are ε_h = ṅt_g/(2K) for a K-loop gate (Ballance 2016; Trout 2018) and ΔF = Γ_h t_g/2 for a single loop (Kirchmair 2009) **[verified]**; the first holds only for ε_h ≪ 0.1, so K is carried per pulse solution and the form is refused outside that range **[corrected]**.

**Motional dephasing.** White frequency noise is L = a†a√(2/τ), under which the coherence of |n⟩ + |n′⟩ decays at (n − n′)²/τ, with 2/τ = ω_m²S_V,white for the rf-derived families **[derived]**. The gate error is ε_d = α_K t_g/τ with α_K = (8K + 3)/(16K²), 0.6875 at K = 1; Ballance's printed 0.686 is the value at t_g/τ = 10⁻³, not the coefficient **[corrected]**. Slow noise H_e = ħε(t)a†a costs I = (2n̄ + 1)|α_t|² Var(S_α) + (δθ²/4) Var(S_α²), α_t = ∫ε(t)γ(t)dt, δθ = 2∫ε(t)|γ(t)|²dt (Hughes 2025), the λ² of the source being variances over the input state: Var(S_α²) = 2N(N − 1) on |0…0⟩, not ⟨S_α⁴⟩ **[corrected]**.

**Mode-frequency drift** has a common-mode part from rf amplitude (ω_sec ∝ V_rf), one draw scaling every rf-derived family, and a differential dc part, drawn as independent per-mode offsets because the device carries no ∂ω_m/∂V per electrode; independent offsets average closure errors as 1/√M instead of adding, so a multi-mode FM or AM-FM robustness result is optimistic by up to √M **[contested]**; a run whose noise model has a non-quiet `mode_drift_differential` says so in its approximations. The rf phase-noise spectrum S_φ is recorded but not applied: the Ω_rf ± ω_t sideband heating of Section 4.1.5 needs the stray field's rf gradient, which the trap record lacks, and the parametric term at 2ω_sec is not implemented (Section 12). A run whose noise model sets `rf_phase_noise`, or an `rf_amplitude_noise` with density at twice a transverse mode's frequency, says in its approximations what it leaves out (`NoiseModel.approximations`).

**Spectator modes** are in the Hamiltonian at JOINT_EXACT and reproduce the closed forms of Section 4.4.7; frozen and dropped modes are reported (Section 5.2).

**Beam-curvature dephasing** (Cetina 2022) **[verified]**. An ion moving along the chain through a focused beam samples its curvature, the opt-in builder term Ω(x̂) = Ω₀(1 + (Ω″/2Ω₀)x̂²) (Ω″/Ω = −(2/w²)(1 − 2x²/w²) for a Gaussian beam), which couples the drive to the axial a†a. On a thermal state the carrier contrast is C = Π_m(1 + θ_m²Ω²t²)^{−1/2} with phase lag φ = Σ_m arctan(θ_mΩt), θ_m = −b_m²ξ_m²(Ω″/Ω)n̄_m; the source's Eq. 2 gives the lag the wrong sign **[corrected]**. It is not a Debye-Waller effect: it involves the modes perpendicular to the beam, with an exponent about 120 times smaller than a k-vector reading and an algebraic envelope, and neither mechanism stands in for the other **[corrected]**.

### 6.3 Qubit dephasing

A hyperfine level shifts by g_Fμ_Bm_F B/h, 1.4 MHz/G per unit m_F at g_F = 1, so a Δm_F = ±1 transition from a clock state moves 1.4 MHz/G and an m_J = ±½ Zeeman qubit 2.8 MHz/G; a clock qubit moves as δν = c₂δB² with c₂ = ½ d²ν/dB² (Harty's 2.4 mHz/mG² for 43Ca+ is d²ν/dB²; Section 13) **[corrected]**, the sensitivities coming from Section 4.5.1. The white level of S_B is L = √(γ_φ/2)σ_z with γ_φ = 2π²(dν/dB)²S_B,white, so γ_φ = 1/T₂ for the white part, and a measured T₂ is split into its Gaussian and exponential parts before use **[derived]**. The slow part is a per-sample offset correlated over the length ξ_c (gate error in Section 4.4.7), and the band between is a sampled Δ_i(t) plus mains harmonics with a per-shot trigger phase, from which Ramsey revivals and the (XY)^N T₂ follow through Section 6.9 **[corrected]**. Laser phase noise matters for phase-sensitive gates (Section 4.4.2): each beam carries its own realization of the declared spectrum, so a Raman beat note sees φ₂ − φ₁ at twice one beam's variance and a co-propagating pair none, checked against Baldwin's ε = [∫S_φF dω](4g²/Δ²)² **[extracted]**.

### 6.4 Amplitude and control errors

Slow Rabi-frequency variation, pointing drift included, is a per-sample scale factor. White intensity noise is L = √D H_drive(t) with carrier-contrast rate Γ_I = DΩ²/2, D the white fractional-Rabi density (a quarter of the intensity's for a single-photon drive) **[derived]**. For noise common to the register the MS error is ε_I ≈ NΓ_I t_g η²[(n̄ + ½) + 3(N − 1)/(8K)], and independent per-beam noise halves the second term; Bermudez 2017 prints it without the factor N and with (N − 1)/4 **[corrected]**. Static pulse-area and detuning errors are Mount's R = exp[−(i/2)θ(1 + ε)(σ_φ + δZ)], the primitive of Section 4.3.5 **[verified]**. Over-rotation emerges from miscalibrated areas; its Pauli twirl p_xx = sin²α (Trout 2018) is a two-qubit reporting option **[verified]**.

### 6.5 Photon scattering and leakage

Scattering rates come from the amplitudes of Section 4.5.5, per π pulse from Section 4.3.2 **[verified]**. Raman scattering flips the spin within and out of the qubit pair (leakage, d > 2, read out by the leaked manifold's polarity); Rayleigh scattering is a recoil kick (Section 4.2.8), with the differential-Rayleigh dephasing of Section 4.5.5 when the qubit states' elastic amplitudes differ; D-level scattering leaks to a shelf. The operators run over emitted polarization and six emission directions about B, exact in the first two moments, each carrying the absorbed beam's kick; out-of-manifold rates go to a sink at d > 2 and are reported as estimates at d = 2 **[background]**. The split ε_S = P_Raman, ε_D = f P_total uses an f derived from the tabulated D-level branchings, never typed in **[derived]**.

### 6.6 Addressing crosstalk

ε_ij = Ω_j/Ω_i is a Rabi (amplitude) ratio from beam i onto ion j, never an intensity ratio **[corrected]**. Leaked drives add as vectors in the spin plane, φ_beam a quasi-static sample, and enter the Hamiltonian exactly **[verified]**. To leading order an MS gate becomes XX(θ)·exp[−iΣ_{j≠1,2}(θ_{1j}X^{(1)} + θ_{2j}X^{(2)})σ_{φ_j}^{(j)}], with Bell fidelity cos²θ₁₃ cos²θ₂₃ and spectator excitation [1 − cos 2θ₁₃ cos 2θ₂₃]/2; the source's printed forms halve the angles, so the small-angle infidelity coefficient is θ², not θ²/4 **[corrected]**. The scheduler's `crosstalk_suppression` splits each MS into two halves with Y(π) on both targets (`"local"`) or Z(π) on every spectator (`"neighbour"`) between them, exact to first order (Fang 2022) **[verified]**. Landsman's bound ½‖ℰ‖◇ ≤ Σ|Θ_rs| for two parallel pairs is vacuous above 1, and MS gates are serialized (Section 7.3) **[corrected]**.

### 6.7 Idle time, memory and preparation

Idle intervals evolve under H₀ with the motional and qubit channels active; identity gates in benchmarking are delays (Harty 2014) **[verified]**. Preparation error comes from Section 4.2.6 and joins SPAM, and every SPAM number states its definition, since RB-fit and one-sided figures differ within the same papers **[corrected]**.

**Collisions** **[background]**: Langevin collisions at Γ_L = n_bg k_L per ion with k_L = (e/(2ε₀))√(α_p/μ_r), 4.8 × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹ for H₂ at 10⁻¹¹ torr on a heavy ion **[recomputed here]**, form a Poisson process per shot with three outcomes: a heating kick of mean energy k_BT m_gas/m_ion on the softest mode (the coefficient a device multiplier), a reorder, or a loss or dark ion (for 171Yb+, the F₇/₂ level). Results carry per-shot heralds and a discarded-shot count; a kick after preparation or a reorder discards the shot rather than re-deriving the sample or the mode structure, as `Diagnostics.approximations` states. The melt itself is out of scope (Section 1.3).

### 6.8 Reporting to circuit-level clients

A gate's simulated channel (Section 5.4) is reported as its average gate infidelity d/(d + 1)(1 − F_e), its Pauli twirl, and a depolarizing rate ε with Λ_ε(ρ) = (1 − ε)ρ + ε/(4ⁿ − 1)Σ_{P≠I}PρP, in which ε is the entanglement infidelity (Chen 2023) **[verified]**. Trout 2018's p/3 and p/15 Kraus weights are the same channel, and Qiskit's λ = ε4ⁿ/(4ⁿ − 1) is a conversion, never the same number (Chen's Forte medians 2.0 × 10⁻⁴ and 46.4 × 10⁻⁴ enter Qiskit as 2.67 × 10⁻⁴ and 49.5 × 10⁻⁴) **[verified]**. `error_model()` exports per native gate kind the channel infidelity, SPAM, durations and noise rates in IonQ's, Quantinuum's and the QDK estimator's vocabularies. Bermudez 2017's three-variant mapping onto Pauli channels is not provided.

### 6.9 Dynamical decoupling and the filter-function description of dephasing and amplitude noise

This layer scores a control sequence's susceptibility to a spectrum analytically (Green 2013; Kabytayev 2014; Biercuk 2009). Its normalizations travel together.

**Toggling frame.** For H = H_c + β(t)·σ with no ½ on σ, the toggling-frame noise is β·R·σ with R_ij(t) = ½Tr[U_c†σ_iU_cσ_j], the adjoint of U_c†, not of U_c **[verified]**. Piecewise control gives R(t) = Σ_l G^{(l)}(t)R^{P_l}(t − t_{l−1})Λ^{(l−1)}, with G^{(l)} the segment boxcar and Λ^{(l−1)} the adjoint of the propagator accumulated before segment l, right-multiplying **[verified]**. With R_ij(ω) = −iω∫₀^τ R_ij(t)e^{iωt}dt, the −iω explicit, 1 − F_av ≃ (1/2π)∫(dω/ω²)Σ_{ijk}S_ij R_jk R*_ik (Green Eq. 23), where F_av = ¼⟨|Tr Ũ(τ)|²⟩ is the F_K of Section 4.3.5, valid while ξ² = (τ²/π)Σ_i∫₀^{ω_ci}S_i dω ≪ 1 **[verified]**. The 1/ω² and the −iω are a matched pair, a one-sided integral carries 1/π, and no 1/ω⁴ weight appears. Green's Eq. 40 lacks a ½ on its Tr(σ_{φ_l}σ_zσ_j) term, which doubles both transverse components **[corrected]**.

**Dephasing variable.** H_deph = b(t)σ_z with no ½, so b is half the splitting fluctuation, and a qubit-frequency spectrum S_δ of δσ_z/2 converts as S_b = S_δ/4. Then χ(τ) = (2/π)∫₀^∞(dω/ω²)S_b(ω)F(ωτ), W = e^{−χ} and 1 − F_av = χ/2 at weak noise; reading S as the splitting PSD makes χ four times too large **[corrected]**. Green's Eqs. 35a and 35b as printed give half this, so the linear form is used for ξ² ≪ 1 and the exponential only to extrapolate **[corrected]**.

**Pulse trains.** With dephasing gated off during the n pulses, F(ωτ) = |1 + (−1)^{n+1}e^{iωτ} + 2cos(ωτ_π/2)Σ_j(−1)^j e^{iδ_jωτ}|² (Biercuk Eq. 2) at both parities; Green's Eq. 45 is its even-n twin and gives no suppression at odd n **[corrected]**. CPMG places δ_j = (2j − 1)/2n and UDD δ_j = sin²[πj/(2n + 2)], and clock-grid rounding must keep A₁ = Σ_j(−1)^jδ_j = ∓½. In the bang-bang limit F ∝ (ωτ)^{2(α+1)} with α = 2 for even-n Carr-Purcell and α = n for UDD, F → (ωτ)^{2n+2}/(16^n(n!)²) **[verified]**. Finite pulses collapse UDD to low-frequency order 4 at odd n, with the floor (ωτ_π)⁴/16, and 6 at even n, orders verified in arbitrary precision because double precision cancels to noise **[recomputed here]**. The noise band must avoid ωτ_π = π. Under S_b ∝ ω⁻⁴, χ converges only from even n ≥ 2, and the infrared cutoff, which no source supplies, is the caller's ω_min, else the band's lowest frequency, the experiment duration or 2π/(1000τ), with d ln χ/d ln ω_min reported (Section 12).

**Amplitude noise.** The control vector ρ_a = ½Σ_l G^{(l)}ρ̂(φ_l)Λ^{(l−1)} is constant per segment, so F_a(ω) = ¼{|Σ_l A_lρ̃_l|² + |Σ_l B_lρ̃_l|²}, with A_l and B_l the cosine and sine differences across segment l and ρ̃_l = ρ̂(φ_l)Λ^{(l−1)}, is pulse-shape independent at fixed total time; the ¼ is load-bearing **[verified]**. dc cancellation is the closed polygon Σ_lθ_lρ̃_l = 0; a primitive pulse rises as ω² and every amplitude-correcting composite as ω⁴ **[recomputed here]**. The printed (1/16)(ωτ_CP)⁴ is a bound, not an equality, so the noise frequency above which SK1 and BB1 hurt is 0.070Ω and 0.132Ω at θ = π, not the bound's 0.025Ω **[corrected]**.

**dc floor.** At ω/Ω ≪ 1 the first-order filter function underestimates the error. Under frozen Gaussian noise 1 − F ≈ ĉ_{m+1}(2m + 1)!!⟨r²⟩^{m+1}, with r = β_a/Ω for amplitude and 2β_d/Ω for detuning and m the pulse's order for that channel (0 for a channel it does not correct) **[corrected]**. The source's ĉ are in Ω = 1 units, so pairing its ĉ₂ = 22.83 with a moment in (rad/s)⁴ errs by Ω^{2(m+1)}, and its BB1 and CORPSE floors are not reproduced: the module carries coefficients fitted from the exact frozen-noise overlap **[corrected]**. A sequence without a ĉ uses the exact Gauss-Hermite frozen-noise average. The reported infidelity is max[(1 − F)_FF, (1 − F)_dc] **[verified]**.

**Attachment.** Route (c) of Section 6.1 is the dc-floor limit and route (d) the filter-function regime; a Monte Carlo over S_b through the Section 4.3.1 Hamiltonian closes the loop, and a decoupling T₂ is where χ(τ) = 1 on the configured spectrum. Multi-axis sequences (XY4, XY8, KDD, CDD, (XY)^N) break the scalar (−1)^l bookkeeping of every published form, so the module composes per-pulse R^P blocks with Λ and validates them against the single-axis forms **[background]**. The first-order dynamically corrected gate is not implementable from the sources.

## 7. Control stack: native gates, compilation, scheduling and calibration

### 7.1 Native gate set

The native set maps one-to-one onto pulses (matrices in Section 7.6) **[verified]**: GPi(φ) and GPi2(φ), carrier π and π/2 pulses at phase φ in the qubit's frame; RZ(θ), a frame shift with no pulse; MS(φ₀, φ₁, θ), a bichromatic two-ion pulse with θ ∈ [0, π/2]; and ZZ(θ), a light-shift pulse (Section 4.4.4) or a wrapped MS (Section 7.6). Arbitrary-area carrier pulses stay inside the pulse layer, so the exported set, and the IonQ round trip, is GPi, GPi2, MS and ZZ. Parameters are radians inside and turns at the IonQ boundary.

### 7.2 Compiler

The IR is a list of operations (name, qubits, params): the native set, the standard gates (X, Y, Z, H, S, S†, T, T†, RX, RY, RZ, CNOT, CZ, SWAP, CP, U3, RXX, RZZ) and measure, reset and recool. Compilation is the one place ideal matrices appear, and every block is verified against its target up to a global phase.

1. A single-qubit gate becomes one virtual RZ, one GPi or GPi2, or RZ(α)·GPi2(0)·RZ(β)·GPi2(0)·RZ(γ), which is complete because GPi2(0)·RZ(β)·GPi2(0) = RY(−β)·RX(π) up to a phase **[recomputed here]**.
2. Every RZ is absorbed into the later phases in time order (φ → φ − θ, Section 7.6), leaving a final frame that the measurement discards.
3. CNOT and CP use the templates of Section 7.7 (CZ = CP(π), SWAP = three CNOTs). An arbitrary SU(4) uses the KAK decomposition in the magic basis with (a, b, c) in the Weyl chamber π/4 ≥ a ≥ b ≥ |c|, three entangling gates in general (Kraus and Cirac 2001) **[background]**.
4. Measure, reset and recool are IR and schedule operations, but only the terminal measurement executes: any of them before a later gate is refused (Section 8.5), and the engine integrates to the last pulse, never through the detection window.

The compiler is correct, not optimal (Section 1.3).

### 7.3 Scheduler

Single-qubit gates on distinct ions overlap only when the device's hardware chain declares parallel addressing, and so do the pulses one gate plays on several ions (the ZZ wrappers and echoes, the crosstalk echoes), each followed by the dead time; entangling gates advance the crystal clock and never overlap. Idle gaps evolve with the noise (Section 6.7). A beam within 2 × 10⁻³ of a tabulated E1 wavelength is resonant light and never a gate drive.

Pulse parameters come only from the calibration table (Section 7.5), never from the device's true values: derived values enter only as `seed` entries, and an `uncalibrated` entry refuses to schedule rather than fall back **[corrected]**. The engine plays each request through the device, Ω_phys = Ω_req Ω_derived/Ω_table, with the light shift and crosstalk likewise, for Raman and optical drives; microwave, light-shift and gradient drives are played as requested. The solvers read the table's mode frequencies but the device's Lamb-Dicke parameters **[contested]**.

### 7.4 Pulse representation

A `Pulse` holds its ions, drive kind, duration, wavevector, metadata and tones, each with δ(t), φ(t) and Ω(t) (ħΩ/2 convention, Section 4.3.1) given as a callable, a sampled array (cubic spline) or a constant; the coefficient they form is Section 5.2's. Entangling pulses carry the AM, FM, PM and AM-FM waveforms of Section 4.4.3, closing every mode, with ∂α/∂δ = 0 robustness optional; single-qubit pulses are square, their edges from Section 7.10.

### 7.5 Calibration emulation

`calibrate(machine, method=...)` builds the `CalibrationTable`. `"closed_form"`, the default, is the surrogate: derived values as `seed` entries and the closed-form waveforms of Section 4.4.3 corrected by exact spot checks, each playing the scheduler's own schedule of the gate under the machine's `Physics` (Stark compensation, crosstalk echo, hardware chain, builder options), one pair per pair class, with the beat note 0.35 of the smallest mode gap above the highest coupled mode, never at a mode midpoint, where the solution flips **[recomputed here]**. `"experiments"` runs the simulated experiments, an opt-in cross-check of the surrogate affordable up to two ions (Section 11.1).

The experiments run in a fixed dependency order: crystal image, field, micromotion, modes, Rabi, Stark and qubit frequency, crosstalk, entangling scans, detection, heating. A fit whose upstream entry is `uncalibrated` refuses, and its own entries become `uncalibrated` **[corrected]**. Populations are shots declared through the readout's (ε_B, ε_D), fitted by weighted least squares; a failed or edge fit marks its entry `uncalibrated`, and fitted entries agree with the derived values within two reported standard errors **[recomputed here]**.

1. **Carrier Rabi**: flopping over ten π times, with the Debye-Waller reduction of every coupled mode and n̄ from the thermometry of Section 4.2.7.
2. **Mode frequencies**: coarse, then fine sideband scans fitted with P = [Ω²/(Ω² + δ²)] sin²((t/2)√(Ω² + δ²)) (Section 13), never the half-Rabi form of Section 7.9, which returns Ω/2 **[corrected]**; the pulse's own light shift Ω²/(2ω_m) is removed by fitting at two amplitudes and extrapolating to zero **[recomputed here]**.
3. **Phase frames and qubit frequency**: a Ramsey experiment per ion, and MS phase scans.
4. **Entangling gate**: the waveform re-solved at the calibrated modes, the closure offset at the leakage minimum, the amplitude scanned ±30%, and χ = π/4 from populations and parity **[corrected]**.
5. **Detection**: 10⁴ bright and dark records for threshold and window, and the long-record mean-count fit of Section 8.2.
6. **Heating**: sideband asymmetry against delay.
7. **Stark shift** per beam: a Ramsey with one beam on at both probe signs, δ_St = (f₋ − f₊)/2 **[recomputed here]**; scheduled spin-flip tones (carrier pulses, MS legs) are detuned by the believed shift and phase-referenced to the pulse start; a σ_z force keeps its calibrated beat note, its shift commuting with the force and absorbed by the frame.
8. **Crosstalk**: the Rabi rate and axis on every neighbour.
9. **Field**: the clock-point or Zeeman scan, run before every fit, since each reads the Zeeman sensitivities.

Micromotion compensation (rf-photon correlation, sideband ratio, two-direction Doppler nulling; Berkeland 1998) stores the shims and the residual β, and a run programs the calibrated shims **[corrected]**. Entries carry their fit time, uncertainty and noise sample, and drifts are trajectories (Sections 6.2, 6.3), so the error grows with elapsed time; an optional servo high-passes a drift, leaving variance σ²/(1 + 2πf_sτ). Tables are cached by device hash (Section 3.3), seed and settings, and invalidated, never silently regenerated.

### 7.6 Native gates, exactly

**Research notation** (Debnath 2016; Maslov 2017) **[verified]**: R_φ(θ) = exp[−i(θ/2)(cos φ σ_x + sin φ σ_y)] = [[cos(θ/2), −i sin(θ/2)e^{−iφ}], [−i sin(θ/2)e^{iφ}, cos(θ/2)]] and XX(χ) = exp(−iχσ_x⊗σ_x), maximally entangling at χ = ±π/4 and carrying χ with no ½ (Section 13).

**IonQ notation**, parameters in turns **[verified]**: GPi(φ) = [[0, e^{−2πiφ}], [e^{2πiφ}, 0]]; GPi2(φ) = (1/√2)[[1, −ie^{−2πiφ}], [−ie^{2πiφ}, 1]], so φ = 0.5 is R_x(−π/2), not the R_x(+π/2) of the documentation's prose **[corrected]**; MS(φ₀, φ₁, θ) = exp[−iπθ GPi(φ₀)⊗GPi(φ₁)], θ ∈ [0, 0.25], equal at θ = 0.25 to (1/√2)[[1, 0, 0, −ie^{−2πi(φ₀+φ₁)}], [0, 1, −ie^{−2πi(φ₀−φ₁)}, 0], [0, −ie^{2πi(φ₀−φ₁)}, 1, 0], [−ie^{2πi(φ₀+φ₁)}, 0, 0, 1]]; ZZ(θ) = exp(−iπθ Z⊗Z); VirtualZ(θ) = RZ(2πθ). XX(χ) = MS(0, 0, θ) with χ = πθ.

**Virtual Z.** Read in time order, RZ(θ) shifts every later GPi, GPi2 and MS phase by φ → φ − θ; the documentation's +θ holds in matrix order **[corrected]**. The hardware dead time is still charged, and the frame folds into the phases of later MS gates **[corrected]**.

**Sign and ZZ.** A pulse applies exp(+iχσσ) with χ signed by the detuning side, so a positive χ plays XX with a π on the second ion's tones, and XX(−π/4) exports as MS(0, π, π/2) in radians **[verified]**. In radians, ZZ(θ) = exp(−i(θ/2)Z⊗Z) = [GPi2(π/2)⊗GPi2(π/2)]·XX(θ/2)·[GPi2(3π/2)⊗GPi2(3π/2)] is played as two GPi2 wrappers around an MS waveform, an inferred construction (Chen 2023; Quantinuum) **[background]**.

**JSON** (IonQ OpenAPI v0.4 and its client SDKs) **[verified]**: `gpi` and `gpi2` take `target` and `phase` (turns); `ms` takes `targets`, `phases` and `angle` (turns, default 0.25); `zz` takes `targets` and `angle`; the job body is `{"type": "ionq.circuit.v1", "backend", "shots", "input": {"gateset", "qubits", "circuit"}}`, every key checked. The OpenQASM 2 importer takes qelib1.inc and inlined gate declarations, so the client SDKs' `gpi`, `gpi2`, `ms` and `zz` definitions import, and refuses `if` and `opaque`.

### 7.7 Compilation templates, verified

Matrix products read right to left and circuits left to right; Maslov's "·" reverses circuit order.

- **CNOT** (Maslov 2017 Fig. 1), in time order: RY(vπ/2)_c, XX(sπ/4), RX(−sπ/2)_c RX(−vsπ/2)_t, RY(−vπ/2)_c is e^{iπvs/4}CNOT for all four signs, the source writing (−1)^{−vs/4} **[corrected]**. s is a free convention because the scheduler realizes either sign from the waveform (Section 7.6), and no published sign table is used **[corrected]**. Trout's Fig. 8 template is not a CNOT and serves as a negative control **[corrected]**.
- **CP(θ)** = [RZ(θ/2)⊗RZ(θ/2)]·ZZ(−θ/2), exact **[recomputed here]**. Debnath's Fig. 2b template carries an extra R_z((sgn(θ)π − θ)/2) on each qubit (overlap 0.854 at θ = π/2), and its Methods' χ = π/4 at θ = π/2 contradicts the caption's π/8 **[corrected]**.
- **Verified but unused**: controlled X^α from one XX and four pulses **[corrected]**; any single-qubit U in two pulses, R(−π, −c − π/2)·R(2b + π, a − c − π/2), the right factor first **[corrected]**; a physical RZ as R(π, x)·R(π, x − θ/2) **[verified]**.
- **Cost model** (Maslov): durations |θ|τ_1q/π and τ_2q, errors ∝ |sin θ|ε and |sin 2χ|E **[verified]**.

### 7.8 Pulse-shaping practice

The solvers reproduce the published families **[verified]**: segmented AM (Debnath 2016; Egan 2021, where the centre ion of an odd chain couples only to symmetric modes), FM on a subset of modes (Landsman 2019), AM-FM with K-th-order stabilization (Blümel 2021), and AM closing every transverse mode of 36 ions (Chen 2023). SK1's order claims hold for square pulses; a shaped SK1 keeps its dc amplitude cancellation but not its detuning behaviour (Section 6.9).

### 7.9 Calibration recipes and benchmark protocols

- **Carrier Rabi**: the thermal Debye-Waller envelope (Section 4.2.7), or for focused beams Cetina's algebraic contrast (Section 6.2) **[corrected]**.
- **Sidebands**: Blümel's P = [Ω_p²/(Ω_p² + δ²/4)] sin²(t√(Ω_p² + δ²/4)) is written for half the usual Ω and is doubled on ingest **[verified]**; the extracted η feeds the solvers.
- **Entangling gate** (Wright 2019): scan the amplitude until P₀₀ = P₁₁, then the parity under an analysis π/2 pulse. (P₀₀ + P₁₁ + C)/2 is exactly the best-phase Bell fidelity, an upper bound on the fixed-phase one **[corrected]**. Partial angles use Debnath's interference sequence, P(|1⟩) = ½(1 − sin θ) **[verified]**.

**Randomized benchmarking.** Clifford RB closes m Cliffords by the inverse of their product, not of each Clifford **[corrected]**, fits F(m) = Ap^m + B (B fixed at 1/2ⁿ when degenerate) and reports r = (1 − p)(2ⁿ − 1)/2ⁿ per Clifford beside the depolarizing entanglement infidelity (4ⁿ − 1)(1 − p)/4ⁿ (Section 13) **[corrected]**. C₁ is the closure of {H, S} (24 elements, 0.8333 pulses per Clifford); C₂ (11520) is sampled from the double cosets of C₁⊗C₁ by {1, CNOT, iSWAP, SWAP}, of sizes 576, 5184, 5184, 576 (Barends 2014) **[recomputed here]**. Simultaneous RB fits each qubit's marginal decay, r_q = (1 − p_q)/2 (Gambetta 2012), the joint decay being an error per layer, never an n-qubit rate **[corrected]**. The Knill-style variant (`variant="knill"`) interleaves random Paulis and Clifford π/2 pulses and fits Bp^L + ½ **[verified]**. Direct RB is not implemented; Chen 2023's extraction matrix is singular as printed and needs error rates, not decay bases **[corrected]**.

**GHZ.** (P₀ + P₁ + C)/2, C the contrast of the parity C cos(Nφ + φ₀) after GPi2(φ) on every qubit, equals max_θ⟨GHZ_θ|ρ|GHZ_θ⟩, an upper bound on the fixed-phase fidelity; both exact fidelities are reported beside it **[corrected]**.

**Quantum volume.** Haar-random SU(4) layers, heavy outputs above the ideal median, and a pass at mean − 2σ > 2/3 with σ = √(h(1 − h)/n_c) over at least 100 circuits (Cross 2019).

**Budget alongside** **[background]**: the Section 9.6 scales, the Section 6.8 channel of each native kind reduced to the benchmarked qubits, and the SPAM errors, composed to first order as r_channel = Σ n_kind ε_kind and F_gates = Π(1 − ε)^n. It is an estimate, not a bound: coherent errors cancel within a Clifford and add across consecutive crosstalk pulses.

### 7.10 The control hardware chain

Between the schedule and the ion sit a DDS, an amplifier, a modulator and the beam path. No source models them, so they are standard instrumentation physics **[background]**, exposed as `HardwareChain` parameters and entering through the drive coefficient of Section 5.2.

- **Quantization**: detunings to f_clk/2^bits, phases to 2π/2^bits, amplitudes to full scale/2^bits; the rounded values are what is played.
- **Amplifier saturation** (optional): Ω = Ω_sat tanh(Ω_prog/Ω_sat).
- **Modulator response**: a first-order low-pass of time constant τ_r (beam diameter over acoustic velocity for light, 1/(2πB) for microwaves) across each pulse train, the Stark shift following the played light. The beat note then leads by arctan(2πμτ_r), which every entangling pulse's leg phases compensate at the τ_r of the drive's own path when the run plays the chain (`Physics.hardware_chain`), and never when it does not **[recomputed here]**. The response also weights each envelope edge's off-resonant carrier transient by |H(μ)| = (1 + (2πμτ_r)²)^(−1/2), so square AM segments are better through a 50 ns modulator than through an ideal one (1 − F = 7.71 × 10⁻⁵ against 1.32 × 10⁻⁴); without the carrier (the sideband RWA) the two agree to 10⁻⁸ **[recomputed here]**.
- **Phase coherence**: `phase_continuous=False` resets the beat note per gate through the leg phases, 2πμ(0)t_g for a constant detuning and a detuning schedule alike, so each gate starts as calibrated; with `True`, the start phase ζ = 2πμt_g is part of the gate and Roos's tilt sin²[(4Ω̄/μ) sin ζ] is reported **[corrected]**. The virtual-Z rule assumes phase-continuous operation at the qubit frequency.
- **Timing**: dead times are idle evolution, and jitter shifts each pulse train rigidly.
- **Beams**: pointing drift is the quasi-static Rabi drift (Section 6.4); the Raman path difference carries the beat-note phase noise (Section 6.3).
- **Detection electronics**: Section 8.8.

A device without these parameters has ideal electronics, and its report says so.

## 8. Readout, SPAM and results

Readout has four layers: atomic rates, photon records, discriminators, error budget. Three conventions are fixed first **[verified]**: bright/dark polarity belongs to the readout scheme, not the qubit (171Yb+ direct fluorescence: |1⟩ = F = 1 bright; the 40Ca+ optical qubit: |1⟩ = D₅/₂ the dark shelf); Γ is an angular population decay rate in s⁻¹; ε_sys enters once, from scattered to detected rate.

### 8.1 Atomic-rate layer

R_∘, R_d and R_b come from the multi-level Bloch steady state of the detection beams (Section 4.2.8): R_∘ is the photon rate of the conditional bright state, R_d and R_b come from the slowest Liouvillian mode that moves population between the bright and dark manifolds. R_∘ counts the cycling line only, since ε_sys carries one filter; the all-line total is the W(Δ) of Section 4.2.8 **[corrected]**. The closed forms below are the checks.

**Bright rate** for 171Yb+ ²S₁/₂ F = 1 → ²P₁/₂ F′ = 0 (Noek 2013; Crain 2019) **[verified]**:

R_∘ = (Γ/18) s_∘/[1 + (2/9)s_∘ + (2Δ/Γ)²],  s_∘ = 2Ω²/Γ² = I/I_sat,

with Γ = 2π × 19.62 MHz the S₁/₂-P₁/₂ partial rate, Ω the full J = ½ line Rabi frequency with no angular factor, and I_sat = 50.8 mW/cm². It saturates at Γ/4, not Γ/2 **[corrected]**. Γ/18 = Γ·½·⅓·⅓ holds the hyperfine factor ⅓ and the polarization share ⅓ at the magic angle 54.74° to B; pure σ± light, or a polarization along or across B, leaves a dark state **[corrected]**. Noek's (Γ/6, 2/3) form is the same result for s = s_∘/3 (Section 8.8). The exact rate comes within 0.15-0.5% of the closed form at the optimal destabilizing field, which scales with the pumping rate (about 5 G at Crain's s_∘ = 2.45, where R_∘ = 0.0880Γ) **[recomputed here]**.

**Acton's stretch-state scheme** (σ⁺ on P₃/₂, nuclear spin I): the leak factors M₁ = 4I(3 + 2I)/(9(1 + 2I)²), M_2π = 4I/(9 + 18I) and M_2− = 16I/(9(1 + 2I)³) are normalized to 2/9, 1/9, 1/9 at I = ½; the printed normalization gives M₁ = 8/9 **[corrected]**.

**Dark states** (Berkeland and Boshier 2002) **[verified]**. A closed manifold J_i → J_f has no dark state for J_f = J_i + 1, one for J_f = J_i and two for J_f = J_i − 1, spanning the null space of M_{m_f m_i} ∝ (−1)^q(−1)^{J_f − m_f}(J_f 1 J_i; −m_f q m_i)E_{−q}; for J_i = 1 → J_f = 0 that is Σ_m c_mE_m = 0, so σ⁺ light leaves span{|1,0⟩, |1,+1⟩} dark. Eq. (8) prints the reversed pairing, which gives the conjugate polarization's dark states; the paper also labels E_{+1}, E_0, E_{−1} as σ⁻, π, σ⁺ (the reverse of Section 13) and orders the 3j columns differently in different equations **[corrected]**. The photon rate is bounded by the saturation ceiling n_e/(n_e + n_g) of Γ: ¼ for the 171Yb+ manifold, ⅓ for a Λ system (Olmschenk 2007's ⅓), ½ for two levels **[background]**. Every computed rate is asserted against it, and the lumped F(η) → F(η/3) model is never applied. Fluorescence peaks at a dark-state evolution rate δ ≈ Ω/2 with Ω ≈ γ/3 and linear polarization at arccos(1/√3) to B (once misprinted "≈ 63°") **[corrected]**; at B = 0 polarization modulation restores it, and the rate is the period average at the one-period propagator's fixed point (Section 4.2.8) **[corrected]**. Berkeland's Eq. (12) omits the two-3j ground feeding and the damping terms that make the map trace preserving, which the builder of Section 4.2.8 has; its Λ-arm repumper window is √αΩ_SP < Ω_DP < γ/2, the printed condition being too strong by 1/α² **[corrected]**.

**Metastable manifold.** A 171Yb+ ion branches 0.501% to D₃/₂ per photon and returns through the 935 nm repump, which redistributes F = 0 and F = 1; in 40Ca+ the 6.4% D₃/₂ branch and the 397/866 nm dark resonances set the budget. A repump manifold enters the Bloch model when the device has its beam. For 171Yb+ the repump level's decay to S₁/₂ is untabulated, so that configuration is refused, and the D₃/₂ branch is folded back into the S line or sent to a sink **[contested]**.

**Leakage** **[recomputed here]**: R_d, bright to dark through F = 1 → F′ = 1 at Δ_HFP with ⅓ into F = 0, and R_b, dark to bright through F = 0 → F′ = 1 at Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS:

R_d ≈ (2/27)(Γ/2)s_∘(Γ/(2Δ_HFP))²,  R_b ≈ (2/9)(Γ/2)s_∘(Γ/(2(Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS)))²,

with Δ_HFP = 2π × 2.1 GHz and Δ_HFS = 2π × 12.6 GHz. Noek's (2/3)(1/3) and (2/3) are these at s = s_∘/3, and the angular algebra of Section 4.5.2 settles the factor 2 between Noek and Crain in Noek's favour **[corrected]**. Both are linear in intensity with no saturation, which keeps the operating point at s_∘ ≲ 1 **[verified]**; R_b/R_d = 3/49 against a measured 0.048 **[verified]**. For I = ½ clock-state readout through P₃/₂ the ceiling is F_max = 1 − (4/9)(γ/2ω_HFP)² **[corrected]**.

**Detection**: R_det = ε_sys R_∘ with ε_sys = ε_geom ε_optics ε_filter ε_det and ε_geom = (1 − cos(arcsin NA))/2 **[verified]**; the background is an additive Poisson rate.

**Shelving**: ε_decay = 1 − exp(−t_det/τ_D), Christensen printing the exponent with the wrong sign **[corrected]**. Transfer is a start distribution per level (in 43Ca+ non-stretch states shelve with up to 30%), so Π_dark is not rank one and the readout is not QND **[corrected]**.

### 8.2 Photon-record layer

Given the sampled internal outcome (Section 5.7), each ion's record is a Poisson process switched by the bright/dark/shelf Markov chain, plus background. The exact count distribution is one matrix exponential of the augmented generator **[derived]**; the fast path samples the chain exactly with piecewise Poisson counts, and the trajectory path runs `mcsolve` with a photon-counting operator. Optional dead time and afterpulsing act on the arrivals. The single-jump forms are the small-kτ checks: Crain's Eq. 1 lacks the no-jump term e^{−R_T t}P_p(n; R₁t), without which it sums to 1 − e^{−R_T t}, and his zero-threshold errors need ε_sys in the no-jump exponent, e^{−(ε_sys R_∘ + R_bg)t} **[corrected]**. A bright-prepared ion's mean count n̄(τ) = εR_∘[(R_b/k)τ + (R_d/k²)(1 − e^{−kτ})], k = R_b + R_d, is the calibration fit for (εR_∘, R_d, R_b), on records long against 1/R_d and 1/R_b **[verified]**.

### 8.3 Discriminator layer

A strategy interface over one record **[verified]**:

- **Threshold**: bright iff the counts over t_b exceed n_c, with (n_c, t_b) optimized.
- **Time-resolved maximum likelihood** (Myerson 2008): p_B = ΠB(n_i) and p_D = (1 − t_b/τ)ΠD(n_i) + (t_s/τ)Σ_jΠ_{i<j}D(n_i)Π_{i≥j}B(n_i), 1/τ the dark class's brightening rate, by the O(N) recursion M_k = M_{k−1}D(n_k), S_k = (S_{k−1} + M_{k−1})B(n_k) in the log domain; ties go to bright.
- **Adaptive**: stop when the posterior error e_D = p_B/(p_B + p_D), or e_B, falls below a cutoff, with equal priors, which the printed form omits **[corrected]**; Noek's and Crain's first-photon protocols.
- **Camera** (Burrell 2010): per-pixel likelihoods, neighbour-conditioned decoding by iterated conditional modes seeded all-dark **[corrected]**, read noise (kr)² linear in the readouts **[corrected]**, and the Airy scale 1.22λ/NA **[contested]**.

A threshold's confusion is exact from the count distributions; any other strategy's is sampled (2 × 10⁴ records per level and ion) with a declared binomial error **[derived]**.

### 8.4 Error-budget layer and the measurement operator

The budget is named channels (preparation, transfer, shelf decay, discrimination, register crosstalk). ε_B (bright read dark) and ε_D (dark read bright) are reported separately and averaged last, ε = ½(ε_B + ε_D) **[verified]**; published tables are state-averaged, and Acton uses min(F_B, F_D) **[verified]**. Register fidelity is (1 − ε_s)^{N_q} **[verified]**.

The internal outcome is sampled projectively from the final state, keeping correlations (Section 5.7), and each ion's bit then comes from its POVM, a P(declared | level) table with the transfer channel folded in, indexed by the sampled level **[corrected]**; post-measurement states are discarded. The product POVM is exact without crosstalk; with crosstalk the confusion is factored by neighbour range, dense to twelve ions, and `Readout(mode="full")` runs the record layers per shot instead. The apparatus presets (Myerson's PMT and Crain's SNSPD chains) are calibration inputs, never species constants.

### 8.5 Readout crosstalk and mid-circuit measurement

A neighbour's scattered light degrades discrimination, not η_d (Wineland 1998) **[corrected]**: it adds counts at the PSF fraction and extra pumping, a bright ion at spacing x illuminating a neighbour with s_nb = 3λ²/(8π²x²), Acton's bound with I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) (his printed ħ for h makes it 2π too large) **[corrected]**. A camera's PSF is an apparatus input: a Gaussian reproduces Burrell's 4.0% nearest-neighbour leakage but not his 0.9% next-nearest wing **[recomputed here]**. A spectator of a detection dephases as a Gaussian exp(−τ²/α²) (Crain 2019), drawn as a per-shot frequency offset rather than a σ_z Lindblad term **[verified]**.

Mid-circuit measurement is refused (Section 7.2). Its stage would add the detection's recoil heating (10³ to 10⁴ photons, Section 4.2.8) and the measured ion's recooling and re-preparation, without changing the pipeline **[corrected]**.

### 8.6 Results

A `Result` holds per shot the bitstring, the count record, the discriminator's confidence and the shot's dynamical sample (`Result.sample_of_shot`), plus counts, per-qubit ε_B and ε_D and the diagnostics; optionally the final state (QuTiP's tensor order) or the trajectories. Keys have qubit 0 as the least-significant bit (Section 13). The IonQ exporters (v0.4 and its client SDKs) **[verified]**: v1 keys the decimal integer of the register with qubit 0 as the least-significant bit (`{"5": 0.5}` on three qubits is `101`), with histogram and per-shot forms; v2 nests zero-padded bitstrings per register in IonQ's wire order, q[0] first (`x q[0]` on three qubits is `"1"` in v1 and `"100"` in v2).

### 8.7 Gaps the sources leave

No source gives a continuous-time arrival likelihood, a model of detector dead time or afterpulsing (user parameters here), a first-principles preparation error (here from Section 4.2.6), correlated register SPAM, the neighbour assignment of end ions, or the temperature dependence of shelving (derived in Section 8.8).

### 8.8 Saturation parameters, apparatus residuals and motion in readout

- **Saturation parameters.** Noek's s is s_∘/3 = I/(3I_sat) and Acton's s is I/I_sat. From Crain's 472 kcps at ε_sys = 4.356%, s_∘ = 2.45 against his quoted I/I_sat = 1.10, a factor 2.2. Since R_∘ maximizes the exact steady state at fixed intensity, a higher measured rate means more light at the ion or a higher ε_sys: an apparatus residual, not missing physics **[corrected]**.
- **Leakage residuals.** At s_∘ = 2.45 the Bloch solve gives R_d = 249 Hz at the fluorescence optimum (4.7 G) and 347 Hz at 1 G, against Crain's 341(13) Hz; R_d depends on the field, so the measured triple constrains the apparatus, never a species constant **[recomputed here]**.
- **Detector non-idealities** (dead time, afterpulsing, SNSPD recovery) are the plausible remainder between Crain's corrected optimum 5.84 × 10⁻⁴ and his measured 6.9 × 10⁻⁴; an optional model takes user parameters **[extracted]**.
- **Micromotion on the detection beam**: R(β) = J₀²(β)R(Δ) + J₁²(β)[R(Δ − Ω_rf) + R(Δ + Ω_rf)] from three Bloch solves; R_∘ loses the sideband share while R_d barely moves, so micromotion raises R_d/R_∘ **[corrected]**. The detection rates are the nominal device's: a sampled field, stray-field, pointing or laser-frequency drift moves the gate drives, not the readout, and the run's approximations say so.
- **Motion**: the shelving transfer is averaged over the thermal velocity along k, untested against data **[background]**; with v_rms = √(Σ_m ħω_m(2n̄_m + 1)/(2m)), first-order Doppler broadening stays well below the 19.7 MHz linewidth until n̄ ≳ 100, past the Lamb-Dicke guard **[recomputed here]**.

# Part IV. Validation, performance, conventions and the application

## 9. Validation suite

The tests define correctness, in the conventions of Section 13 (a source's convention appears only as a labelled conversion). **Pinned tests** (closed forms, recomputed values, identities, conversions) fail the build; without a stated tolerance they hold to 10⁻⁹ relative for closed forms, the printed digits for recomputed values and 10⁻¹² for operator identities. **Consistency anchors** (laboratory numbers) report against the published uncertainty (two sigma), or 20% where none is given, and do not fail the build (Section 12). Tests run at JOINT_EXACT unless marked, multi-ion rows on their preset's resolved modes with the frozen-spectator contribution reported. A source marked [corrected] prints a wrong form; the target is the corrected one.

### 9.1 Trap and crystal

| Target | Anchor | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Monodromy β | √(a + q²/2) to O(q⁴); edge q = 0.9080 at a = 0; β(a, q) = β(a, −q), so ν_x = ν_y when a_x = a_y | RMP 2003 Eq. 15; Mathieu tables |
| Floquet function sign | under a − 2q cos 2ξ at q = 0.1: c_{±1}/c₀ = −q/((2 ± β)² − a) = −0.023322, −0.026875; u̇(0)/(iνu(0)) = 1.1036 | RMP 2003 (opposite rf phase origin) |
| Floquet companion matrix | A = diag(−0.004, −0.004, 0.008), Q rotated 20°, q = 0.30: ∣λ∣ = 1, β = 0.0894, 0.2061; the printed sign gives ∣λ∣ = 1.3244, 0.7550 | House 2008 Eqs. 4-10 [corrected] |
| Pseudopotential and trajectory | ω_r = QV₀/(√2Ω_T mR²) (Q the charge) = qΩ_T/(2√2) at a = 0; micromotion ratio q/2, second harmonic q²/32 | Wineland 1998 Eqs. 4, 6 |

| Multi-ion heating | uniform noise heats only the COM at N× (9 × 7.2 = 65 quanta/s at 2.74 MHz); uncorrelated noise heats every mode equally; even-parity modes of a symmetric chain have ṅ = 0; Σħω_kṅ_k = (e²S_E/4)(1/m₁ + 1/m₂) | Brownnutt 2015; Lechner 2016; Wübbena 2012 |

| Budgets | Harty 6.8 × 10⁻⁴; Christensen 3.4 × 10⁻⁴ against 2.9(6) × 10⁻⁴; Egan 0.71%/0.22%; product POVM = record path at zero crosstalk, a bounded reported difference at 4% leakage, Bell correlations kept | Harty 2014; Christensen 2020 [corrected]; Egan 2021 |
| Camera | ε_D → t_exp/(2τ) = 1.71 × 10⁻⁴ at 400 µs, no gain from time resolution; register error Σe^{−R_k}; σ = 4.1 µm PSF gives 4.0% at 14 µm | Burrell 2010 |
