### 9.9 Numerical convergence

Every case above changes its reported probabilities by less than its tolerance when the tolerances are tightened tenfold (loosened tenfold for the integrator ladder) and the caps raised by 2 within the dimension ceiling (`Numerics.convergence_check` reports the tolerance arm as `Diagnostics.convergence`); the boundary population stays below the Section 5.5 threshold with the Section 5.1.1 margin; frozen-spectator runs reproduce the joint χ within the reported bound; one and several workers agree shot by shot and to 10⁻¹² (trajectories averaged in keyed order); `improved_sampling` and plain trajectories converge to the `mesolve` histogram.

| Target | Anchor | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Displacement operator | expm against the Laguerre elements (n, n′ < d/2): 2 × 10⁻¹³, 2 × 10⁻⁶, 10⁻³ at d = 8 and ≤ 10⁻¹⁴ at 40 (η = 0.1, 0.5, 1.0); the analytic top column loses 9.3% at (10, 0.1) | derived |
| ENR | expm of the ENR generator to 2 × 10⁻¹⁰ at N_exc = 6 (η = 0.1); the ENR marginal equals the embedded partial trace | derived |
| Picture equivalence | Schrödinger-motion and sideband interaction pictures agree to 1.5 × 10⁻⁸ (blue sideband, n_max = 11) | derived |

## 11. Performance

### 11.1 Measured cost

Reference machine: QuTiP 5.3.1, NumPy 2.5, SciPy 1.18, Python 3.13, Apple Accelerate BLAS, 18 CPUs. Fixture: two 171Yb+ ions at ω_x, ω_y, ω_z = 3.0, 2.9, 1.0 MHz with the Raman Δk in the transverse plane at 30° from x (modes x-COM 3.000, x-rocking 2.828 and y-COM 2.900 MHz with η = 0.080, ±0.0824 and 0.047), a bichromatic Mølmer-Sørensen pulse at ε/2π = 10 kHz and the maximally entangling closure ηΩ/ε = 1/2 (a 100 µs single loop), the exact drive operator of Section 5.1.1, `sesolve` at atol 10⁻¹⁰ and rtol 10⁻⁸ from |↓↓⟩|0…0⟩, d_m Fock levels per mode. Times are per 100 µs gate **[recomputed here]**:

| Joint dimension | Assembled, merged dense | Assembled, merged CSR | Factorized kernel | Factorized kernel, rotating frame (default) | Evaluations per 20 µs, Schrödinger picture |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 48 (1 mode, d_m = 12) | 0.15 s | 0.1 s | 1.6 s | 0.7 s | 1.9 × 10⁴ |
| 256 (2 modes, d_m = 8) | 0.6 s | 1.7 s | 2.5 s | 0.8 s | 2.1 × 10⁴ |
| 864 (3 modes, d_m = 6) | 6 s | 25 s | 4.9 s | 1.2 s | 2.4 × 10⁴ |
| 2048 (3 modes, d_m = 8) | 2.1 min | 3.6 min | 12 s | 1.9 s | 3.5 to 3.8 × 10⁴ |

`dop853` is 10 to 25% faster than `vern9` on all but one row, and the two integrators' final states agree to 1.3 × 10⁻⁷ (dimension 48) to 5.8 × 10⁻⁷ (2048) in norm, which is what "identical results" means in this specification; the two constructions of one operator agree to 10⁻¹³ to 2 × 10⁻⁸. Of the assembled forms the merged dense operator is fastest at every measured size: for two ions Σ_i(σ₊^i D_i + h.c.) fills half the matrix, where a CSR product (0.5 to 0.6 ns per non-zero, memory-bound) loses to the dense BLAS product (0.16 ns per element at dimension 2048). The engine's `auto` kernel assembles the small spaces and factorizes the large ones; `mesolve` always uses CSR, because there the Liouvillian's memory decides. The rotating frame (Section 11.3 item 10) cuts the right-hand-side evaluations 3.2, 4.1, 5.0 and 7.7 times on these rows. The real 100 µs pulse of the two-ion example device on the space [2, 2, 9, 15] that the cap rule selects takes 5.1 s in the Schrödinger picture and 1.0 s in the rotating frame (43 against 7 integrator steps per period of the 3 MHz mode). Density-matrix evolution stops being practical near dimension 300 (a 10⁵ × 10⁵ Liouvillian), so dissipation at scale runs on trajectories.

On the two-ion example machine (`presets.yb171_chain(2)`): the surrogate calibration takes about 6 s; a Bell circuit with 2000 shots about 3.5 s (five carrier pulses and one entangling gate on the space [2, 2, 11, 13]); a 384-Clifford single-qubit RB sequence 2 s (its carrier pulses run on the internal space through the propagator cache); a two-qubit Clifford about 10 s; the Bell circuit at `level="GATE_LOCAL"` about 5 s (17 s on the reference tomography route); a two-qubit quantum-volume circuit about a minute; calibration by simulated experiments about twelve minutes. The three-ion GHZ circuit at dimension 1152 takes several minutes per run. Randomized benchmarking to the 10⁻⁵ level therefore runs hundreds of single-qubit Cliffords and thousands of shots, while two-qubit RB and quantum volume run at tens of Cliffords and a handful of circuits, and their results say so.

### 11.2 Cost model

Wall time is the number of right-hand-side evaluations times the cost of one application of H(t) to the state.

- **Evaluations.** 1.9 × 10⁴ to 3.8 × 10⁴ per 20 µs with `dop853` in the Schrödinger picture (27 to 52 steps per period of the 3 MHz mode at 12 evaluations per step, growing with the cap), 1.2 times more with `vern9`: a 100 µs pulse needs 1 to 2 × 10⁵ evaluations and a 10 µs single-qubit pulse 1 to 2 × 10⁴, and the rotating frame divides both by 3 to 8.
- **Cost per evaluation.** Dominated by the drive operator: σ₊^i ⊗ Π_m D_m has 2^{N−1} Π_m d_m² non-zeros per ion, and the merged Σ_i(σ₊^i D_i + h.c.) has N 2^N Π_m d_m², a fraction N/2^N of the (2^N Π_m d_m)² matrix, shared by the tones through the coefficient. A CSR product costs 0.5 to 0.6 ns per non-zero (memory-bound at about 35 GB/s, 20 bytes per non-zero); a dense product 0.16 ns per element beyond cache and 0.07 to 0.09 ns inside it. At dimension 2048 that is 1.2 ms per evaluation in CSR and 0.65 ms dense, and 3.0 ms for four unmerged dense terms.
- **Scaling.** Cost = a + b × (elements touched per evaluation) × evaluations with a ≈ 0.02 s and b = 0.16 ns (dense beyond cache) or 0.58 ns (CSR) predicts every measured row within about 20% (`Machine.estimate` uses it). Doubling d_m on three modes multiplies the elements by 64 and the evaluations by about 1.4; a fourth mode at d_m = 8 multiplies the elements by 64; an extra ion multiplies the dense cost by 4 and the CSR cost by 2(N + 1)/N. Four ions with three modes at d_m = 8 (dimension 8192; 6.7 × 10⁷ dense elements, 1.1 GB, or 1.7 × 10⁷ non-zeros) cost about 10 ms per evaluation assembled, 25 to 30 minutes per 100 µs gate, which the factorized kernel (24 against 512 amplitudes per mode product) brings back to minutes. Eight ions with eight axial modes at N_exc = 6 have a drive operator of 1.15 × 10⁹ non-zeros (18.5 GB per ion), which is why the reductions below are not optional and why the JOINT_EXACT range of Section 5.4 is a dimension budget.

### 11.3 Reduction techniques, in the order they are applied

1. **ENR truncation** (option) for a group of cold, undriven modes carried dynamically: C(M + N_exc, N_exc) states instead of Π_m d_m, with the exponential of the ENR sum generator as the operator (Section 5.1.1). An ENR group is not a product space, so item 4 does not apply to it and the two are never composed on the same modes.
2. **Mode selection per pulse.** A mode leaves a pulse's resolved set when its closed-form residual displacement and entangling-angle contribution (Section 4.4.3 integrals over the pulse's actual detuning schedule) are both negligible, |α_m(τ)|²(2n̄_m + 1) < 10⁻⁶ and |χ_m(τ)| < 10⁻⁴ by default, never on η alone: a mode with η = 10⁻³ two kilohertz from a tone contributes more than one with η = 0.05 a megahertz away. A mode above this pair but with |χ_m| below the freeze tolerance (0.05 rad) is frozen rather than dropped. Dropped modes evolve freely (their heating still applies) and the summed dropped contribution is reported.
3. **Frozen spectators** (Section 5.2): modes far from every tone leave the joint space and enter through per-shot Debye-Waller factors, with the off-resonant excitation bound reported.
4. **Mode-factorized application of the drive operator** (product spaces only). Because Π_m D_m is a tensor product, applying it mode by mode costs Σ_m d_m multiply-adds per amplitude instead of Π_m d_m (24 against 512 for three modes at d_m = 8), through a matrix-free right-hand side registered as a QuTiP data-layer type, exact to round-off. The measured gain is smaller than that ratio because each factor step has a fixed cost (about 2.5 µs per step plus 0.6 ns per multiply-add, against 0.57 ns per non-zero assembled), so the `auto` kernel follows the measured crossover: assembled below dimension 440, factorized above (299 against 24 µs per evaluation at 2048). An ENR group and every `mesolve` segment stay assembled.
5. **Cached operators.** The σ₊ ⊗ D_i matrices (or their factors) per (ion, Δk, space) are built once. Pulse propagators are reused only on internal-state-only spaces (Section 5.3), never on the joint space; there the cached propagator is the step's channel and the GATE_LOCAL tomography reads its Kraus operator off it, while on a joint space the tomography propagates the Π_i d_i internal basis columns, never the D × D propagator. One engine serves a whole GATE_LOCAL walk, and the one-ion idle channels are cached by (ion, duration, engine setup, sample, options).
6. **Interaction frame for slow pulses** (option): for microwave or co-propagating Raman single-qubit gates with negligible motional coupling, the motion factors out and the pulse evolves on the internal space alone with the exact Debye-Waller reduction folded into Ω.
7. **Sideband-truncated interaction picture** (Section 5.2), when the cap is large and few sidebands matter.
8. **GATE_LOCAL** (Section 5.4) above the joint-exact range.
9. **Trajectories and parallelism.** Trajectories, branches and quasi-static samples are spread over the cores with QuTiP's parallel `map`, which forks and pickles, so every `QobjEvo` coefficient is a module-level named function, a `Coefficient` or an array: a lambda or closure raises `PicklingError` under `map="parallel"` on 5.3.1 (only `map="loky"`, an optional dependency, survives it), and the builder never emits closures **[corrected]**.
10. **Exact rotating frame.** Every ket segment integrates iφ̇ = Θ†V(t)Θφ with Θ = e^{−iH₀t} for the diagonal H₀ = H_mot + H_int (plus a constant Stark shift): the phases are applied around the unchanged drive operators of items 4 and 5, collapse operators that are eigenoperators of ad_{H₀} carry e^{iλt}, and expectation values, stored and final states are phased back. A segment with a collapse operator that is not an eigenoperator (a recoil kick, the intensity-noise channel) stays in the Schrödinger picture, because `mcsolve` forms c†c from the operators it is given. Nothing is expanded or truncated: results equal the Schrödinger-picture integration to the solver tolerance, and `SegmentReport.frame` names the picture each segment used **[recomputed here]**.

### 11.4 Interactive budgets for the application

The budgets of Section 14.7 follow from Section 11.1. Re-simulating one trajectory of a zoomed 100 µs entangling pulse with its recorded noise sample takes under a second up to about dimension 864 and about 2 s at 2048 in the rotating frame; anything that needs an ensemble (a representative jump record, a process matrix from 16 tomography inputs) and recalibration run as background jobs with progress, the record's stored traces shown at once and stale badges until they finish. The re-derivation of modes, η, pulse-solver solutions and closed-form channel estimates after a device change is immediate, with pulse re-solves of at most about 10 s per pair in the background. App-side channel replay of a 20-qubit, 100-gate circuit takes seconds.

### 11.5 Memory

States are dense complex128; at dimension 10⁶ a state is 16 MB, and a trajectory ensemble is stored only as final states or sampled observables. An assembled drive operator at dimension 2048 (2.1 × 10⁶ non-zeros) is about 40 MB in CSR and 67 MB dense and grows as Π_m d_m²; the factorized kernel stores only the per-mode D_m. The level policy refuses to build a joint space above `joint_dimension_max` (default 4096, from the cost model of Section 11.2) or a drive operator above `nnz_max` non-zeros (default 2 × 10⁷, estimated from Π_m d_m² and the ion count) and routes `level="auto"` to GATE_LOCAL; an explicit `level="JOINT_EXACT"` above either guard is refused, and a cap that would grow past a guard during a run is refused the same way. Parallel maps fork their workers, each a copy-on-write image of the caller that can grow toward its size, so `Numerics.workers = None` caps the count to keep the workers' worst-case total (each as large as the parent's peak resident size) within half of physical memory; an explicit request is capped the same way, `QUTIP_TRAP_MAX_WORKERS` caps the default count only, a count of one runs in-process on the serial map, and `Diagnostics.workers` reports what ran.

## 12. Open physics and inputs the sources do not supply

- **Anomalous heating has no first-principles model.** The heating rate follows exactly from the electric-field noise spectral density S_E(ω) **[verified]**, but S_E is empirical: measured values span ten orders of magnitude, the distance exponent is disputed (2.6 to 4.0) and no microscopic mechanism is established **[verified]**. S_E(ω) is therefore a device input (constant, power law or tabulated) with its provenance, and every heating number is apparatus-local.
- **Noise enters as spectra the sources do not supply.** Electric-field, magnetic-field, laser-phase and intensity noise are device spectra with declared white parts; the sources give exponents and single numbers (α_S = 0.8(1), no spatial correlation length, no mechanism for the 1.2× and 1.35× excess over the uniform-field model at 15 and 25 ions, and the King and Rohde measurements disagree on whether the non-centre-of-mass modes heat, which the correlation-length input decides), so the absolute infidelities of the filter-function layer (Section 6.9) are conditional on the device's spectra. No source bounds non-Gaussian, non-stationary or quantum-bath noise; the filter functions assume zero-mean Gaussian wide-sense-stationary classical noise, and no source supplies an infrared regulator, so ω_min is an input reported beside χ (Section 6.9).
- **Published fidelities are not reproducible from first principles.** No mode-participation matrix and no AM or FM segment amplitudes are published for any chain whose fidelities are quoted, so Section 9 treats such reproductions as consistency anchors with stated assumptions.
- **The ZZ native gate has no published physical model**; the MS gate dressed by two single-qubit rotations is inferred from the 220 µs duration difference, implemented as that construction and labelled inferred (Section 4.4.4).
- **Composite pulses and decoupling.** PD6's phase vector at arbitrary angle needs numerical continuation rather than a printed formula; Low-Yoder-Chuang's AP3 and NS2 closed forms and prefactors were not transcribed, so same-order sequences are ranked by order, not by prefactor; no source gives a rule for choosing a family from a measured error triple, a scheduler objective, a second-order filter function, a closed-form detuning filter function, a multiplicative amplitude-noise filter function, multi-axis decoupling or an implementable dynamically corrected gate. The direct-RB error-rate prefactor is undecidable from the published data ((15/16)(1 − p) and 1 − p both fit), and is recorded as an open convention (Section 7.9).
- **The Bermudez three-variant mapping of ε onto Pauli channels** is named by the sources but specified by none with equations, so it is omitted; the Pauli twirl and the depolarizing rate of Section 6.8 are provided.
- **Comb drives.** The comb literature supplies no spontaneous-emission rate, no multi-ion multi-mode construction, no wave-front tolerance that closes, no beat-note or repetition-rate noise spectra, no fourth-order shift for a resonant carrier drive, and one species at one wavelength (171Yb+ at 355 nm); the crossover between the ultrafast-kick and resolved-sideband pictures is unbounded, the fourth-order Stark series is marginal at the quoted full power (Ω₀ above the detuning it expands in), and the factor 2 between the two internally consistent intensity-to-Rabi chains is contested (Section 4.3.7).
- **Readout apparatus quantities** (detection efficiency, background, a D-manifold branching fraction the sources do not tabulate for 171Yb+) are presets, never species constants; no hyperfine-resolved coherent-population-trapping Liouvillian and a dark-resonance map for 88Sr+ only are published (Section 8.1).
- **Cetina's quasi-static amplitude model is validated only for n̄ ≫ 1**; sideband-cooled modes should show revivals no source characterizes, and the exact position-operator implementation (Section 6.2) is compared with the closed forms only where they apply.
- **Micromotion beyond first order in q**, the rf phase relative to the pulse envelope, beam-intensity and pointing spectra and the laser linewidth as a spectrum are absent from the sources; the Floquet treatment of Section 4.1.1 covers the first, and the drive coefficients take the rest as supplied spectra. The quantized multi-ion Floquet frame is absent from the sources and Landa's classical coupled-mode construction is not built, so every N > 1 result is in the pseudopotential frame with C₀ per axis.
- **Unvalidated couplings.** The photon-recoil kernel of Section 4.2.8 is untested as the recoil operator of the Raman and Rayleigh dissipators (Section 4.5.5), and the E2 ac Stark shift of Section 4.5.7 has no published number to validate it.
- **Fock truncation for the exact exponential Hamiltonian at large n̄ has no sourced criterion**; the convergence policy of Section 5.5 is standard practice, enforced by the paired-cutoff test.
- **Implementation limits, stated where they arise:** rf phase-noise sideband heating and 2ω_sec parametric heating are not in a run's noise model, and the differential dc mode drift is drawn as independent per-mode offsets (Section 6.2), each named in the approximations of a run whose noise model carries it; the played chain converts Raman and optical drives only, so a miscalibrated table leaves microwave, light-shift and gradient drives error-free, and the table's fitted η is read by no solver (Section 7.3); the 171Yb+ 935 nm repump cycle cannot close without the untabulated 297 nm branch (Section 8.1); mid-circuit measurement is refused (Section 8.5).
- **Transport**, **planar crystals**, **mixed-species gates** and **recalibration in the loop** are out of scope (Section 1.3).

## 13. Conventions the simulator fixes

Every quantity below has at least two conventions in the primary literature, and published factor-of-2 (and 2π, and √N) errors trace to mixing them. The simulator adopts one convention per quantity and names the source it follows; the third column also names the forms in the sources that must not be imported as they stand. Modules restate the row they implement, and tests pin the conversions. The `conv.*` records of the provenance ledger are the machine-readable form of these rows.

| Quantity | Convention | Follows; forms not to import |
|---|---|---|
| Frequencies | internal frequencies angular (rad/s); every public number an ordinary frequency in Hz, converted once with an explicit 2π; names carry the unit (`_hz`, `_rad_s`, `_per_s`); a linewidth `gamma_hz` is the upper level's total decay rate over 2π | RMP 2003 (which writes ν for an angular frequency). Not: "γ = 2π × 19.6 MHz", "γ/2π = 60 MHz" and "19.7 MHz" used interchangeably; Leung's 3.045 MHz read as rad/s |
| Names of public numbers | every public float carries its unit suffix (`_hz`, `_s`, `_m`, `_w`, `_gauss`, `_rad`, `_v`, `_pa`, `_cps`; `_turns` only at the IonQ boundary); error-model names: `p_*` a probability per operation, `*_rate` per second, `*_ratio` a fraction of another probability, `*_scale` a dimensionless multiplier | this package |
| Logarithms | natural | Burrell 2010. Not: Harty's Fig. 3 log₁₀ |
| Mathieu equation | d²x/dξ² + [a − 2q cos 2ξ]x = 0, ξ = ω_rf t/2, a = 4Z∣e∣Uα/(mω_rf²), q = 2Z∣e∣Ũα′/(mω_rf²); the Mathieu q and the ion charge Q are never one name (the code calls the charge `charge`) | Leibfried et al. 2003. Not: Wineland 1998's and Berkeland 1998's +2q cos (a half-period shift); House 2008's Q = −q with its Eq. 7 matrix of the wrong sign (a stable trap reads unstable) |
| Characteristic exponent | the monodromy matrix, β on the principal branch (−1, 1], ω_j = β_jΩ/2; β ≈ √(a + q²/2) only as a check | Floquet theory. Not: RMP Eqs. 10-11 (index error) |
| Floquet function and rf phase origin | the adopted sign fixes the origin: u(t) ≈ e^{iνt}[1 − (q/2)cos ω_rf t]/(1 − q/2), c_{±1}/c₀ = −q/((2 ± β)² − a) | Section 4.1.1. Not: the RMP's quantum section and Wineland's [1 + (q/2)cos], the same physics half an rf period later |
| Micromotion factors | η_eff = ηC₀ with the Wronskian-normalized C₀ of Section 4.1.1, applied once, in `Crystal.lamb_dicke`; excess micromotion as the phase modulation J_n(β) on every drive (gates, cooling, detection) | Section 4.1.1; Wineland Eqs. 71-72; Berkeland Eq. 21 (misprint corrected). Not: the RMP's (1 + q/2)⁻¹; J_n applied to gates only |
| Coupled Floquet normalization | companion matrix F = [[0, 𝟙], [−(A + 2Q cos 2τ), 0]], U(0) = 𝟙, monodromy at τ = π; Vᵗ(0)U(0) = i/2 elementwise; Γ⁻¹ by the closed form, never a dagger | Landa 2012. Not: House's sign; the paper-literal inverted C₋₂ step |
| rf amplitude and pseudopotential | V_rf the peak of a cos(Ω_rf t) drive; ψ = Q²∣E∣²/(4mΩ²) in joules; Chiaverini's and Madsen's V₀ are differential amplitudes (±V₀/2), Home's Φ a pseudopotential in volts, Kielpinski's φ an energy | House; Wesenberg. Not: an rms reading (doubles the depth, √2 on every rf frequency) or a peak-to-peak one (quarters the depth); the charge applied twice |
| Surface-electrode geometry | widths from gap centre to gap centre; ion height h; the ion at the minimum of the total potential, not the rf null; the escape point a saddle from G′(Z) = 0; the rf pseudopotential isotropic transversely at a 2D null, so the dc sets the axes | House; Wesenberg; Nizamani. Not: the rf Hessian angle as a mode axis; a maximum finder for the saddle; Nizamani's q_N in ω = qΩ/(2√2) |
| Ladder operators | x̂ = x₀(a + a†), x₀ = √(ħ/2mω) | RMP Eq. 26. Not: Brownnutt arXiv v1's √(ħmω); the factor 4 under the root in Ozeri 2007 and Baldwin 2021 |
| Zero-point energy | dropped, H = Σħω_k a†a | Wineland 1998; Marquet 2003 (James keeps ħω/2, a convention) |
| Mode index | every mode index is a position in `Crystal.modes`, ordered axial, transverse_1, transverse_2, ascending in frequency within a family; eigenvectors unit norm, last component positive | Section 4.1.3. Not: eigensolver order; James's transverse numbering by decreasing frequency |
| Mass-weighted eigenvectors and parity | orthonormal in u_i = √m_i z_i only (physical amplitudes carry 1/√m_i); the overall sign a gauge, relative signs physical; inversion parity p = +1 is an antisymmetric displacement pattern, exempt from uniform-field noise and uncoolable by a centre ion | Morigi-Walther; Kielpinski; Wübbena; Home. Not: renormalizing raw displacements (20 to 160% off at μ ≠ 1); Morigi-Walther Eq. 9's oblique form; "even" read as "in-phase" |
| Lamb-Dicke parameter | η_{i,m} = (Δk·ê_m) c_{i,m} √(ħ/(2m_i ω_m)) with the ion's own mass and the mass-weighted unit-norm eigenvector component, the participation counted once (Section 4.1.7) | Wineland 1998 Eq. 33; Monroe 2020; Home 2013 Eq. 19; Morigi-Walther Eq. 12. Not: the per-ion COM η of Sørensen-Mølmer and Leung, which contains 1/√N; Lee's 1/√2 inside η; Ozeri's z₀ = √(ħ/4Mω) (Srinivas's r₀ with the total mass belongs to the gradient force, Section 4.4.5) |
| Heating rate | a quoted rate is d⟨n⟩/dt at n = 0, Γ_h = ΓN̄; thermal diffusion Γ(2N̄ + 1); collapse operators √(Γ(N̄ + 1)) a and √(ΓN̄) a†; ṅ ∝ ω^{−(α+1)} when S_E ∝ ω^{−α} | Brownnutt 2015 Eqs. 15-17; Wineland Eq. 62 (typo fixed). Not: the journal's 2aρ†a†; the equal-rate pair {√ṅ a, √ṅ a†} with a finite N̄ (it is the N̄ ≫ 1 limit) |
| Electric-field noise density | single-sided S_E, Γ_h = e²S_E/(4mħω), converted at ingest to the noise layer's two-sided angular spectra | Brownnutt Eqs. 11-12. Not: the two-sided e²S_E/(2mħω) unconverted; the arXiv Turchette Eq. 3 (the PRA is consistent) |
| Noise spectra | every stored spectrum two-sided in angular frequency with the e^{−iωt} kernel and a separately declared white level (Section 6.1) | Section 6.1 |
| Motional dephasing operator | L = a†a √(2/τ); the coherence of ∣n⟩ + ∣n′⟩ decays at (n − n′)²/τ | Ballance 2016 |
| Qubit dephasing operator | L = √(γ_φ/2) σ_z, so the coherence decays at γ_φ and γ_φ = 1/T₂ for the white part; a measured T₂ is split into its quasi-static (Gaussian) and white (exponential) parts before use | Section 6.3. Not: conventions giving 2/T₂ |
| Debye-Waller statistics | shot to shot: Ω constant within a shot and distributed across shots; never the ensemble-mean factor inside one shot | Wineland 1998; Cetina 2022 |
| Rabi frequency | H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊e^{…} + h.c., carrier π pulse at Ωt = π; the π time is convention independent, so what breaks is any formula written in the symbol | RMP 2003; Monroe 2020; Lee 2005. Not: P = sin²(Ωt) (Wineland 1998, Monroe 1995, Blümel 2021 Eq. S28), Uys's Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ) and Wineland's stretched-element g with Ωτ_π = π/2, each half this Ω; Joshi's Ω referenced to the S₁/₂-P₃/₂ stretched line (√(3/2) in Ω, 3/2 in s on the D1 σ line) |
| Single-photon coupling g | not a stored symbol: the code stores Ω, with ingest maps g = Ω/2 (Monroe 1995, Wineland 1998, Ozeri, Section 4.5.5) and g = Ω (the Lee comb chain, Section 4.3.7); Monroe's Ω = g₁g₂/Δ is Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ) | Sections 4.2.2, 4.3.7, 4.5.5 |
| Two-photon Rabi frequency | Ω_R = Σ_e Ω₂*Ω₁/(2Δ_e) with the single-photon Ω_j = E_j⟨e∣d·ε̂_j∣g⟩/ħ in the ħΩ/2 convention and Δ_e = ω_L − ω_eg; light shift δ_g = Σ∣Ω∣²/(4Δ_e) (Section 4.5.4) | Wineland 2003 Eqs. 2.3, 2.10 (his overall minus sign is a phase convention). Not: RMP A13 and Wineland 2003 read as Ω₁Ω₂/Δ_R, 2× too large |
| Rabi frequency from intensity | Ω = E₀∣ε_q⟨F′ m_F + q∣d_q∣F m_F⟩∣/ħ with E₀ = √(2I/(ε₀c)); on the cycling transition Ω = Γ√(I/(2I_sat)), and s = I/I_sat = 2Ω²/Γ² there only | Steck. Not: Noek's s in the (Γ/6, 2/3) detection form, which is I/(3I_sat) |
| Saturation intensity | I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) with Γ the angular partial rate of the cycling branch, Γ_partial = 2π γ_hz × branching (Section 4.5.2) | Steck. Not: Acton's ħ for h; `gamma_hz` unconverted |
| Saturation parameters | s₀ = I/I_sat per beam on resonance (Ejtemaee) and s = (Ω²/2)/(Γ²/4 + Δ²) per beam, detuning-suppressed and normalized on the S₁/₂-P₃/₂ stretched line (Joshi), namespaced and never aliased; s_Joshi = (3/2)s₀(Γ/2)²/((Γ/2)² + Δ²) **[background]** | Ejtemaee-Haljan 2017; Joshi 2020 |
| Detuning | δ = ω_drive − ω_transition, red negative; μ the beat-note detuning from the carrier; δ_{i,m} = μ_i − ω_m always two-indexed; the composite-pulse detuning error δ̂ = ε_d = Δ/Ω (Kabytayev and Cummins's ε_d = f = δ/ν₁) | RMP 2003. Not: Landsman's δ measured from a mode; Mount's dimensionless δ; Sørensen-Mølmer's ε = ν − δ from the sideband |
| Itano-Wineland detuning | their (ω₀ − ω) is positive for red: the sign flips on import and the recoil shift is added | Itano-Wineland 1982 Eqs. 16-29 |
| EIT detuning | Δ > 0 and Ω_r² = 4ν(ν + Δ) in the adopted sign, Ω_r = √(Ω₁² + Ω₂²) | Section 4.2.3. Not: Morigi's Δ, the negative of this one (her optimum 4ν(ν − Δ)) |
| Effective wavevector | Δk = k₁ − k₂ with beam 1 coupling to the upper level, ∣Δk∣ = 2k sin(θ_cross/2) from the configured geometry; k for one beam, 0 for a microwave | RMP; Wineland 1998; Lee 2005. Not: Landsman's k₂ − k₁; Lee's arXiv v1 Eq. 2 (the journal version is right) |
| Optical phase on σ₊ | e^{+i(Δk·X̄_i − Δφ_i)}; beam-path and ion-position drift one scalar per ion per pulse | Monroe 2020 Eqs. 16, 18. Not: the e^{−i(…)} of Monroe Eqs. 3, 5 and Lee Eqs. 3, 5 |
| Polarization components | ε̂ = Σ_q ε_q ê_q in the spherical basis about B, ê_{±1} = ∓(x̂ ± iŷ)/√2, ê₀ = ẑ; helicity q_γ = +1 (σ⁺), 0 (π), −1 (σ⁻) for absorption ∣F m_F⟩ → ∣F′ m_F + q_γ⟩; the operator index q_op = m_lower − m_upper = −q_γ | Steck QAO Eqs. 7.188-7.190, 7.419-7.422. Not: Steck's tables listing σ⁺ under q = −1; Berkeland's E_{+1}, E₀, E_{−1}, which are σ⁻, π, σ⁺ (relabelled at ingest) |
| 3j polarization index | read q off the column ordering of the equation implemented, never across equations: (J_f J_i 1; −m_f m_i q) forces q = m_f − m_i, (J_i 1 J_f; −m_i q m_f) forces q = m_i − m_f | Berkeland Eqs. 4, 12. Not: importing one into the other, which zeroes every σ channel and breaks trace preservation **[recomputed here]** |
| Spin and motion phases | φ_s = (φ_b + φ_r)/2 and φ_m = (φ_b − φ_r)/2 of the tone phases, σ_φ = e^{−iφ_s}σ₊ + e^{iφ_s}σ₋; the force axis carries a further π/2 from the i of the sideband coupling (Section 4.3.4) | Haljan 2005. Not: Lee 2005's opposite sign of φ_S |
| Beat-note phase of the force | sine convention, Ω(t) sin(μt), force zero at t = 0 | Choi 2014 |
| Rotation generators | R(θ, φ) = exp[−iθσ_φ/2] carries θ/2; XX(χ) = exp(−iχσ_xσ_x) carries χ, maximally entangling at χ = π/4; no shared generator helper; p_xx = sin²α for the two-qubit case only. Composite pulses rely on R(2π, φ) = −I, and R(θ, φ + π) as the inverse is an ideal-pulse identity that fails under detuning error | Maslov 2017; Trout 2018. Not: tabulated composite angles halved into "pulse areas" (every 2π filler becomes 4π, breaking SK1, BB1, P2 and the Trotter-Suzuki ladder) |
| Gate parameters | radians internally, IonQ turns at the API boundary (1 turn = 2π): phases enter as e^{±2πiφ}, angles as cos(πθ), sin(πθ); the matrices of GPi, GPi2, MS(φ₀, φ₁, θ), ZZ(θ) and RZ are those of `control/native.py`, whose docstring also states their tensor order and its relation to qiskit-ionq's matrices | IonQ documentation. Not: the GPi2 prose/matrix disagreement at 0.5 turns |
| Operator order in templates | matrix products written right to left, circuit time left to right; every template checked numerically | Maslov 2017 Sec. 2.2. Not: Maslov's "." read as time order |
| Virtual-Z propagation | RZ(θ) shifts every later pulse phase φ → φ − θ, gates read in time order | Section 7.6. Not: IonQ's printed +θ, which holds in matrix order |
| Result bit order | in every bitstring key of a `Result`, qubit 0 is the least-significant bit, the rightmost character: "101" on three qubits is qubit 0 = 1, qubit 1 = 0, qubit 2 = 1, and the IonQ v1 decimal key "5"; `Result.final_state` is in QuTiP's tensor order (ion 0 the first factor), converted by `run.job.to_register_order` | IonQ OpenAPI v0.4; qiskit-ionq and PennyLane-IonQ client code. Not: IonQ's v2 result strings, which run the other way (the exporter converts); PennyLane's big-endian reversal |
| Entangling angle | χ_ij = Σ_m η_imη_jm ∫∫[Ω_i(t)Ω_j(t′) + Ω_j(t)Ω_i(t′)]… with the pair sum over i < j, maximally entangling at χ = π/4 | Choi 2014; Maslov 2017; Debnath 2016. Not: Choi's printed factor 2 beyond proportional envelopes; Blümel's factor-2-free kernel with target π/8 (double the kernel to convert); Roos's and Kirchmair's π/8 comes from S without ½ and is not rescaled |
| Spin operator in MS formulas | S_α = Σ_iσ_α^i; the force on S_y is −(ħηΩ/2)(a†e^{iεt} + h.c.) for the per-tone Ω; closure ηΩ/ε = 1/(2√K) in every spin normalization, with the phase target −π/2 on J_y², −π/8 on S_y², π/4 on σ_yσ_y (Section 4.4.1) | Sørensen-Mølmer 2000; Kirchmair 2009; Roos 2008. Not: Roos's Ω_c = ∣ε∣/(4η), which reads ∣ε∣/(2η) in this Ω |
| Displacement integral | Choi's form with sin(μt) inside the integrand and bare e^{iω_m t}, or δ_m = ω_m − μ with a slow envelope, never mixed; FM uses θ_m(t) = ∫δ_m dt′ | Choi 2014. Not: Landsman's bare-ω_m form with a slow envelope |
| Observable rotation | O_V = V†OV | Kirchmair Eq. 11. Not: the VOV† abbreviation |
| Crosstalk ratio | ε_ij = Ω_j/Ω_i, a Rabi (amplitude) ratio from beam i onto ion j | Fang 2022. Not: intensity ratios |
| Sideband excitation lineshape | P = [Ω²/(Ω² + δ²)] sin²((t/2)√(Ω² + δ²)), half depth at δ = Ω, π time π/Ω; the calibration fit function (Section 7.5) | the Rabi row. Not: the half-Rabi Ω²/(Ω² + δ²/4) sin²(t√(Ω² + δ²/4)) of Wineland and Blümel, doubled on ingest |
| Sideband-cooling trapping condition | Ω_{n−1,n}t = 2mπ | Section 4.2.2. Not: Ωt = mπ (Wineland 1998, Monroe 1995) |
| Hyperfine dipole matrix elements | ⟨F m_F∣d_q∣F′ m_F′⟩ = ⟨J‖d‖J′⟩(−1)^{F′+J+1+I}√((2F′+1)(2J+1)){J J′ 1; F′ F I}(−1)^{F′−1+m_F}√(2F+1)(F′ 1 F; m_F′ q −m_F), with Γ = ω³(2J+1)∣⟨J‖d‖J′⟩∣²/(3πε₀ħc³(2J′+1)) for the lower level J and upper J′ (Section 4.5.2) | Steck, Rb87 Eqs. 35-38 |
| Dipole-element normalization | the Brink-Satchler reduced element (Steck; no 1/√(2j+1)); the stretched cycling element of Ozeri, Uys and Wineland is √(1/2)⟨J‖er‖J′⟩ on a J = 1/2 → 3/2 line; Edmonds' round bracket is √(2j+1) larger; Steck's Γ_{J_gJ_e} is the rate out of J_e into J_g (b/τ); every stored element and rate carries its convention tag | Steck QAO Eqs. 7.237-7.283. Not: a reduced element in a stretched-element formula (a factor 2 on a D2 line) |
| Hyperfine factors | S_FF′ = (2F′+1)(2J+1){J J′ 1; F′ F I}² (absorption, sums to one over F′) and b(F′ → F) = (2F+1)(2J′+1){…}² (emission, sums to one over F), two directed quantities | Steck Rb87 Eqs. 40-42 |
| Hyperfine operator | H_hfs = A I·J/ħ² + B[3(I·J)²/ħ⁴ + (3/2)(I·J)/ħ² − I(I+1)J(J+1)]/(2I(2I−1)J(2J−1)) in SI | Steck QAO Eq. 7.134. Not: Eq. 7.132, which halves every ħ exponent |
| Hyperfine sign and g-factors | A_hfs signed, inferred from μ_I when not printed (9Be+, 43Ca+ and the 171Yb+ ³D[3/2]₁/₂ are inverted); measured g_J, not Landé; g_S = +2.0023193; g_I = −(μ_I/(Iμ_N))(m_e/m_p) with a plus sign in H_Z = μ_B(g_J J_z + g_I I_z)B/ħ (43Ca+ g_I = +2.0467 × 10⁻⁴); "1.39-1.40 MHz/G" is g_Fμ_B/h, not g_F (171Yb+ F = 1: g_F = 1.001128, 1.4012 MHz/G); 9Be+'s negative moment puts F = 1 above F = 2 | Steck; Langer 2005; Harty 2014. Not: Harty's g_I with Langer's sign (83.76 kHz and 0.207 G off for 43Ca+); A > 0 for an inverted level (it removes the clock points) |
| Field curvature | `d2nu_dB2_hz_per_g2` (Harty's 2.4 mHz/mG²) and `taylor_c2_hz_per_g2` = ½d²ν/dB² (Langer's 0.305 Hz/µT²; 171Yb+'s quoted 310.8 Hz/G²) stored separately; accumulated phase 2π∫[ν(B) − ν(B₀)]dt with the bracket in Hz | Harty 2014; Langer 2005. Not: one field named "curvature"; the missing 2π in Langer's figures |
| Zeeman-degenerate dark states | the straight pairing Σ_m c_m E_m = 0 for J_i = 1 ↔ J_f = 0; in general the null space of M_{m_f m_i} ∝ (−1)^q(−1)^{J_f−m_f}(J_f 1 J_i; −m_f q m_i)E_{−q} with q = m_f − m_i; σ⁺ light (E_{+1} = 0) leaves span{∣1,0⟩, ∣1,+1⟩} dark | Berkeland-Boshier 2002 Eqs. 6-9c. Not: the printed Eq. 8 and Eqs. 9a-9b, which return the conjugate polarization's dark states |
| Saturation ceiling of a closed manifold | P_f^max = n_e/(n_e + n_g) = (2J_f + 1)/((2J_i + 1) + (2J_f + 1)), an upper bound on any Bloch-solve photon rate: Γ/4 for three ground and one excited sublevel, Γ/3 for a Λ system, Γ/2 for a two-level atom **[background]** | Berkeland Eqs. 13-14, 21 (the special cases). Not: the two-level Γ/2 on a degenerate manifold |
| Dark-state destabilization optimum | δ ≈ Ω/2 for the dark state's evolution rate, δ = 2δ_B only for g_J = 2 on J_i = 1 and δ = δ_B for a g_F ≈ 1 hyperfine level; θ_BE = arccos(1/√3) = 54.7356°; Ω ≈ γ/3 **[contested]** | Berkeland Secs. III, IV A 1, V. Not: δ and δ_B interchanged; the misprinted "≈ 63°"; Sec. III's Ω ≈ δ ≈ γ/5 |
| Polarization-modulation mappings | AOM scheme 2δ_B = δ_AOM+ and θ_BE = arctan(√2 E_σ⁺/E_π); modulated schemes n_max ≈ 2Φ/π for Φ > π, δ ≈ Φδ_mod; no steady state exists, so the solve propagates ≳ 1000/γ and averages over one modulation period | Berkeland Secs. IV A 2, IV B 2. Not: the θ_BE mapping printed as its reciprocal; n_max = 2π/Φ |
| Scattering-rate object | one multi-level optical-Bloch steady state supplies the cooling rate W(Δ), the readout rate R_∘, ρ_ee and the dark-state physics, its photon rate asserted against the closed-manifold ceiling (Section 4.2.8) | Section 4.2.8. Not: the two-level Lorentzian, which saturates at Γ/2 where 171Yb+ detection saturates at Γ/4 (ρ_ee → s_∘/18 at low intensity) |
| Bright/dark polarity | a property of the readout scheme, stored as an enum; for 171Yb+ ∣0⟩ (F = 0) is dark and ∣1⟩ (F = 1) bright | direct fluorescence and shelving invert it within the same papers |
| Readout figure of merit | ε = ½(ε_B + ε_D), both reported: ε_B the probability that a bright ion reads dark, ε_D that a dark ion reads bright | Myerson; Burrell; Harty; Christensen. Not: Acton's min(F_B, F_D) |
| Detection efficiency | ε_sys applied once, at the scattered-to-detected rate; background R_bg separate | Crain 2019 (Eq. 3 corrected). Not: Acton's α/η |
| Quadrupole (E2) coupling | Ω_{mm′} = (eE₀k/(2ħ))∣⟨S‖r²C^{(2)}‖D⟩∣∣Λ(m, m′)∣∣g^{(q)}∣ with q = m − m′, k = 2π/λ_vac of the single beam, C^{(2)}_q = √(4π/5)Y_{2q} (Racah), rank-2 tensors normalized to 2/3, A = cαk⁵∣⟨‖⟩∣²/(15(2j′ + 1)) with the upper j′; carrier π pulse at t = π/Ω; every stored wavelength a vacuum wavelength (Section 4.5.7) | James 1998; Roos 2000. Not: q = Δm; unit-normalized rank-2 tensors (√(3/2) large); r²Y_2m (√(4π/5) large); the lower-level 2j + 1 (√3 small); air wavelengths (274 ppm in k); the Boulder half-Ω |
| D-state lifetime element | Kreuter/Johnson A = (1/15)(ω/c)⁵∣⟨v‖Q‖w⟩∣²/(2j_v + 1) with the upper-level degeneracy, prefactor 1.11995 × 10¹⁸/λ[Å]⁵ s⁻¹ on vacuum wavelengths, namespaced from the Racah element above | Kreuter 2005 Eqs. 7-8. Not: the (1/75)(ω/c)⁵ convention (elements √5 larger) **[recomputed here]** |
| Blackbody-stimulated rate | W = A n̄(ν, T), the spontaneous rate multiplied by the Bose occupation; the upward rate carries g_u/g_l | Kreuter 2005 Eq. 1. Not: dividing by n̄ (8.75× low at 300 K); one W for both directions |
| Collision-induced rates | per ion and density-driven, R = Σ_s Γ_s p_s/(k_BT) with Γ_s a specific coefficient in cm³ s⁻¹, entering as √R collapse operators, independent of the ion number | Knoop-Vedel-Vedel 1995 via Kreuter 2005 |
| Rayleigh dephasing dissipator | c = ½√Γ_el σ_z: the dissipator prefactor is Γ_el/4 and the coherence decays at Γ_el/2, all consistent; the Raman operators √Γ_du∣u⟩⟨d∣ carry the plain rate | Uys 2010 Eqs. 6-8. Not: √(Γ_el/2)σ_z, which doubles the dephasing **[recomputed here]** |
| Raman detuning optimum | minimize the ratio R_SE/∣Ω∣ including Ω's own Δ dependence: Δ = (√2 − 1)ω_F, P_SE = 2√2 πγ/ω_F per π pulse on an m_F = 0 clock line | Wineland 2003 Eqs. 2.15-2.18. Not: minimizing R_SE alone (0.442493 ω_F) **[recomputed here]** |
| Recoil angular factor | α_m = ∫dΩ N(k̂)(k̂·ê_m)² with N normalized to one over the sphere; η̃_m² = α_m(kx₀,m)², the recoil quanta per photon (Section 4.2.8) | Stenholm 1986 Eq. 4.21. Not: rival values from different patterns: the RMP's ξ = 2/5 is σ along the beam, Ozeri's 5/12 an isotropic crossed-beam average, Itano's 2/5 π perpendicular and Cirac's 2/5 σ parallel; Joshi's 1/3 (isotropic) against its kernel's 2/5 and 1/5 |
| Emission-pattern normalization | a three-dimensional density normalized to one over the sphere; the one-dimensional reduction f̃(u) = 2πf(θ, φ) normalized on [−1, 1]; Cirac's W(u) = 2f̃(u) under du/2 | Itano-Wineland 1982 Eqs. 6-8; Steck Eqs. 1.46-1.48; Cirac 1992 Eq. 3. Not: dropping Cirac's ½ |
| Emission diffusion | D = (α/2)Σ_{i→j}ρ_iiΓ_{i→j}η_ij² with full partial rates and each channel's own η; 2D = αη_em²R_sc; absorption recoil only through e^{ik_L·x̂} in the Hamiltonian, α weighting emitted photons only | Cirac Eqs. 41, 55; Marzoli Eq. B4; Steck Eq. 5.417. Not: Marzoli's Eq. 4 read as full rates (2×); α on the absorbed photon |
| Where η² sits | inside S(±ν) and D in A_± = 2Re[S(∓ν) + D]; factored out with an explicit cos²θ_L in the closed forms, never both | Cirac Eq. 30; Eschner Eq. 6. Not: Roos's cos φ folded into η |
| Cooling coefficients A_± | bare rates A_± = W(Δ ∓ ν) + (η̃²/η²)W(Δ) with η̃² = α(k_em x₀)², the Fock rate equation carrying the drive's η²; the carrier weight reduces to α for one resonant beam along the mode axis and vanishes only for EIT at the dark resonance | Stenholm 1986 Eqs. 5.49-5.54; Cirac 1992 Eq. 30; Marzoli 1994 Eq. 2; Eschner 2003 Eq. 6. Not: RMP Eqs. 117-119's η² inside; Cirac's Eqs. 50, 52 |
| Momentum diffusion and recoil temperature | V_p → D_p t with no factor 2; k_BT_r = (ħk)²/m = 2ħω_r | Steck Eqs. 1.113, 5.430, 5.517 |
| Effective two-level roles | Ξ: effective ground ∣0⟩, collapse √Γ′∣0⟩⟨2∣, cooling at δ′ = −ν; V: effective ground ∣2⟩, collapse √Γ′∣2⟩⟨0∣, cooling at δ₂₀ = +ν; γ′/Γ′ = (Γ₁₀ + Γ₁₂)/(2Γ₁₀) or /(2Γ₁₂) | Marzoli Eqs. 12, 15; Roos Eq. 3.21. Not: V coded with the Ξ assignment |
| Doppler limit | computed from the force model; the closed forms ħΓ/2 (ξ = 1) and 0.35ħΓ (ξ = 2/5) differ by 1.43 | RMP Eq. 106; Berkeland Eq. 13 |
| Recoil kernel | twelve collapse operators J_mq = p_mq C_m √Γ e^{−ik_qẑ}σ_m⁻ over four decay channels and three recoil classes k_q ∈ {−k, 0, +k}, entered incoherently, Σ_q p_mq² = 1 and Σ_q p_mq²(k_q/k)² = α_m | Joshi 2020 Eq. A.3, Table A1. Not: the printed coherent q-sum inside one operator (decay inflated 2.7856× for σ, 2.3314× for π), the missing −i/ħ and Γ for √Γ **[recomputed here]** |
| Polarization-gradient depth | ξ = Δs/(3ω), the light-shift modulation amplitude over the mode frequency (so "depth equals the trap frequency" is ξ = ½); cooling needs Δ > 0; the substitution 2φ → 2kz + 2φ applies to the pumping rates only | Joshi 2020 Eqs. 1-2, 6-10, A.2. Not: ξ as the full depth; red detuning from alkali PGC; the substitution in the potentials (halves the gradient period) |
| Depolarizing normalization | Λ_ε(ρ) = (1 − ε)ρ + ε/(4ⁿ − 1)Σ_{P≠I}PρP with ε the entanglement infidelity, the same channel as uniform p/3 and p/15 Kraus weights; Qiskit's λ = p 4ⁿ/(4ⁿ − 1) is a conversion (Section 6.8) | Chen 2023; Trout 2018 |
| RB error rate | r = (1 − p)(2ⁿ − 1)/2ⁿ, the average error per Clifford; (4ⁿ − 1)(1 − p)/4ⁿ is the entanglement infidelity of the depolarizing channel with the same p, kept under that name; simultaneous RB fits each qubit's own marginal decay (r_q = (1 − p_q)/2) (Section 7.9) | Magesan 2011; Gambetta 2012. Not: Wright's "p is the gate fidelity"; Chen's Eq. 3 |
| Gate fidelity measure | F_K = ¼∣Tr(U_id†U)∣² internally for a unitary error; F_C = √F_K and F_avg = (2F_C² + 1)/3 as conversions; every ingested coefficient tagged with its measure, and converted before it meets an RB or depolarizing number | Kabytayev 2014 Sec. II B; Cummins 2003 Eq. 7; Merrill-Brown Eqs. 10-11. Not: Low-Yoder-Chuang's ‖UV†‖ (identically 1) and Brown-Harrow-Chuang's 1 − ‖V†U‖ (identically 0) |
| Composite-pulse order | order n ⇔ propagator residual O(ε^{n+1}) ⇔ operator-distance slope n + 1 ⇔ infidelity slope 2(n + 1); scored by the spectral-norm distance, converted as E²/2 below E ≈ 10⁻⁸ | Brown-Harrow-Chuang; Low-Yoder-Chuang Eq. 3 |
| Static control-error primitive | M(θ, φ; ε_a, ε_d) = exp[−iθ{(1 + ε_a)ρ̂(φ)·σ + ε_dσ_z}/2]: ε_a scales the angle, ε_d is a detuning in units of the Rabi rate and does not reverse with the drive phase; the addressing error ε_N has ε_N = 0 ideal | Kabytayev 2014 Sec. II A; Merrill-Brown Sec. 2.2.1; Cummins Eq. 5. Not: Cummins's power error against the optical power; R(θ(1 + ε_N), φ) |
| Composite-pulse sequence order | (θ_l, φ_l) stored in time order and folded right to left, the target phase added to every entry; a negative area emitted as a positive one at φ + π; durations τ = Σ_lθ_l/Ω | Kabytayev Table I; Merrill-Brown Sec. 2. Not: Low-Yoder-Chuang's φ₁, which is last in time (reversed on ingest) |
| Filter-function normalization | S two-sided in angular frequency with the e^{−iωt} kernel, prefactor 1/(2π) two-sided or 1/π one-sided, weight 1/ω² with the explicit −iω in R_ij(ω) (a matched pair); the dephasing variable b(t) in H = b(t)σ_z with no ½, so S_b = S_δ/4; χ(τ) = (2/π)∫(dω/ω²)S_b F(ωτ), W = e^{−χ}, reporting 1 − F_av = (1 − W)/2; ω_min = 2π/T_total reported with d ln χ/d ln ω_min (Section 6.9) | Green 2013 Eqs. 4, 14-24; Biercuk 2009 Eqs. 1-3; Kabytayev Eq. 3. Not: Biercuk's S_β = 4S_b read as S_b; Green's Eqs. 35a/b (exponent short by 2); Green Eq. 45 (even n only) |
| Comb drives | ν_rep, ν_AOM and every laser input ordinary (Hz), every Hamiltonian coefficient angular, one converter; τ the sech field-envelope parameter (never a FWHM); one sech(ω_qτ/2) factor; the two-photon Rabi frequency on chain I, Ω₀ = g₀²/(2Δ) **[contested]**; I_sat namespaced by chain; the pulse count N = f_rep t and Ω₀ = f_rep θ with f_rep ordinary; the amplitude-noise budget with Ω₀ in Hz (Section 4.3.7) | Section 4.3.7 |
| Ion mass | `Species.mass_u` is the ion mass, the cited atomic mass minus one electron mass (171Yb+: 170.93578 u from 170.9363302 u) | AME via NIST. Not: the atomic mass (η at N = 17 moves in the fourth digit) |

Symbol collisions the code keeps apart: the Mathieu parameter and the ion charge (q, Q); the mode eigenvector component and the characteristic exponent (b, β); n_max (highest Fock index) and d_m = n_max + 1 (mode dimension); nuclear spin and laser intensity (I); pulse area and polarization (π); laser detuning and Zeeman splitting (Δ, δ); Ramsey arm, shelf lifetime and acquisition window (τ); mode index and ion mass (m); the polarization-gradient cooling rate and the blackbody-stimulated rate (W); a heating rate and the Hamiltonian (H); the natural linewidth, a Sisyphus cooling rate and a collisional coefficient (Γ); the mean phonon and mean thermal-photon numbers (n̄); the recoil factor, a dark-state branching fraction and a slope (α); the 3j index, the recoil class and the Mathieu parameter (q). Egan's "even modes" are the spatially symmetric (odd-index) ones.

## 14. The zoomable application

### 14.1 The idea and the contract

The application presents one simulated trapped-ion quantum computer at five levels of abstraction. The user starts where a cloud customer stands, with a circuit and a histogram, and zooms in step by step until the screen shows the Hamiltonian terms, matrix elements and collapse operators the simulator is integrating. Every level is runnable: the user can execute the machine while looking at it from any height. The application is a separate package, `qutip_trap_app`, that uses only the public API of `qutip_trap`; nothing in the core is written, scheduled or changed for the GUI, the core's tests do not run the app's, and a need the core does not meet is judged on the core's own merits while the app works around it. Two axes are kept apart: the **abstraction of the view** (what the screen shows) and the **fidelity of execution** (which engine of Section 5.4 computed it). Each level has a default engine, any level can display a run made by a deeper engine, and shallower engines are always labelled as derived.

Four rules make the ladder honest rather than decorative.

1. **One record, many views.** Every level is a view over the same run record (Section 14.3); nothing on any screen comes from a separate illustrative model. A histogram bar is the count of recorded shots, a Bloch vector the reduced state of the recorded joint state, an ion's drawn position the equilibrium solver's output at scale.
2. **Coarse-graining commutes with simulation, within a stated bound.** A shallower engine reproduces the deeper one within the bound it reports (the residual displacement of GATE_LOCAL, the channel-derivation residual of the app-side channel replay); the app checks this on demand.
3. **Every displayed formula and number carries its provenance.** The tags appear as chips linked to the Part II item and its source; a quantity without a tag is a bug.
4. **Every result carries its convergence report.** The truncation-boundary population and the tolerance and cap re-checks of Section 5.5 are shown with every trace, and a failing check colours the result rather than hiding it.

### 14.2 The zoom ladder

| Level | What the user sees | What the user can change | Default engine | Zoom-in targets |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0, Machine | The device card (species, ion count, native gate set, calibrated gate errors, SPAM), a circuit editor with OpenQASM 2 and IonQ JSON import, the shot count, the results histogram with counts and probabilities, and the compiler's target distribution beside the simulated one, never in its place | Circuit, shots, qubit-to-ion mapping | Channel replay (app-side, built from the core's Section 6.8 channel summaries), labelled as derived | A histogram bar opens the shots behind it; a shot opens its photon-count records; a gate opens Level 1; the device card opens the Level 4 pages; the SPAM figure opens the readout page |
| 1, Circuit | The compiled native-gate timeline on ions (GPi, GPi2, MS or XX, ZZ, virtual-Z frame updates), each gate with its target unitary, calibrated parameters (χ, phases, duration) and derived error channel; the register state after each gate as reduced density matrices, Bloch vectors and Pauli expectations; the phase register per ion | Gate parameters, compiler template choices, idle intervals | Channel replay or deeper | A gate opens its pulses at Level 2; a channel opens its derivation (process tomography of the simulated pulse); the phase register opens the virtual-Z rule (Section 7.6) |
| 2, Schedule | Pulses on a time axis: which ions each beam illuminates and from which direction, the tones with their detunings against the mode spectrum, phases, amplitude and frequency waveforms with their segment tables, durations and dead times, the crosstalk light on neighbours, and loop-closure indicators per mode | Waveforms, detunings, amplitudes, durations, crosstalk parameters | GATE_LOCAL | A pulse opens Level 3; a tone opens the Hamiltonian term it produces; a mode opens the crystal page at Level 4 |
| 3, Dynamics | Inside one pulse for one dynamical sample: qubit populations and coherences against time, Fock distributions per mode as heatmaps, the phase-space trajectories α_m(t) closing or failing to close, the accumulated χ(t), the Debye-Waller factor, quantum jumps marked where they occurred, the process matrix of the finished pulse, and the quasi-static noise values drawn for this sample | Initial motional state per mode, noise rates, truncation caps and solver tolerances (with the convergence check re-run) | JOINT_EXACT, re-simulated on demand for the zoomed pulse | A Hamiltonian term opens its matrix elements and the Ω_{n′,n} table at Level 4; a jump opens its collapse operator and the derivation of its rate |
| 4, Physics | The first-principles pages: species (level diagram with the driven transitions, branching, hyperfine structure, the qubit pair), trap (electrode potentials to Mathieu a and q to β and ν, pseudopotential, the stability diagram with the operating point, micromotion), crystal (equilibrium positions to scale, normal modes with animated eigenvectors, η per ion, mode and Δk), light (beams with k-vectors and polarizations, the Raman configuration, Ω from intensity, Stark shifts, scattering rates against detuning), noise (the S_E model and heating rates, dephasing spectra, the crosstalk matrix), cooling (stages and n̄(t) per mode), readout (R_∘, R_d and R_b against saturation, count histograms, the discriminator and its threshold, the error budget), and the Hamiltonian builder page showing the H(t) terms and collapse operators assembled for a chosen pulse | Any device parameter: voltages and rf amplitude, laser powers, detunings and geometry, temperatures, noise densities, detection window | JOINT_EXACT with the preparation and readout layers, so a run is the whole process from cooling to photon records | Zooming out re-derives every level above through the calibration emulation |

A **numerics panel** accompanies every level: Hilbert-space dimensions and truncation caps in use, the boundary population, the tolerance re-check, the trajectory count, the wall time and the engine that produced what is on screen.

### 14.3 One run record, many views

The run record is the single data structure the levels read: job (device hash, seed, package version, convergence policy) → compiled circuit → schedule → per-sample dynamics traces → readout records → results. A dynamics trace stores, at configurable sample times, expectation values, reduced internal-state density matrices, per-mode occupation distributions, the α_m(t) trajectories and the jump list; full joint kets are stored only below a configurable joint dimension. Anything not stored is recomputed on demand: zooming into a pulse re-simulates that pulse alone from its recorded initial state and noise sample (Section 11.4), a background job when the space is large, and every result is cached in the record. Readout records hold photon counts per ion per sub-bin and the discriminator's posterior, so a shot opens down to its count trace. A record is keyed by the digest of its canonical encoding, arrays included.

### 14.4 Knobs propagate downward

The device model at Level 4 is the single source of truth. A change there (a trap voltage, a beam power, a detection window) re-derives the analytic layer immediately (mode structure, η, pulse-solver solutions, the closed-form gate-channel estimates, the device card's estimated columns) and invalidates the calibration table, whose regeneration (the surrogate calibration of Section 7.5) runs only as a user-initiated background job; dependent views show a stale badge until it completes, and the device card states which of its numbers are analytic estimates and which are calibrated. A change at a shallower level is a request rather than an edit: asking for XX(0.3) at Level 1 invokes the pulse solver; a detuning set by hand at Level 2 is applied as written, and the gate at Level 1 then shows its actual unitary, not the requested one. Requests the device cannot satisfy are shown with the reason (loop closure impossible within the power limit, an angle outside the calibrated range). No level floats free of the device model.

### 14.5 Correctness affordances

- **Provenance chips** on every formula and number, generated from the provenance ledger `docs/provenance/ledger.yaml` (one record per quantity: `id`, `symbol`, `tag`, `section`, `source`, `equation`, `corrected_form`), which the core's `Device.derived()` provenance and the app's chips both consume, so coverage is a set difference over ids; hovering a chip shows the tag, the source and the corrected form where the source is corrected.
- **Convergence badges** on every result and trace, red when a re-check of Section 5.5 fails.
- **Verify deeper.** At Levels 0 to 2 one action runs the same job at the next-deeper engine, on a subset of shots when the full run would be slow, and shows the discrepancy against the bound of Section 9.8 or the channel-derivation residual.
- **Target beside simulated.** Levels 0 and 1 show the compiler's target semantics next to the simulated result, never as the result.
- **Units and conventions.** Frequencies are displayed in Hz with the 2π conversion visible; the Section 13 row that governs a displayed quantity is one hover away.
- **No cartoons.** Every drawn element is computed: ion positions from the equilibrium solver, beams from the configured geometry, mode vectors from the Hessian, spectra from the built Hamiltonian. The few illustrative elements, such as a level diagram's vertical spacing, are labelled "not to scale".
- **Reproducibility.** Seed, device hash, package version and convergence report travel with every record.
- **Presets from the validation suite.** Entries of Section 9 load as a device configuration plus experiment (Harty's microwave randomized benchmarking, Kirchmair's thermal Mølmer-Sørensen scan, Myerson's readout optimum, among others); the app runs them and places the published number beside the simulated one, each with its provenance chip.
- **Explain panel.** At every level, the Part II subsection that governs what is on screen is shown beside it.

### 14.6 Flet implementation

- **Routes mirror the ladder** with the declarative `ft.Router`: `/job/{id}` for Level 0, `/job/{id}/circuit/{gate}` for Level 1, `/job/{id}/schedule/{pulse}` for Level 2, `/job/{id}/dynamics/{pulse}/{sample}` for Level 3 and `/device/{page}` for the Level 4 pages, with a breadcrumb zoom bar and keyboard zoom; browser history works in the served web mode because the routes are real.
- **Core contract.** The app calls only public core API that exists for the core's own reasons: the device model and its hash, the `Machine` with `run` and `submit` (a `Job` whose `RunSpec` records the request), the `Result` with its diagnostics and convergence report, the experiments on the machine, the per-pulse evolution and process-tomography entry points (with the traces' per-time Fock marginals and the GATE_LOCAL walk's per-step register), and the compiler's target unitaries. It imports the core in one module, `core.py`. The channel-replay engine is built app-side from the Section 6.8 summaries. A view that needs data the core does not expose is shown as unavailable rather than patched into the core.
- **Layering.** `viewmodel/` is pure Python with no Flet import: it turns run records into view data, performs the coarse-graining and resolves provenance, and is where the correctness tests run. `views/` holds the Flet controls per level. `workers.py` runs core calls in a separate process, streaming progress and partial results through `page.pubsub`, with view updates scheduled by `page.run_task`, so the UI never blocks on a solver; under WebAssembly the worker runs in-process.
- **Charts.** `flet-charts` line and scatter charts for time series, calibration scans and phase-space loops; bar charts for histograms; Flet's canvas for level diagrams, mode eigenvectors and the stability diagram; heatmaps (Fock distributions) as PNG images rendered in pure Python; data tables for matrix elements, segment tables and parameters.
- **Testing.** The app's tests run in its own CI, not the core's: pytest on the view-models and `flet test` for navigation and run flows; the physics is never tested through the UI. They pin: coarse-graining (Level 1 populations and Pauli expectations after gate k, computed from the recorded Level 3 joint state, equal the reduced density matrices to 10⁻¹²); channel derivation (an app-side replay of a two-ion Bell circuit matches JOINT_EXACT within the residual it reports, and a hotter motional state widens residual and discrepancy together); downward propagation (an rf-amplitude change at Level 4 moves β, ν, η and the recalibrated gate durations consistently, and the device card follows); request semantics (XX(0.3) requested at Level 1 plays a pulse whose simulated unitary is XX(0.3) within the calibration tolerance; a detuning set by hand at Level 2 shows its actual unitary at Level 1); provenance coverage (every catalogue quantity resolves to a tagged ledger record, so an untagged quantity fails the build); the convergence badge (red for an under-truncated run that fails the cap re-check, not at the policy's caps); the re-simulation cache (zooming into a pulse twice recomputes once, the cached trace equal to a fresh one to solver tolerance); navigation (a Bell job from a histogram bar to a Hamiltonian matrix element in at most six clicks); presets (each Section 9 preset loads, runs and shows the published number beside the simulated one with the correct tag).
- **Packaging.** `flet run` for the desktop window, `flet run --web` for the served browser mode, `flet build macos`, `windows` or `linux` for bundles with the physics dependencies as binary wheels; the static Pyodide web build is the GitHub Pages site (Section 1.5).

### 14.7 Interactive budgets

| Action | Budget | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Zoom into a pulse: one trajectory of a 100 µs entangling pulse with the recorded noise sample and jump record | ≤ 1 s at joint dimension ≤ 256; ≤ 10 s at ≤ 864 | Section 11.1: 0.8 s and 1.2 s per 100 µs gate |
| Zoom into a pulse: jump ensemble and process matrix | background job with progress (tens to hundreds of trajectories; 16 tomography inputs) | Section 5.4 |
| Level 4 parameter change: recalibration by the surrogate | user-initiated background job, stale badge until it completes | Section 7.5: closed-form waveforms with exact spot checks, seconds for the two-ion device |
| Zoom into a pulse at larger dimension | recorded traces at once; re-simulation as a background job with progress | Sections 11.1, 11.2 |
| Level 0 or 1 run by channel replay, 20 qubits, 100 gates | seconds | density matrix to 12 qubits, sampling beyond |
| Level 4 parameter change: re-derivation of modes and η | immediate | closed forms and small eigenproblems |
| Level 4 parameter change: pulse re-solve per pair | ≤ 10 s in the background, with stale badges | the AM and FM solvers of Section 4.4.3 |

### 14.8 Out of scope

Live hardware submission, multi-user server deployment, and mobile bundles (possible with `flet build`, but the physics stack's wheel availability on Android and iOS is not addressed).
